Ultraviolet renormalisation in \(\varphi^4_2\) however spoils any “easy” convexity of the potential and it is not clear how to gain sufficient control of (13) in this case. Similar issues appear in other methods of stochastic quantisation as soon as the infinite volume limit is considered for \(P (\varphi)_2\) Euclidean quantum fields [26, 27, 2]. Despite these difficulties, we show that the wFBSDE is still a useful tool which can be used effectively to prove the large deviations of the \(\varphi^4_2\) measure in the semiclassical limits. Let us remark that Lacoin, Rhodes and Vargas obtained large deviations results in the case of the Liouville model in [40, 41].

Remark 28. Let us note that all the discussion in this section can be extended to general \(P (\varphi)_2\) models with a positive coefficient in the highest order polynomial. Moreover also \(O (N)\) invariant vector models could be handled in similar ways. Convexity of the classical potential (i.e. before Wick ordering) is only needed in Section 4.1 below to prove large deviations.

We will need the following enhancement \(\mathbb{W}\) of the extended GFF \(W\). Let

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{W}_t^2 := \llbracket W_t^2 \rrbracket, \quad \text{and} \quad \mathbb{W}^3_t := \llbracket W^3_t \rrbracket,\)

where here \(\llbracket \cdot \rrbracket\) denotes the Wick powers with respect to the Gaussian field \(W\). Then

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{W} := (W, \mathbb{W}^2, \mathbb{W}^3) \in L^p (\mathbb{P}, \mathfrak{S})\)

for all \(p \geqslant 1\), where \(\mathfrak{S} := C ([0, 1], (C^{- \delta} (\rho^{\delta}))^3)\) for a fixed \(\delta > 0\) which can be chosen as small as we like. By abuse of notation we will denote also \(\mathbb{W}= (W, \mathbb{W}^2, \mathbb{W}^3)\) the canonical process on \(\mathfrak{S}\).

In the following we will always consider a family of \((\Lambda)_{| \Lambda | \in \mathbb{R}}\) given by \(\Lambda = | \Lambda |^{1 / 2} S\) for some fixed set \(S\) of volume \(1\) with \(C^1\)-boundary. This allows us to prove that \(\mathbb{1}_{\Lambda}\) is sufficiently regular to make sense of quantities like \(\int_{\Lambda} \llbracket \phi^4 \rrbracket\), \(\int_{\Lambda} \llbracket W^4_1 \rrbracket\) and in general of products with Wick monomials of the GFF.

Lemma 29. For any \(p \geqslant 1\), \(s < \frac{1}{p}\) and \(\iota > \frac{1}{16}\) we have that \(\mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \in B_{p, p}^s (\rho^{\iota})\), furthermore

\(\displaystyle \sup_{| \Lambda |} \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \|_{B_{p, p}^s (\rho^{\iota})} < \infty .\)

Proof. We have that \(\nabla \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} = - \nu \mathrm{d} \sigma\) where \(\mathrm{d} \sigma\) is the surface measure on \(\partial \Lambda\) and \(\nu\) is the outer unit normal. It is known that \(\| f \|_{B_{p, p}^s (\rho)} \lesssim \| f \|_{B_{p, p}^{s - 1} (\rho)} + \| \nabla f \|_{B_{p, p}^{s - 1} (\rho)}\) (see e.g. [27], Lemma A.5). This implies that for any \(s < 1\), \(\sup_{| \Lambda |} \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \|_{B_{1, 1}^s (\rho^{\iota})} < \infty\). Clearly \(\sup_{| \Lambda |} \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \|_{L^{\infty} (\rho^{\iota})} < \infty\) and by Besov space interpolation (see e.g. [27] Lemma A.3)

\(\displaystyle \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \|_{B_{p, p}^{\theta s} (\rho^{\iota})} \lesssim \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \|^{\theta}_{B_{1, 1}^s (\rho^{\iota})} \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \|^{1 - \theta}_{L^{\infty} (\rho^{\iota})}\)

where

\(\displaystyle \frac{1}{p} = \theta .\)
\(\Box\)

Lemma 30. The functional \(\check{F}^{\Lambda} := \check{F}^{V_{\Lambda}}\) is admissible and for all \(\mu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}\)

\(\displaystyle \check{F}^{\Lambda} (\mu) = \check{F}^{\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}} (\mu) =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) + \mathcal{E} (Z)],\)

with

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) := 4 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \mathbb{W}^3_1 Z_1 + 6 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \mathbb{W}^2_1 (Z_1)^2 + 4 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} W_1 (Z_1)^3 + \lambda \int_{\Lambda} (Z_1)^4 . \) (23)

Proof. It is known that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} \left[ e^{- q \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \llbracket W^4 \rrbracket} \right] < \infty,\)

for any \(q > 0\) (see e.g. [24] or [5]) and

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [| V_{\Lambda} (W) |^p] \leqslant \mathbb{E} [| \| \llbracket W^4 \rrbracket \|_{B^{- \delta}_{\infty, \infty} (\rho^{\delta})} |^p] < \infty,\)

for some \(\delta > 0\), which shows that \(F\) is a tame functional. Now it remains to prove the equality in (23). For this it is enough to show that for \(Z\) such that \(\| Z \|_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})} < \infty\) \(\mu\)-almost surely, we have

\begin{eqnarray*} \int_{\Lambda} \llbracket (W_1 + Z_1)^4 \rrbracket & = & \int_{\Lambda} \llbracket W^4_1 \rrbracket + 4 \int_{\Lambda} \llbracket W^3_1 \rrbracket Z_1 + 6 \int_{\Lambda} \llbracket W^2_1 \rrbracket (Z_1)^2 + 4 \int_{\Lambda} W_1 (Z_1)^3 + \int_{\Lambda} (Z_1)^4 . \end{eqnarray*}

To see this, recall that if \(C^{\infty}_c \ni \psi_{\varepsilon} \rightarrow \delta_0\), is a standard mollifier, \(W^{\varepsilon} = \psi_{\varepsilon} \ast W\) and \(a_{\varepsilon} = 6\mathbb{E} [(W^{\varepsilon}_1 (0))^2]\), \(b_{\varepsilon} = - 3\mathbb{E} [(W^{\varepsilon}_1 (0))^2]^2\)

\begin{eqnarray*} \int_{\Lambda} \llbracket (W_1 + Z_1)^4 \rrbracket & = & \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \int_{\Lambda} (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast (W_1 + Z_1))^4 - a_{\varepsilon} \int_{\Lambda} (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast (W_1 + Z_1))^2 - b_{\varepsilon} | \Lambda |\\ & = & \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \int_{\Lambda} (W^{\varepsilon}_1)^4 - a_{\varepsilon} (W^{\varepsilon}_1)^2 - b_{\varepsilon} + 4 \int_{\Lambda} \left( (W^{\varepsilon}_1)^3 - \frac{a_{\varepsilon}}{2} W^{\varepsilon}_1 \right) Z^{\varepsilon}_1\\ & & + 6 \int_{\Lambda} \left( (W^{\varepsilon}_1)^2 - \frac{a_{\varepsilon}}{6} \right) (Z^{\varepsilon}_1)^2 + 4 \int_{\Lambda} W^{\varepsilon}_1 (Z^{\varepsilon}_1)^3 + \int_{\Lambda} (Z^{\varepsilon}_1)^4 \end{eqnarray*}

where we have denoted \(Z^{\varepsilon}_1 = \psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z_1\). Now it is well known that almost surely in \(C^{- \delta} (\rho^{\delta})\) we have \(W^{\varepsilon}_1 \rightarrow W_1\) and

\(\displaystyle (W^{\varepsilon}_1)^4 - a_{\varepsilon} (W^{\varepsilon}_1)^2 - b_{\varepsilon} \rightarrow \llbracket W^4_1 \rrbracket, \quad \left( (W^{\varepsilon}_1)^3 - \frac{a_{\varepsilon}}{2} W^{\varepsilon}_1 \right) \rightarrow \llbracket W^3_1 \rrbracket, \quad \left( (W^{\varepsilon}_1)^2 - \frac{a_{\varepsilon}}{6} \right) \rightarrow \llbracket W^2_1 \rrbracket .\)

Furthermore \(Z^{\varepsilon} \rightarrow Z\) in \(H^1\) which implies \((Z^{\varepsilon}_1)^2 \rightarrow Z^2_1, (Z^{\varepsilon}_1)^3 \rightarrow Z^3_1\) in \(W^{2 \delta, 1}\) for some \(\delta > 0\), which gives the statement. From this calculation it also follows that \((\mathbb{W}, Z) \mapsto \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z)\) is continuous on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathfrak{H}_w\).

To check the lower semicontinuity of \(\check{F}^{\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}}\) recall from Section 3 of [5] that

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) +\mathcal{E} (Z) \geqslant - Q (\mathbb{W})\)

with \(Q (\mathbb{W}) = C (\| \mathbb{W} \|^p_{\mathfrak{S}} + 1)\) for some \(p \geqslant 1\). Furthermore observe that the function

\(\displaystyle (\mathbb{W}, Z) \mapsto \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) +\mathcal{E} (Z)\)

is lower semicontinuous on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathfrak{H}_w\). Since the first marginal in \(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\) is fixed, the Portmanteau theorem gives, for any \(\mu_n \rightarrow \mu\) in \(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\):

\begin{eqnarray*} \liminf_{n \rightarrow \infty} \check{F}^{\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}} (\mu_n) & = & \liminf_{n \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) +\mathcal{E} (Z) + Q (\mathbb{W})] -\mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [Q (\mathbb{W})]\\ & \geqslant & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) +\mathcal{E} (Z) + Q (\mathbb{W})] -\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [Q (\mathbb{W})]\\ & = & \check{F}^{\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}} (\mu) . \end{eqnarray*}

Finally, by a slight adaptation of the proof of Lemma 34 below we see that there exists \(\delta > 0\) and \(C_{\delta, | \Lambda |} < \infty\) such that

\(\displaystyle \check{F}^{\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}} (\mu) \geqslant \delta \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{E} (Z) + \lambda \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z_1 \|^4_{L^4}] - C_{\delta, | \Lambda |},\)

for all \(\mu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}\), from which the strong coercivity condition (16) follows.\(\Box\)

The admissibility of \(\check{F}^{\Lambda}\) implies the variational representation for \(\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}\) and the existence of minimisers \(\mu^{f, \Lambda}\) of \(\check{F}^{f, \Lambda} := \check{F}^{f +\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}}\) with the corresponding wFBSDE equation.

Lemma 31. For any bounded and continuous \(f : \mathfrak{S}_0 \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\) there exists a minimiser \(\mu^{f, \Lambda} \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\) of \(\check{F}^{f, \Lambda} := \check{F}^{f +\mathcal{V}_{\Lambda}}\). Moreover if \(f\) is differentiable in the directions of \(H^1\) any minimiser \(\mu\) satisfies the wFBSDE equation

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ 4 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} Z^3 K + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, K) \right]\\ & \qquad = -\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot K + \lambda \int_{\Lambda} (4\mathbb{W}^3 + 12\mathbb{W}^2 Z + 12 W Z^2) K \right] \end{array} \) (24)

for any admissible control \(K\).

Proof. As we already remarked the existence of \(\mu^{f, \Lambda}\) follows from the admissibility of \(\check{F}^{f, \Lambda}\). The functional \(Z \mapsto \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{})\) is differentiable in \(H^1\) and

\(\displaystyle \nabla \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) \cdot K = 4 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \mathbb{W}^3_1 K_1 + 12 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \mathbb{W}^2_1 Z_1 K_1 + 12 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} W_1 (Z_1)^2 K_1 + 4 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} (Z_1)^3 K_1 .\)

Provided \(Z \mapsto f (W + Z)\) is also differentiable, we have

\(\displaystyle 0 = \nabla \check{F}^{f, \Lambda} (\mu) (K) =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\nabla f (W + Z) \cdot K + \nabla \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) \cdot K + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, K)]\)

which is (24).\(\Box\)

Remark 32. Note that \(Z \mapsto \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{})\) is twice differentiable, with

\(\displaystyle \nabla^2 \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) (K, K') = 12 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \mathbb{W}^2_1 K_1' K_1 + 24 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} W_1 Z_1 K_1' K_1 + 12 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} (Z_1)^2 K_1' K_1 .\)

Therefore if \(f\) is twice differentiable then we have also the second order condition:

\(\displaystyle 0 \leqslant \nabla^2 \check{F}^{f, \Lambda} (\mu) (K, K') =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\nabla^2 f (W + Z) (K, K') + \nabla^2 \mathcal{V}_{\Lambda} (\mathbb{W}, Z_{}) (K, K') + 2\mathcal{E} (K', K)]\)

for any minimiser \(\mu\) and any pair \(K, K'\) of admissible controls.

We record also the normalised variational representation for \(\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda} (f)\).

Lemma 33. Denote by \(\mu^{\Lambda} \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}\) a minimiser of \(\check{F}^{0, \Lambda}\). Then we have

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}_{\Lambda} (f) = \inf_{\upsilon \in \bar{\mathcal{Y}} (\mu^{\Lambda})} \{ \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} [f (W + Z + K)] + \check{H}^{\Lambda} (\upsilon) \}\)

with

\(\displaystyle \check{H}^{\Lambda} (\upsilon) := \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \dot{Z}_s (1 - \Delta) \dot{K}_s \mathrm{d} s + \frac{1}{2} \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \dot{K}_s (1 - \Delta) \dot{K}_s \mathrm{d} s \right]\)

\(\displaystyle +\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ 4 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket K + 6 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \llbracket (W + Z)^2 \rrbracket K^2 + 4 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \llbracket (W + Z) \rrbracket K^3 + \lambda \int_{\Lambda} K^4 \right] .\)

Proof. This follows from Lemma 27 by direct computation.\(\Box\)

Next we show that the wFBSDE (24) gives us an a-priori bound uniform in \(| \Lambda |\) on the minimiser in terms of weighted norms of \(\mathbb{W}\). For this we first need some analytic bounds.

Lemma 34. Assume that, for \(\delta > 0\) small enough, either

  1. \(\operatorname{supp} (\nabla f (W + Z)) \subset \Lambda\) and

    \(\displaystyle \| \nabla f (W + Z) \|_{L^{4 / 3} (\rho^{3 / 4})} \leqslant \delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} (W + Z) \|^3_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})} + C,\)

  2. \(\displaystyle \| \nabla f (W + Z) \|_{H^{- 1} (\rho^{1 / 2})} \leqslant \delta \| W + Z \|_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})} .\)

Then there exists a constant \(C < \infty\) such that

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \mathbb{E} \left| \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (Z \rho) + \lambda \int_{\Lambda} (4\mathbb{W}^3 \rho Z + 12\mathbb{W}^2 \rho Z^2 + 12 W \rho Z^3) \right|\\ & \leqslant & \delta \mathbb{E} \left( \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \rho Z^4 +\mathcal{E} (\rho^{1 / 2} Z) \right) + C. \end{eqnarray*}

Proof. First observe that we have that \(\int_{\Lambda} \rho Z^4 = \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \rho^{1 / 4} Z \|^4_{L^4}\), and \(\mathcal{E} (\rho^{1 / 2} Z) \geqslant C \| \rho^{1 / 2} Z \|^2_{H^1}\), for instance by fundamental theorem of calculus. By our assumptions on \(f\) we have, in the first case

\begin{eqnarray*} | \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (Z \rho) | & \leqslant & \| \nabla f (W + Z) \|_{L^{4 / 3} (\rho^{3 / 4})} \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|_{L^4 (\rho^{1 / 4})}\\ & \leqslant & \delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} (W + Z) \|^3_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})} \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|_{L^4 (\rho^{1 / 4})} + C\\ & \leqslant & C \delta (\| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^4_{L^4 (\rho^{1 / 4})} + \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} W \|^3_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})} \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|_{L^4 (\rho^{1 / 4})}) + C\\ & \leqslant & 2 \delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^4_{L^4 (\rho^{1 / 4})} + C \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} W \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})} + C. \end{eqnarray*}

While in the second case

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} | \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (Z \rho) | & \leqslant & \| \nabla f (W + Z) \|_{H^{- 1} (\rho^{1 / 2})} \| Z \|_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})}\\ & \leqslant & \delta \| W + Z \|_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})} \| Z \|_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})}\\ & \leqslant & \delta \| W \|_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})} \| Z \|_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})} + \delta \| Z \|^2_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})}\\ & \leqslant & \| W \|^2_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})} + 2 \delta \| Z \|^2_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})} . \end{array}\)

For the second term, by Cauchy–Schwarz we obtain

\(\displaystyle \left| \int_{\Lambda} \rho \mathbb{W}^3 Z \right| \leqslant C_{\delta} \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \mathbb{W}^3 \|^2_{H^{- 1} (\rho^{1 / 2})} + \delta \| Z \|^2_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})} .\)

For the third term, we use Lemma A.7 from [27] to get

\begin{eqnarray*} \left| \int_{\Lambda} \mathbb{W}^2 \rho Z^2 \right| & \leqslant & \| \rho^{1 / 8} \mathbb{W}^2 \|_{C^{- \varepsilon}} \| \rho^{7 / 8} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z^2 \|_{B_{1, 1}^{\varepsilon}}\\ & \leqslant & \| \rho^{1 / 8} \mathbb{W}^2 \|_{C^{- \varepsilon}} \| \rho^{3 / 8} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|_{L^2} \| \rho^{1 / 2} Z \|_{H^{2 \varepsilon}}\\ & \leqslant & C \| \rho^{1 / 8} \mathbb{W}^2 \|_{C^{- \varepsilon}} \| \rho^{1 / 4} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|_{L^4} \| \rho^{1 / 2} Z \|_{H^{2 \varepsilon}}\\ & \leqslant & C_{\delta} \| \rho^{1 / 8} \mathbb{W}^2 \|^4_{C^{- \varepsilon}} + \delta (\| \rho^{1 / 4} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^4_{L^4} + \| \rho^{1 / 2} Z \|^2_{H^{2 \varepsilon}}) . \end{eqnarray*}

For the final term we proceed similarly. By Lemma 29 we have \(\sup_{| \Lambda |} \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \|_{B_{p, p}^s (\rho^{\iota})} < \infty\) for any \(s < 1 / p\) and any \(\iota > 1 / 16\). Then with \(\varepsilon < 1 / 16\), for any \(\iota \in (1 / 16, 1 / 8)\) and writing \(\mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z^3 = (\mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z)^3\), we have by fractional Leibniz rule

\begin{eqnarray*} \left| \int_{\Lambda} \rho W Z^3 \right| & \leqslant & \| \rho^{1 / 8 - \iota} W \|_{\mathcal{C}^{- \varepsilon}} \| \rho^{3 / 8 + \iota} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|_{H^{\varepsilon}} {\| \rho^{1 / 4} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^2_{L^4}}_{}\\ & \lesssim & \| \rho^{1 / 8 - \iota} W \|_{\mathcal{C}^{- \varepsilon}} \| \rho^{5 / 8 + 3 \iota} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^{1 / 3}_{W^{3 \varepsilon, 1}} \| \rho^{1 / 4} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^{8 / 3}_{L^4}\\ & \leqslant & C \| \rho^{1 / 8 - \iota} W \|_{\mathcal{C}^{- \varepsilon}} \| \rho^{1 / 2} Z \|^{1 / 3}_{H^1} \| \rho^{1 / 4} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^{8 / 3}_{L^4}\\ & \leqslant & C_{\delta} \| \rho^{1 / 8 - \iota} W \|^6_{\mathcal{C}^{- \varepsilon}} + \delta (\| \rho^{1 / 2} Z \|^2_{H^1} + \| \rho^{1 / 4} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^4_{L^4}) . \end{eqnarray*}

In the second line we used the interpolation inequality of Proposition 11 with \(\theta = 1 / 3\) (the weights being commuted with the derivatives as explained above)

\(\displaystyle \| \rho^{3 / 8 + \iota} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|_{H^{\varepsilon}} \lesssim \| \rho^{5 / 8 + 3 \iota} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^{1 / 3}_{W^{3 \varepsilon, 1}} \| \rho^{1 / 4} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^{2 / 3}_{L^4},\)

(note that \(\frac{1}{2} = \frac{1 / 3}{1} + \frac{2 / 3}{4}\) and \(\frac{3}{8} + \iota = \frac{1}{3} \left( \frac{5}{8} + 3 \iota \right) + \frac{2}{3} \cdot \frac{1}{4}\)), which keeps the indicator \(\mathbb{1}_{\Lambda}\) on every factor \(Z\) measured in \(L^4\). In the third line we used the fractional Leibniz rule, Lemma 29 with \(p = 2\), \(s = 4 \varepsilon < 1 / 2\), and \(5 / 8 + 2 \iota \geqslant 1 / 2\):

\(\displaystyle \| \rho^{5 / 8 + 3 \iota} \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|_{W^{3 \varepsilon, 1}} \lesssim \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \|_{B_{2, 2}^{4 \varepsilon} (\rho^{\iota})} \| \rho^{5 / 8 + 2 \iota} Z \|_{H^{4 \varepsilon}} \lesssim \| \rho^{1 / 2} Z \|_{H^1},\)

uniformly in \(| \Lambda |\), while the last line is Young's inequality with exponents \((6, 6, 3 / 2)\). We can choose \(\iota = 1 / 8 - \varepsilon > 1 / 16\) to get the claim. \(\Box\)

Theorem 35. Assume that \(f\) satisfies the conditions of Lemma 34. There exists a constant \(C\) independent of \(| \Lambda |\) such that, for any minimiser \(\mu^{f, \Lambda} \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}\) of \(\check{F}^{f, \Lambda} (\mu)\) we have

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \Lambda}} \left[ 2 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \rho Z^4 +\mathcal{E} (\rho^{1 / 2} Z) \right] \leqslant C. \) (25)

Proof. Note that the map \(Z \mapsto \rho Z\) is continuous from \(\mathfrak{H}\) to \(\mathfrak{H}\). Take \(K = \rho Z\) in (24) to get that any minimiser \(\mu^{f, \Lambda} \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}\) satisfies

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \Lambda}} \left[ 4 \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \rho Z^4 + \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} ((1 - \Delta)^{1 / 2} \rho^{1 / 2} \dot{Z}_s)^2 \mathrm{d} s \right]\\ = & -\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \Lambda}} \left[ \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \dot{Z}_s [(1 - \Delta), \rho^{1 / 2}] \rho^{1 / 2} \dot{Z}_s \mathrm{d} s \right]\\ & -\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \Lambda}} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) (Z \rho) + \lambda \int_{\Lambda} \rho (4\mathbb{W}^3 Z + 12\mathbb{W}^2 Z^2 + 12 W Z^3) \right] . \end{array} \) (26)

From Lemma 36 below and Lemma 34 we deduce that the r.h.s. is controlled by a small fraction of the l.h.s. \(+\) a constant, from which we obtain the claim.\(\Box\)

Lemma 36. \(\)

\(\displaystyle \left| \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \dot{Z}_s [(1 - \Delta), \rho^{1 / 2}] \rho^{1 / 2} \dot{Z}_s \mathrm{d} s \right| \leqslant \varepsilon \left[ \int^1_0 \| \dot{Z}_s \|^2_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})} \mathrm{d} s \right]\)

Proof. We observe that \(1\) obviously commutes with \(\rho^{1 / 2}\), and

\(\displaystyle [\Delta, \rho^{1 / 2}] = (\Delta \rho^{1 / 2}) + 2 \nabla \rho^{1 / 2} \cdot \nabla .\)

Using the assumptions (4) on the weight \(\rho\) we deduce

\(\displaystyle \| [\Delta, \rho^{1 / 2}] f \|_{L^2} \leqslant \varepsilon (\| \rho^{1 / 2} f \|_{L^2} + \| \rho^{1 / 2} \nabla f \|_{L^2}) .\)
\(\Box\)

Let us now show that these estimates give tightness of \((\theta_{\Lambda})_{\Lambda}\) as \(| \Lambda | \rightarrow \infty\) on a suitable weighted Sobolev space.

Lemma 37. Consider \((\theta_{\Lambda})_{\Lambda}\) as a family of measures on \(H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho)\). We have for some \(\delta > 0\),

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\Lambda} \left[ \int \exp (\delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \phi \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho)}) \mathrm{d} \theta_{\Lambda} + \int \exp (\delta \| \phi \|^2_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho)}) \mathrm{d} \theta_{\Lambda} \right] < \infty .\)

In particular \((\theta_{\Lambda})_{\Lambda}\) is exponentially tight on \(H^{- 2 \varepsilon} (\rho)\) and any accumulation point \(\theta\) satisfies

\(\displaystyle \int \exp (\delta \| \phi \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho)}) \mathrm{d} \theta < \infty .\)

Proof. Since \(\rho \lesssim 1\) we have \(\| \cdot \|_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho)} \lesssim \| \cdot \|_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})}\) and \(\| \cdot \|_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho)} \lesssim \| \cdot \|_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})}\), so that, up to changing \(\delta\), it is enough to prove the bounds with the weights \(\rho^{1 / 2}\) and \(\rho^{1 / 4}\) respectively. Moreover bounded sets of \(H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})\) are relatively compact in \(H^{- 2 \varepsilon} (\rho)\), which gives the exponential tightness.

Let \(g_N \in C^{\infty} (\mathbb{R}, \mathbb{R})\) be such that \(g_N (x) = x\) for \(| x | < N\), with \(g_N\) bounded and \(0 \leqslant g_N' \leqslant 1\) (so that \(0 \leqslant g_N (x) \leqslant x\) for \(x \geqslant 0\)). Define \(f^N (\phi) = g_N (\delta \| \phi \|^2_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})})\) we have \(\nabla f^N (\phi) = (g_N)' (\delta \| \phi \|^2_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})}) 2 \delta \langle D \rangle^{- \varepsilon} (\rho \langle D \rangle^{- \varepsilon} \phi)\) so, since \(\rho \lesssim 1\),

\(\displaystyle \| \nabla f^N (W + Z) \|_{H^{- 1} (\rho^{1 / 2})} \leqslant C \delta \| W + Z \|_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})},\)

and this bound is uniform in \(N\). Since condition (b) of Lemma 34 only involves \(\nabla f\), it holds also for \(- f^N\), and Theorem 35 applied to the minimiser \(\mu^{- f^N, \Lambda}\) of \(\check{F}^{- f^N, \Lambda}\) gives

\(\displaystyle \sup_{N, \Lambda} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{- f^N, \Lambda}} [\| Z \|^2_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})}] < \infty .\)

Now \(\log \int \exp (f^N) \mathrm{d} \theta_{\Lambda} = -\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda} (- f^N)\), so the lower bound in eq. (18) (with \(- f^N\) in place of \(f\)) and \(0 \leqslant f^N (\phi) \leqslant \delta \| \phi \|^2_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})}\) give

\(\displaystyle \log \int \exp (f^N) \mathrm{d} \theta_{\Lambda} \leqslant \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{- f^N, \Lambda}} [f^N (W + Z)] \leqslant 2 \delta \mathbb{E} [\| W \|^2_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})}] + C \delta \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{- f^N, \Lambda}} [\| Z \|^2_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})}] \leqslant C < + \infty,\)

and taking the sup over \(N\) we get

\(\displaystyle \log \int \exp (\delta \| \phi \|^2_{H^{- \varepsilon} (\rho^{1 / 2})}) \mathrm{d} \theta_{\Lambda} \leqslant C\)

a fact that implies exponential tightness of \((\theta_{\Lambda})_{\Lambda}\). For the second statement we proceed similarly. Indeed if \(\theta\) is an accumulation point of \((\theta_{\Lambda})_{\Lambda}\) then

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \int \exp (\delta \| \phi \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho)}) \mathrm{d} \theta & \leqslant & \sup_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \int \exp (\delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \phi \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho)}) \mathrm{d} \theta\\ & \leqslant & \sup_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \limsup_{\Lambda \rightarrow \infty} \int \exp (\delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \phi \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho)}) \mathrm{d} \theta_{\Lambda}\\ & \leqslant & \sup_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \sup_{\Lambda \supseteq \tilde{\Lambda}} \int \exp (\delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \phi \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho)}) \mathrm{d} \theta_{\Lambda} . \end{array}\)

This gives the claim provided that for \(f^N (\phi) = g_N (\delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \phi \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})})\) we have

\(\displaystyle \sup_N \log \int \exp (f^N) \mathrm{d} \theta_{\Lambda} \leqslant C.\)

Indeed we compute \(\nabla f^N (\phi) = (g_N)' (\delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \phi \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})}) 4 \delta \mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \langle \mathrm{D} \rangle^{- \varepsilon} (\rho (\langle \mathrm{D} \rangle^{- \varepsilon} (\mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \phi))^3)\), from which, using that \(\langle \mathrm{D} \rangle^{- \varepsilon}\) is bounded on \(L^{4 / 3}\), that \(\rho \lesssim 1\) and that, for \(\varepsilon < 1 / 4\), multiplication by \(\mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}}\) is bounded on \(W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})\) uniformly in \(\tilde{\Lambda}\), we can check that

\(\displaystyle \| \nabla f^N (\phi) \|_{L^{4 / 3} (\rho^{3 / 4})} \leqslant C \delta \| \rho (\langle \mathrm{D} \rangle^{- \varepsilon} (\mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \phi))^3 \|_{L^{4 / 3}} \leqslant C \delta \| \rho^{1 / 4} \langle \mathrm{D} \rangle^{- \varepsilon} (\mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \phi) \|^3_{L^4} \leqslant C \delta \| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} \phi \|^3_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})},\)

and \(\operatorname{supp} (\nabla f^N (\phi)) \subseteq \Lambda\). Applying Lemma 34 (condition (a), which holds for \(- f^N\) as well) and Theorem 35 we get that the minimiser \(\mu^{- f^N, \Lambda}\) of \(\check{F}^{- f^N, \Lambda}\) satisfies

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\tilde{\Lambda}} \sup_{\Lambda \supseteq \tilde{\Lambda}} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{- f^N, \Lambda}} [\| \mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} Z \|^4_{L^4 (\rho^{1 / 4})}] \leqslant \sup_{\Lambda} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{- f^N, \Lambda}} [\| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^4_{L^4 (\rho^{1 / 4})}] = : C < \infty,\)

uniformly in \(N\). As a consequence, the lower bound in eq. (18) (with \(- f^N\) in place of \(f\)) gives, as above,

\(\displaystyle \log \int \exp (f^N) \mathrm{d} \theta_{\Lambda} \leqslant \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{- f^N, \Lambda}} [f^N (W + Z)] \leqslant 8 \delta \mathbb{E} [\| \mathbb{1}_{\tilde{\Lambda}} W \|^4_{W^{- \varepsilon, 4} (\rho^{1 / 4})}] + C \delta \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{- f^N, \Lambda}} [\| \mathbb{1}_{\Lambda} Z \|^4_{L^4 (\rho^{1 / 4})}] \leqslant C' < + \infty,\)

which implies the claim.\(\Box\)

We have actually a more refined result: let \(\mu^{f, \Lambda}\) be a minimiser for \(\check{F}^{f, \Lambda}\). By Theorem 35 the family \((\mu^{f, \Lambda})_{\Lambda}\) is tight in \(\mathcal{P} (\mathfrak{S} \times \hat{\mathfrak{H}}_w)\).

Lemma 38. Denote by \(\mu \in \mathcal{P} (\mathfrak{S} \times \hat{\mathfrak{H}}_w)\) any accumulation point of \((\mu^{f, \Lambda})_{\Lambda}\) as \(| \Lambda | \rightarrow \infty\). Then the following equation holds:

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot K + 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket K + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, K) \right] = 0. \) (27)

for any \(K : \mathfrak{S} \times \hat{\mathfrak{H}}_w \rightarrow \hat{\mathfrak{H}}_w\) continuous, adapted to the filtration generated by \((\mathbb{W}_t, Z_t)_{t \in [0, 1]}\) and compactly supported.

Proof. Let the support of \(K\) be contained in \(B_N (0)\) for some \(N \geqslant 0\). Let \(\chi \in C^{\infty}_c (\mathbb{R}^2)\) such that \(\chi = 1\) on \(B_N (0)\) so that \(K = \chi K\). Then Theorem 35 implies that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \Lambda}} [\| \chi Z \|^2_{H^1} + \| \chi Z \|^4_{L^4}] \leqslant C,\)

where the constant depends on \(N\) but not on \(\Lambda\) and we have

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot K + 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket K + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, K) \right]\\ & = & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (\chi K) + 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket \chi K + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, \chi K) \right] \end{eqnarray*}

Now observe that

\(\displaystyle 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket \chi K = H (\mathbb{W}, Z, K) + 4 \lambda \int Z^3 \chi K\)

where

\(\displaystyle H (\mathbb{W}, Z, K) = 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \mathbb{W}^3 \chi K + 12 \lambda \int \mathbb{W}^2 Z \chi K + 12 \lambda \int WZ^2 \chi K.\)

Let \(\tilde{\chi} \in C^{\infty}_c (\mathbb{R}^2)\) and \(\tilde{\chi} = 1\) on the support of \(\chi\). It is not hard to see from the definition that

\(\displaystyle | H (\mathbb{W}, Z, K) | \lesssim \| \tilde{\chi} \mathbb{W} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} (1 + \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{4 / 3}_{L^4} \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{2 / 3}_{H^1}) \| \chi^{1 / 3} K \|_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}}\)

Indeed,

\(\displaystyle \left| \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \mathbb{W}^3 \chi K \right| = \left| \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \tilde{\chi} \mathbb{W}^3 \chi K \right| \lesssim \| \tilde{\chi} \mathbb{W} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} \| \chi K \|_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}},\)

while, by using interpolation from the first to the second line, we have

\begin{eqnarray*} \left| \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \mathbb{W}^2 Z \chi K \right| & \lesssim & \| \chi^{1 / 3} \mathbb{W} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}} \| \chi^{1 / 3} K \|_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}}\\ & \lesssim & \| \chi^{1 / 3} \mathbb{W} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{2 / 3}_{L^4} \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{1 / 3}_{H^1} \| \chi^{1 / 3} K \|_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}}\\ & \lesssim & \| \tilde{\chi} \mathbb{W} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} (1 + \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{4 / 3}_{L^4} \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{2 / 3}_{H^1}) \| \chi^{1 / 3} K \|_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}} \end{eqnarray*}

and similarly

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \left| \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} W Z^2 \chi K \right|\\ & \lesssim & \| \tilde{\chi} \mathbb{W} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^2_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}} \| \chi^{1 / 3} K \|_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}}\\ & \lesssim & \| \tilde{\chi} \mathbb{W} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{4 / 3}_{L^4} \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{2 / 3}_{H^1} \| \chi^{1 / 3} K \|_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}} . \end{eqnarray*}

From this we get

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} & & \left| \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (\chi K) + 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket \chi K + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, \chi K) \right|\\ & \lesssim & \| \tilde{\chi} (W + Z) \|_{\mathcal{C}^{- \varepsilon}} \| \chi K \|_{H^1} + \left( \int^1_0 \| \chi^{1 / 2} \dot{Z}_t \|^2_{H^1} \mathrm{d} t \right)^{1 / 2} \left( \int^1_0 \| \chi^{1 / 2} \dot{K}_t \|^2_{H^1} \mathrm{d} t \right)^{1 / 2}\\ & & + \| \tilde{\chi} \mathbb{W} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} (1 + \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{4 / 3}_{L^4} \| \chi^{1 / 3} Z \|^{2 / 3}_{H^1}) \| \chi^{1 / 3} K \|_{B_{3, 3}^{1 / 3}}\\ & & + \| \chi^{1 / 4} Z \|^3_{L^4} \| \chi^{1 / 4} K \|_{L^4} . \end{array}\)

The r.h.s. of this last equation is integrable by assumptions on \(K\) and bounds on \(Z\). From Lemmas 12 and 14 of [5] we know that there exists a family \((K^M)_{M \geqslant 1}\) adapted to the filtration generated by \((W_t)_t\) with

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rll} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| \chi^{1 / 4} K \|^4_{L^4} - \| \chi^{1 / 4} K^M \|^4_{L^4}] & \rightarrow & 0,\\ \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \left( \int^1_0 \| \chi^{1 / 2} (\dot{K}_t) \|^2_{H^1} \mathrm{d} t \right) - \int^1_0 \| \chi^{1 / 2} (\dot{K}^M_t) \|^2_{H^1} \mathrm{d} t \right] & \rightarrow & 0, \end{array} \) (28)

as \(M \rightarrow \infty\). After possibly passing to a subsequence, we can assume that \(\chi^{1 / 4} K^M \rightarrow \chi^{1 / 4} K\) in \(L_w^4 (\mu, L_w^4 (\mathbb{R}^2))\) where \(L_w^4\) denotes \(L^4\) with the weak topology, and in \(L_w^2 (\mu, L^2 (\mathbb{R}_+ \times \mathbb{R}^2))\). This together with the bound (28) implies that

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \lim_{M \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (\chi K^M) + 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket \chi K^M + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, \chi K^M) \right]\\ & = & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (\chi K) + 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket \chi K + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, \chi K) \right] . \end{eqnarray*}

At this point we have reduced the problem to showing that

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \lim_{\Lambda \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \Lambda}} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (\chi K^M) + 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket \chi K^M + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, \chi K^M) \right]\\ & = & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (\chi K^M) + 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket \chi K^M + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, \chi K^M) \right] \end{eqnarray*}

since by assumption

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \Lambda}} \left[ \nabla f (W + Z) \cdot (\chi K^M) + 4 \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \llbracket (W + Z)^3 \rrbracket \chi K^M + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, \chi K^M) \right] = 0.\)

This follows from the weak convergence \(\mu^{f, \Lambda} \rightarrow \mu\) and the bound

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \Lambda}} [\| \chi Z \|^2_{H^1} + \| \chi Z \|^4_{L^4}] \leqslant C,\)

since we can approximate \(Z\) by bounded admissible controls \((Z^L)_L\) defined as \(Z^L_t = Z_{t \wedge T_L}\) with the stopping time

\(\displaystyle T_L := 1 \wedge \inf \{ t \in [0, 1] : \| \chi Z_t \|^2_{H^1} + \| \chi Z_t \|^4_{L^4} \geqslant L \},\)

in such a way that

\(\displaystyle \sup_{t \in [0, 1]} \| \chi Z_t^L \|^2_{H^1} + \| \chi Z_t^L \|^4_{L^4} \leqslant L,\)

almost surely. Note that \(T_L \rightarrow 1\) as \(L \rightarrow \infty\) and therefore indeed \(Z^L_t \rightarrow Z_t\) as \(L \rightarrow \infty\).\(\Box\)

To summarise the result of this section: we obtained tightness of the finite volume \(\varphi^4_2\) measures and described any possible infinite volume limit via a novel kind of stochastic equation, the wFBSDE (27). Note that it cannot be interpreted anymore as the first order condition of a functional.

Unfortunately, due to the strongly non-convex character of the renormalised potential we have currently limited information on its solutions. One expects that in the “single phase region” [24] (e.g. where \(\lambda\) is small) there should be uniqueness, and alternatively that multiple solutions should appear in the “two phase region”. Despite these limitations eq. (27) is useful and in the next section we show that it allows us to prove large deviations for \(\varphi^4_2\) in the semiclassical limit.

4.1Large deviations

In this section we want to investigate the large deviations of \(\varphi_2^4\) in the infinite volume in the semiclassical limit \(\hbar \rightarrow 0\) where \(\hbar > 0\) is a parameter which is inserted in the model as follows. For any \(f : \mathcal{S}' (\mathbb{R}^2) \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\), let \(\mathcal{W}^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} (f)\) be defined by

\(\displaystyle e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} \mathcal{W}^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} (f)} := \int e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} f (\phi)} \theta^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} (\mathrm{d} \phi),\)

where

\(\displaystyle \theta^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} (\mathrm{d} \phi) := \frac{\exp \left( - \frac{1}{\hbar} V_{\Lambda} (\phi) \right) \theta^{0, \hbar} (\mathrm{d} \phi)}{\int \exp \left( - \frac{1}{\hbar} V_{\Lambda} (\phi) \right) \theta^{0, \hbar} (\mathrm{d} \phi)} .\)

Here \(\theta^{0, \hbar}\) is the Gaussian measure on \(\mathcal{S}' (\mathbb{R}^2)\) with covariance \(\hbar (1 - \Delta)^{- 1}\) and the Wick ordering is now taken with respect to \(\theta^{0, \hbar}\). With some minor modifications to the derivation in the previous section we have the identity

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} (f) = \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \check{F}^{f, \Lambda, \hbar} (\mu) - \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \check{F}^{0, \Lambda, \hbar} (\mu),\)

where

\(\displaystyle \check{F}^{f, \Lambda, \hbar} (\mu) := \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W + Z) + V_{\Lambda} (\hbar^{1 / 2} W + Z_{}) +\mathcal{E} (Z)], \qquad \mu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}} .\)

Let \(\mu^{f, \Lambda, \hbar}\) be a minimiser for this admissible \(\check{F}^{f, \Lambda, \hbar}\). Reasoning as in Lemma 37 above we have that the family \((\mu^{f, \Lambda, \hbar})_{\Lambda, \hbar}\) is exponentially tight for any bounded and continuous \(f\). For each \(\hbar > 0\) we pick a weakly convergent subsequence of \((\mu^{0, \Lambda, \hbar})_{\Lambda}\) with limit \(\mu^{0, \hbar}\) (and, for a given \(f\), a further subsequence along which \(\mu^{f, \Lambda, \hbar} \rightarrow \mu^{f, \hbar}\)). Since \(\theta^{\hbar}_{\Lambda}\) is the law of \(\hbar^{1 / 2} W_1 + Z_1\) under \(\mu^{0, \Lambda, \hbar}\), we have then also \(\theta^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} \rightarrow \theta^{\hbar} =\operatorname{Law}_{\mu^{0, \hbar}} (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_1 + Z_1)\) along the same subsequence and clearly, for all bounded continuous \(f\) we have

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} (f) \rightarrow \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (f),\)

where \(\mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (f)\) is now defined as

\(\displaystyle e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (f)} := \int e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} f (\phi)} \theta^{\hbar} (\mathrm{d} \phi) .\)

Even though in this setting we do not have sufficient information to establish uniqueness of the limiting measures \((\mu^{f, \hbar})_{\hbar}\), we are still able to obtain a Laplace principle as \(\hbar \rightarrow 0\) for the corresponding family \((\theta^{\hbar})_{\hbar}\). Note that the family \((\theta^{\hbar})_{\hbar}\) is exponentially tight by Lemma 37.

Theorem 39. Any family of accumulation points \((\theta^{\hbar})_{\hbar}\) of \((\theta^{\hbar}_{\Lambda})_{\hbar, \Lambda}\) satisfies a Laplace principle on \(\mathcal{S}' (\mathbb{R}^2)\) with rate function

\(\displaystyle J (\phi) = \left\{ \begin{array}{lll} \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \phi^4 + \frac{1}{2} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \phi (1 - \Delta) \phi, & & \text{for $\phi \in H^1 (\mathbb{R}^2)$;}\\ + \infty, & & \text{otherwise.} \end{array} \right.\)

That is for \(f : \mathcal{S}' (\mathbb{R}^2) \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\) continuous and bounded

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (f) = \inf_{\psi \in \mathcal{S}' (\mathbb{R}^2)} \{ f (\psi) + J (\psi) \} .\)

Proof. Assume that \(f\) is of the form

\(\displaystyle f (\psi) = C \int \rho ((1 - \Delta)^{- 1 / 2} (\psi - \varphi))^2 \) (29)

for \(C \geqslant 0\) and \(\varphi \in C^{\infty}_c (\mathbb{R}^2)\).

It is enough to prove the theorem for this particular family of functions since we already know that \((\theta^{\hbar})_{\hbar}\) are exponentially tight, and the family given by (29) is rate function determining in the sense of Definition 3.15 of [20], as it isolates points and is bounded below.

From (20) we have

\(\displaystyle \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} \alpha} \mathcal{W}^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} (\alpha f) =\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\alpha f, \Lambda, \hbar}} [f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W + Z)] = \frac{\int f (\phi) e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} \alpha f (\phi)} \theta^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} (\mathrm{d} \phi)}{\int e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} \alpha f (\phi)} \theta^{\hbar}_{\Lambda} (\mathrm{d} \phi)} .\)

By taking a further subsequence we can also have that \(\mu^{\alpha f, \Lambda, \hbar} \rightarrow \mu^{\alpha f, \hbar}\). We can then pass to the limit and obtain

\(\displaystyle \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} \alpha} \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (\alpha f) =\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\alpha f, \hbar}} [f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W + Z)],\)

for any \(f, \alpha\) and arbitrarily chosen accumulation point \(\mu^{\alpha f, \hbar}\). Note that we do not have to do a diagonal argument over the uncountably many choices of \(\alpha, f\): for any given \(\alpha, f\) we need just to take a subsequence of the sequence chosen to have \((\theta^{\hbar}_{\Lambda})_{\Lambda}\) converge in order to establish the equality.

Now recall that the minimiser \(\mu^{f, \hbar}\) satisfies an infinite volume wFBSDE of the form

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} 0 & = & \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \hbar}} \left[ \nabla f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W + Z) \cdot H + 4 \lambda \hbar^{3 / 2} \int \mathbb{W}^3 H + 12 \lambda \hbar \int \mathbb{W}^2 Z H \right.\\ & & \left. + 12 \lambda \hbar^{1 / 2} \int W (Z)^2 H + 4 \lambda \int Z^3 H + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, H) \right] \end{array} \) (30)

for any \(H\) with compact support. Furthermore we can carry over the proof of Lemma 34, and obtain that

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\hbar < 1} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{f, \hbar}} [\| Z \|_{L^4 (\rho^{1 / 4})}^4 + \| Z \|^2_{H^1 (\rho^{1 / 2})}] < \infty .\)

This implies that the family \((\mu^{f, \hbar})_{\hbar}\) is also tight and therefore we can consider any accumulation point \(\mu^f\) as \(\hbar \rightarrow 0\) which will satisfy the same estimate. By passing to the limit in (30) via by now standard estimates similar to those in Lemma 38 we obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} 0 & = & \mathbb{E}_{\mu^f} \left[ \nabla f (Z) \cdot H + 4 \lambda \int Z^3 H + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, H) \right], \end{array} \) (31)

valid for any adapted and compactly supported \(H\). Note that this equation does not contain anymore the GFF. This equation has a unique solution. Indeed if \(\nu\) is another solution we can couple the two solutions in \(\upsilon \in \bar{\mathcal{Y}} (\mu^f)\) such that \(\nu =\operatorname{Law}_{\upsilon} (W, Z + K)\) and have

\(\displaystyle 0 =\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ [\nabla f (Z + K) - \nabla f (Z)] \cdot H + 4 \lambda \int [(Z + K)^3 - Z^3] H + 2\mathcal{E} (K, H) \right] .\)

Now taking \(H = \chi K\) for \(\chi \in C^{\infty}_c (\mathbb{R}^2)\) and \(\chi \geqslant 0\), we obtain

\(\displaystyle 0 =\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ [\nabla f (Z + K) - \nabla f (Z)] \cdot (\chi K) + 4 \lambda \int \chi [(Z + K)^3 - Z^3] K + 2\mathcal{E} (K, \chi K) \right] .\)

By convexity of \(f\) and \((\cdot)^4\) we have that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ [\nabla f (Z + K) - \nabla f (Z)] \cdot (\chi K) + 4 \lambda \int \chi [(Z + K)^3 - Z^3] K \right] \geqslant 0.\)

Sending \(\chi \rightarrow 1\) we obtain that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} [\mathcal{E} (K, K)] \leqslant 0 \Rightarrow K = 0 \Rightarrow \nu = \mu^f .\)

Therefore \(\mu^f\) is the unique minimiser of the admissible (convex) functional

\(\displaystyle \check{F} (\nu) := \mathbb{E}_{\nu} \left[ f (Z) + \lambda \int Z^4 +\mathcal{E} (Z, Z) \right] .\)

Let \(\nu^{\phi} =\operatorname{Law}_{\mathbb{P}} (W, s \mapsto s \phi)\) for \(\phi \in H^1\) then

\(\displaystyle \check{F} (\nu^{\phi}) = f (\phi) + \lambda \int \phi^4 + \frac{1}{2} \int \phi (1 - \Delta) \phi = f (\phi) + J (\phi) .\)

Using the fact that the variational problem in the time variable for the functional

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{E} (Z, Z) = \frac{1}{2} \int^1_0 \int \dot{Z}_s (1 - \Delta) \dot{Z}_s \mathrm{d} s\)

is minimised for \(\dot{Z}_s\) constant, we have for any \(\nu\)

\(\displaystyle \check{F} (\nu) =\mathbb{E}_{\nu} \left[ f (Z) + \lambda \int Z^4 +\mathcal{E} (Z, Z) \right]\)
\(\displaystyle \geqslant \mathbb{E}_{\nu} \left[ f (Z) + \lambda \int Z^4 + \frac{1}{2} \int Z_1 (1 - \Delta) Z_1 \right] =\mathbb{E}_{\nu} [f (Z) + J (Z)]\)
\(\displaystyle \geqslant \inf_{\phi \in H^1} [f (\phi) + J (\phi)] .\)

As a consequence we obtain that

\(\displaystyle \inf_{\nu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}} \check{F} (\nu) = \inf_{\phi \in H^1} [f (\phi) + J (\phi)],\)

and that \(\mu^f = \nu^{\phi^f}\) for the unique \(\phi^f\) which attains the convex deterministic minimisation problem on the r.h.s..

To sum up, we have established that

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} \alpha} \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (\alpha f) = \lim_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\alpha f, \hbar}} [f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W + Z)] = f (\phi^{\alpha f}) .\) (32)

Integrating eq. (32) and using dominated convergence

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (\alpha f) = \int^{\alpha}_0 \lim_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} \beta} \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (\beta f) \mathrm{d} \beta = \int^{\alpha}_0 \lim_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\beta f, \hbar}} [f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W + Z)] \mathrm{d} \beta\)
\(\displaystyle = \int^{\alpha}_0 f (\phi^{\beta f}) \mathrm{d} \beta =: \mathcal{W}^0 (\alpha f) .\)

This also implies that the derivative in \(\alpha \in [0, 1]\) of \(\alpha \mapsto \mathcal{W}^0 (\alpha f)\) exists and

\(\displaystyle \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} \alpha} \mathcal{W}^0 (\alpha f) = f (\phi^{\alpha f}) .\)

To conclude it is enough to show that this coincides with

\(\displaystyle \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} \alpha} \widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^0 (\alpha f),\)

where

\(\displaystyle \widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^0 (\alpha f) = \inf_{\psi \in H^1 (\mathbb{R}^2)} \{ \alpha f (\psi) + J (\psi) \} .\)

Recall that \(\phi^{\alpha f}\) is the minimiser of \(\phi \mapsto \alpha f (\phi) + J (\phi)\) so the Euler–Lagrange equation gives

\(\displaystyle \alpha \nabla f (\phi^{\alpha f}) \cdot h + \nabla J (\phi^{\alpha f}) \cdot h = 0.\)

At this point we note that

\(\displaystyle 0 = \alpha [\nabla f (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) - \nabla f (\phi^{\alpha f})] \cdot (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f})\)
\(\displaystyle + [\nabla J (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) - \nabla J (\phi^{\alpha f})] \cdot (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f})\)
\(\displaystyle + \gamma \nabla f (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) \cdot (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f}) .\)

By convexity of \(f\) and strong convexity of \(J\) we have

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \| \phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f} \|^2_{H^1}\\ & \lesssim & [\alpha (\nabla f (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) - \nabla f (\phi^{\alpha f})) + \nabla J (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) - \nabla J (\phi^{\alpha f})] \cdot (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f}) . \end{eqnarray*}

Now

\(\displaystyle \gamma \int \nabla f (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) \cdot (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f}) \mathrm{d} x \lesssim \gamma \| \nabla f (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) \|_{H^1} \| \phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f} \|_{H^1}\)

so

\(\displaystyle \| (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f}) \|_{H^1}^2 \lesssim \gamma \| \nabla f (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) \|_{H^1} \| \phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f} \|_{H^1}\)

so dividing by \(\| \phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f} \|_{H^1}\) we have

\(\displaystyle \| \phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f} \|_{H^1} \leqslant \gamma \| \nabla f (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) \|_{H^1} \leqslant \gamma (\| \phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} \|_{H^1} + C) \lesssim \gamma .\)

We can then calculate

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^0 ((\alpha + \gamma) f) - \widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^0 (\alpha f) & = & \gamma f (\phi^{ (\alpha + \gamma) f}) + \alpha (f (\phi^{ (\alpha + \gamma) f}) - f (\phi^{\alpha f}))\\ & & + J (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) - J (\phi^{\alpha f}) . \end{array}\)

Given that \(\| \phi^{ (\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f} \|_{H^1} \lesssim \gamma\) we have, since by Sobolev embedding \(\| \cdot \|^4_{L^4}\) is locally Lipschitz in the \(H^1\) norm

\begin{eqnarray*} & & (\alpha (f (\phi^{ (\alpha + \gamma) f}) - f (\phi^{\alpha f})) + J (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f}) - J (\phi^{\alpha f}))\\ & = & \alpha \nabla f (\phi^{\alpha f}) (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f}) + \int (1 - \Delta) (\phi^{\alpha f}) (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f}) \\ + 4 \lambda \int (\phi^{\alpha f})^3 (\phi^{(\alpha + \gamma) f} - \phi^{\alpha f}) + O (\gamma^2)\\ & = & O (\gamma^2), \end{eqnarray*}

where in the last line we used the Euler–Lagrange equation for \(\phi^{\alpha f}\). This implies in turn

\(\displaystyle \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} \alpha} \widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^0 (\alpha f) = \lim_{\gamma \rightarrow 0} \frac{1}{\gamma} (\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^0 ((\alpha + \gamma) f) - \widetilde{\mathcal{W}}^0 (\alpha f)) = \lim_{\gamma \rightarrow 0} \frac{1}{\gamma} \gamma f (\phi^{ (\alpha + \gamma) f}) = f (\phi^{\alpha f})\)

which concludes the proof.\(\Box\)

5Exponential interaction

In this section we will discuss a variational description of the exponential interaction, that is the measure

\(\displaystyle \nu^{\xi, \exp} (\mathrm{d} \phi) = \frac{e^{- V^{\xi} (\phi)} \theta^0 (\mathrm{d} \phi)}{\int e^{- V^{\xi} (\phi)} \theta^0 (\mathrm{d} \phi)},\)

where now \(V^{\xi}\) is given by

\(\displaystyle V^{\xi} (\phi) = \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \llbracket \exp (\beta \phi) \rrbracket,\)

with some \(\beta^2 \in (0, 8 \pi)\) and where \(M^{\beta} := \llbracket \exp (\beta \phi) \rrbracket\) is the Gaussian Multiplicative Chaos (GMC) and \(\xi \in C^{\infty}_c (\mathbb{R}^2, \mathbb{R}_+)\) is a (smooth and compactly supported) spatial cutoff. We start with some preliminaries about the rigorous construction of the GMC.

This model is also known as the \(\exp (\beta \varphi)_2\) model (or Høegh–Krohn model), and has been introduced in [35]. See e.g. [2] for a discussion of its stochastic quantisation in the elliptic setting. For this model we repeat the construction of the variational description of the infinite volume measure. At variance with what happens in \(P (\varphi)_2\) theories, renormalisation does not spoil the convexity properties of the potential and therefore the study of the related wFBSDE can go further and in particular we will be able to prove the necessary locality of the perturbed equation. This in turn will allow us to prove the \(\Gamma\)-convergence of the variational problem along with the infinite volume limit and establish eq. (15) in its rigorous form.

As a result of this analysis, we are able to obtain a concise and well-behaved description of an infinite volume Gibbs measure related to an Euclidean quantum field.

We start with a definition and some properties of the GMC, i.e. the renormalised exponential of the GFF.

Proposition 40. Let \(W\) be a GFF and \(W_{\varepsilon} = \psi_{\varepsilon} \ast W\) where \(\psi_{\varepsilon} \rightarrow \delta_0\) and \(\psi_{\varepsilon} \in \mathcal{S} (\mathbb{R}^2)\) and let \(\beta^2 \in (0, 8 \pi)\). The Borel measures \((M^{\beta, \varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}\) on \(\mathbb{R}^2\) given by

\(\displaystyle M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (A) = \int_A \exp \left( \beta W_{\varepsilon} (x) - \frac{\beta^2}{2} \mathbb{E} [W^2_{\varepsilon} (x)] \right) \mathrm{d} x, \qquad A \in \mathcal{B} (\mathbb{R}^2),\)

converges almost surely set-wise to a measure

\(\displaystyle M^{\beta} (A) = \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (A), \qquad A \in \mathcal{B} (\mathbb{R}^2) . \) (33)

We call \(M^{\beta}\) the Gaussian multiplicative chaos (GMC) with parameter \(\beta\) associated to the GFF \(W\). It enjoys the following properties

  1. For \(1 < p < 8 \pi / \beta^2\) and for any compact set \(A \subseteq \mathbb{R}^2\)

    \(\displaystyle \sup_{\varepsilon} \mathbb{E} [| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (A) |^p] < \infty .\)

    Consequently

    \(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [| M^{\beta} (A) |^p] < \infty .\)

  2. For \(1 < p < 8 \pi / \beta^2\)

    \(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (B (0, \lambda)) |^p]^{1 / p} \leqslant C \lambda^{2 - (p - 1) \beta^2 / 4 \pi} \mathbb{E} [| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (B (0, 1)) |^p]^{1 / p},\)

    where \(B (x, r) \subseteq \mathbb{R}^2\) denotes the open ball centred in \(x\) of radius \(r\).

Proof. For a proof of (33) see [9]. The property a) is due to Kahane [39], while the estimate in b) is due to Hoshino, Kawabi and Kusuoka [36].\(\Box\)

From these properties we can determine easily the Besov regularity of \(M^{\beta}\) following [36] (Section 2).

Proposition 41. Assume that \(s > (\beta^2 / (4 \pi)) (p - 1)\), \(1 < p < 8 \pi / \beta^2\), then

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [\| M^{\beta} \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- s} (\rho^{1 / 2})}] < \infty .\)

Moreover \(\sup_{\varepsilon} \mathbb{E} [\| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- s} (\rho^{1 / 2})}] < \infty\) and \(\mathbb{E} [\| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - M^{\beta} \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- s} (\rho^{1 / 2})}] \rightarrow 0\) as \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\).

Proof. We have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathbb{E} [\| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- s} (\rho^{1 / 2})}] & = & \sum_{i \geqslant 0} 2^{- isp} \mathbb{E} \| \Delta_i M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \|_{L^p (\rho^{1 / 2})}^p\\ & \leqslant & C \sum_{i \geqslant 0} 2^{- i sp} \mathbb{E} | \Delta_i M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (0) |^p . \end{array}\)

Recall that the Littlewood–Paley operators \((\Delta_i)_{i \geqslant - 1}\) are defined by \(\Delta_i = \psi (\mathrm{D} / 2^i)\) for \(i \geqslant 0\) and some Schwartz function \(\psi : \mathbb{R}^2 \rightarrow \mathbb{R}_{\geqslant 0}\). Now using translation invariance of the law of \(M^{\beta, \varepsilon}\) and the fact that \(\mathcal{F}^{- 1} \psi\) is a Schwartz function we estimate

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathbb{E} | \Delta_i M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (0) |^p & = & 2^{2 p i} \mathbb{E} \left| \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} (\mathcal{F}^{- 1} \psi) (2^i x) M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (x) \mathrm{d} x \right|^p\\ & \leqslant & \sum_{n \in \mathbb{Z}^2} (1 + | n |^2)^{- 2} \mathbb{E} \left| \int_{B (n, 1)} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (2^{- i} x) \mathrm{d} x \right|^p\\ & = & \sum_{n \in \mathbb{Z}^2} (1 + | n |^2)^{- 2} 2^{2 p i} \mathbb{E} \left| \int_{B (2^{- i} n, 2^{- i})} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (x) \mathrm{d} x \right|^p\\ & \lesssim & 2^{p (p - 1) \beta^2 i / (4 \pi)} . \end{array}\)

Since the constants are uniform in \(\varepsilon\) (and the block \(i = - 1\) is treated in the same way), summing over \(i\) gives \(\sup_{\varepsilon} \mathbb{E} \| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- s} (\rho^{1 / 2})} < \infty\) for every \(s > (\beta^2 / (4 \pi)) (p - 1)\).

For the convergence, fix \(i \geqslant - 1\) and \(x \in \mathbb{R}^2\). We have \(\Delta_i M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (x) = \int K_i (x - y) M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (\mathrm{d} y)\) with a Schwartz kernel \(K_i\). The almost sure set-wise convergence (33), applied to a countable family of dyadic squares, and the positivity of the measures imply that \(\int g \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \rightarrow \int g \mathrm{d} M^{\beta}\) almost surely for every continuous compactly supported \(g\); since moreover \(\mathbb{E} [M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (\mathrm{d} y)] = \mathrm{d} y\), the contribution of the tails of \(K_i\) is small in \(L^1 (\mathbb{P})\) uniformly in \(\varepsilon\), and therefore \(\Delta_i M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (x) \rightarrow \Delta_i M^{\beta} (x)\) in probability. The estimate above, with \(p\) replaced by some \(\tilde{p} \in (p, 8 \pi / \beta^2)\), gives \(\sup_{\varepsilon, x} \mathbb{E} | \Delta_i M^{\beta, \varepsilon} (x) |^{\tilde{p}} < \infty\), and by Fatou's lemma the same bound holds for \(M^{\beta}\). By uniform integrability \(\mathbb{E} | \Delta_i (M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - M^{\beta}) (x) |^p \rightarrow 0\) boundedly in \(x\), so that, integrating in \(x\) as in the first step of the proof, \(\mathbb{E} \| \Delta_i (M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - M^{\beta}) \|^p_{L^p (\rho^{1 / 2})} \rightarrow 0\) for every \(i\). Finally take \(s' \in ((\beta^2 / (4 \pi)) (p - 1), s)\). By Fatou's lemma \(\mathbb{E} \| M^{\beta} \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- s'} (\rho^{1 / 2})} \leqslant \liminf_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E} \| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- s'} (\rho^{1 / 2})} < \infty\) (which gives in particular the first claim of the proposition), hence, uniformly in \(\varepsilon\),

\(\displaystyle \sum_{i \geqslant I} 2^{- isp} \mathbb{E} \| \Delta_i (M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - M^{\beta}) \|^p_{L^p (\rho^{1 / 2})} \leqslant 2^{- I (s - s') p} \mathbb{E} \| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - M^{\beta} \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- s'} (\rho^{1 / 2})} \lesssim 2^{- I (s - s') p} .\)

Together with the convergence of each block this gives \(\mathbb{E} \| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - M^{\beta} \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- s} (\rho^{1 / 2})} \rightarrow 0\).\(\Box\)

In this model we let \(\mathfrak{S} := \mathfrak{S}_0 \times \mathfrak{M}\), where \(\mathfrak{M}\) is the set of positive Radon measures on \(\mathbb{R}^2\) which belong to \(B_{p, p}^{- s} (\rho^{1 / 2})\), for some fixed \(p, s\) as in Proposition 41, endowed with the topology of \(B_{p, p}^{- s} (\rho^{1 / 2})\) (a closed cone in a separable Banach space, hence a Polish space). By Proposition 41, \(M^{\beta} \in \mathfrak{M}\) almost surely; note that \(M^{\beta}\) has infinite total mass, so it is not a probability measure on \(\mathbb{R}^2\). Then \(\mathbb{W}= (W, M^{\beta}) : \mathfrak{S}_0 \rightarrow \mathfrak{S}\) will be the appropriate enhanced GFF. By the usual abuse of notation we will denote also \(\mathbb{W}= (W, M^{\beta})\) the canonical process on \(\mathfrak{S}\).

We also need a slightly improved definition of the relaxed trial space \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\).

Definition 42. Set

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{X}^{\exp} := \{ \mu =\operatorname{Law} (W, M^{\beta}, Z) \in \mathcal{P} (\mathfrak{S} \times \widehat{\mathfrak{H}}_w) : Z \in \widehat{\mathfrak{H}}^a \text{with } \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z \|_{\mathfrak{\hat{H}}}^2] < \infty \},\)

and

\(\displaystyle \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp} := \{ \mu \in \mathcal{P} (\mathfrak{S} \times \widehat{\mathfrak{H}}_w) : \exists (\mu_n)_n \subseteq \mathcal{X}^{\exp} : \mu_n \rightarrow \mu \text{weakly}, \sup_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [\| Z \|_{\widehat{\mathfrak{H}}}^2] < \infty \}\)

We equip \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\) with the following topology: a sequence \((\mu_n)_n \subseteq \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\) converges to \(\mu\) if

  1. \(\mu_n \rightarrow \mu\) weakly in \(\mathcal{P} (\mathfrak{S} \times \widehat{\mathfrak{H}}_w)\),

  2. \(\sup_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [\| Z \|_{\mathfrak{\hat{H}} }^2] < \infty\).

For technical reasons we first establish the variational formulation for the approximate potential \(V^{\xi, \varepsilon}\) where

\(\displaystyle V^{\xi, \varepsilon} (\phi) = \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \llbracket \exp (\beta \psi_{\varepsilon} \ast \phi) \rrbracket\)

and the Wick ordering is now taken with respect to the covariance of \(W_{\varepsilon} = \psi_{\varepsilon} \ast W\).

Lemma 43. The potential \(V^{\xi, \varepsilon}\) is admissible and

\(\displaystyle \check{F}^{f, \xi, \varepsilon} (\mu) := \check{F}^{V^{\xi, f, \varepsilon}} (\mu) =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ f (W + Z) + \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \exp (\beta (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z)) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} +\mathcal{E} (Z) \right]\)

for all \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\).

Proof. Note that \(M^{\beta, \varepsilon}\) is positive and that Proposition 41 implies that for \(p\) close enough to \(1\) and \(\frac{1}{p} + \frac{1}{q} = 1\)

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [\exp (- q V^{\xi, \varepsilon})] +\mathbb{E} [| V^{\xi, \varepsilon} |^p] < \infty,\)

so \(V^{\xi, \varepsilon}\) is a tame functional. From the properties of the exponential it is clear that

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \llbracket \exp (\beta (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z) + \beta W_{\varepsilon}) \rrbracket \mathrm{d} x\\ & = & \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \exp (\beta (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z)) \llbracket \exp (\beta W_{\varepsilon}) \rrbracket \mathrm{d} x\\ & = & \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \exp (\beta (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z)) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} . \end{eqnarray*}

We have for all \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\):

\begin{eqnarray*} \check{F}^{f, \xi, \varepsilon} (\mu) & := & \check{F}^{V^{\xi, f, \varepsilon}} (\mu)\\ & = & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ f (W + Z) + \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \exp (\beta (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z)) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} +\mathcal{E} (Z) \right]\\ & \geqslant & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{E} (Z)] - C \geqslant \frac{1}{2} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z \|_{\widehat{\mathfrak{H}}}^2] - C \end{eqnarray*}

from which also the coercivity of \(\check{F}^{V^{\xi, f, \varepsilon}}\) is clear. Furthermore it is not hard to see that

\(\displaystyle (W, Z) \rightarrow \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \llbracket \exp (\beta (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z) + \beta (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast W)) \rrbracket \mathrm{d} x\)

is continuous as a map \((\mathfrak{S}_0, H^1) \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\) (note that here the renormalisation is finite) and convergence of \(W, Z \text{ in } (\mathfrak{S}_0, H^1)\) implies convergence in \(L^{\infty} \times L^{\infty}\) of \(\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z, \psi_{\varepsilon} \ast W\). To conclude admissibility it remains to show that \(\check{F}^{V^{\xi, f, \varepsilon}}\) is lower semicontinuous on \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\). Since we have already shown the interaction to be continuous it remains to do this for \(\mathcal{E}\). Indeed we have for any \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\) and any sequence \((\mu_n)_n\) such that \(\mu_n \rightarrow \mu\) in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\)

\(\displaystyle \liminf_n \mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [\mathcal{E} (Z)] \geqslant \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{E} (Z)],\)

this implies lower semicontinuity.\(\Box\)

We now show that we can remove the ultraviolet cutoff in the variational formulation. We introduce the unregularized functional

\(\displaystyle \check{F}^{f, \xi} (\mu) = \check{F}^{V^{\xi, f}} (\mu) =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ f (W + Z) + \lambda \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \exp (\beta Z) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} +\mathcal{E} (Z) \right] .\)

We will show that as \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\) \(\check{F}^{f, \xi, \varepsilon}\) \(\Gamma\)-converges to \(\check{F}^{f, \xi}\).

\(\Gamma\)-convergence is a notion of convergence introduced by De Giorgi well suited for the study of variational problems. The book [13] is a nice introduction to \(\Gamma\)-convergence in the context of the calculus of variations. For the convenience of the reader we recall here the basic definitions and results.

Definition 44. Let \(\mathcal{T}\) be a topological space and let \(F, F_n : \mathcal{T} \rightarrow (- \infty, \infty]\). We say that the sequence of functionals \((F_n)_n\) \(\Gamma\)-converges to \(F\) iff

  1. For every sequence \(x_n \rightarrow x\) in \(\mathcal{T}\)

    \(\displaystyle F (x) \leqslant \liminf_{n \rightarrow \infty} F_n (x_n) ;\)

  2. For every point \(x\) there exists a sequence \(x_n \rightarrow x\) (called a recovery sequence) such that

    \(\displaystyle F (x) \geqslant \limsup_{n \rightarrow \infty} F_n (x_n) .\)

Definition 45. A sequence of functionals \(F_n : \mathcal{T} \rightarrow (- \infty, \infty]\) is called equicoercive if there exists a compact set \(\mathcal{K} \subseteq \mathcal{T}\) such that for all \(n \in \mathbb{N}\)

\(\displaystyle \inf_{x \in \mathcal{K}} F_n (x) = \inf_{x \in \mathcal{T}} F_n (x) .\)

A fundamental consequence of \(\Gamma\)-convergence is the convergence of minima.

Theorem 46. If \((F_n)_n\) \(\Gamma\)-converges to \(F\) and \((F_n)_n\) is equicoercive, then \(F\) admits a minimum and

\(\displaystyle \min_{\mathcal{T}} F = \lim_{n \rightarrow \infty} \inf_{\mathcal{T}} F_n .\)

For a proof see [18].

Lemma 47. \(\check{F}^{f, \xi, \varepsilon}\) \(\Gamma\)-converges to \(\check{F}^{f, \xi}\) on \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\) as \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\).

Proof. We first show the liminf inequality. We have for any sequence \((\mu^{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}\) such that \(\mu^{\varepsilon} \rightarrow \mu\) in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\)

\(\displaystyle \liminf_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} [\mathcal{E} (Z)] \geqslant \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{E} (Z)], \qquad \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} [f (W + Z)] =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [f (W + Z)] .\)

Now let \(g^N : \mathbb{R} \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\) be defined by \(g^N (x) = x\) if \(x < N\), \(g^N (x) = N\) if \(x > N\). Then \(g^N \leqslant N\), \(g^N\) is Lipschitz with constant \(1\) and \(g^N (x) \rightarrow x\). Set \(Z_t^N = g^N (Z_t)\) and \(\mu_{N, \varepsilon} =\operatorname{Law}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} (W, Z^N)\). Then by monotone convergence

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \lim_{N \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^N) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta}\\ & = & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \end{eqnarray*}

It remains to prove that

\begin{eqnarray*} \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon}) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} & = & \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^N) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta}, \end{eqnarray*}

then the statement will follow from

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} & = & \lim_{N \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^N) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta}\\ & = & \lim_{N \rightarrow \infty} \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon}) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon}\\ & \leqslant & \liminf_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^{\varepsilon}) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} . \end{eqnarray*}

We have that \(\| \exp (\beta Z^N) \|_{L^{\infty}} \leqslant \exp (\beta N)\) and by chain rule

\(\displaystyle \| \rho \nabla \exp (\beta Z^N) \|_{L^2} = \| \rho \beta \nabla Z^N \exp (\beta Z^N) \|_{L^2} \lesssim \exp (\beta N) \| \rho \nabla Z^N \|_{L^2} .\)

By interpolation

\(\displaystyle \| \exp (\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon}) \|_{B_{p', p'}^{\delta} (\rho)} \lesssim \| \exp (\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon}) \|^{1 - 2 / p'}_{L^{\infty}} \| \exp (\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon}) \|^{2 / p'}_{H^1 (\rho)},\)

provided \(2 / p' > \delta\). Now let \(p = p' / (p' - 1)\). Note that we can choose \(p\) and \(\delta\) such that \(\frac{8 \pi}{\beta^2} > p\) and \(\frac{\beta^2}{4 \pi} (p - 1) < \delta\) and \(2 / p' > \delta\) by taking

\(\displaystyle \frac{2}{p} (p - 1) > \delta > \frac{\beta^2}{4 \pi} (p - 1) .\)

We assume, as we may, that \(p \leqslant 2\) (so that \(p' \geqslant 2\)) and that these values of \(p\) and \(s = \delta\) are the ones used in the definition of the space \(\mathfrak{M}\). To prove the convergence it suffices to argue along subsequences. By the Skorokhod representation theorem [37] there exist a probability space \((\tilde{\Omega}, \tilde{\mathcal{F}}, \tilde{\mathbb{P}})\) and random variables \((W^{\varepsilon}, \tilde{M}^{\varepsilon}, Z^{\varepsilon})\) with law \(\mu^{\varepsilon}\) converging almost surely in \(\mathfrak{S} \times \widehat{\mathfrak{H}}_w\) to \((W, M^{\beta}, Z)\) with law \(\mu\); in particular \(\tilde{M}^{\varepsilon} \rightarrow M^{\beta}\) in \(B_{p, p}^{- \delta} (\rho^{1 / 2})\) and \(Z^{\varepsilon} \rightarrow Z\) weakly in \(H^1_{\operatorname{loc}}\) almost surely. On this space we denote by \(M^{\beta, \varepsilon}\) the regularised chaos built from \(W^{\varepsilon}\) and we set \(Z^{N, \varepsilon} := g^N (\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z^{\varepsilon})\), \(Z^N := g^N (Z)\): these are the quantities entering \(\check{F}^{f, \xi, \varepsilon}\) and \(\check{F}^{f, \xi}\). Since \((W^{\varepsilon}, \tilde{M}^{\varepsilon})\) has the law of \((W, M^{\beta})\), the random variable \(\| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - \tilde{M}^{\varepsilon} \|_{B_{p, p}^{- \delta} (\rho^{1 / 2})}\) has the law of \(\| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - M^{\beta} \|_{B_{p, p}^{- \delta} (\rho^{1 / 2})}\) on the original space, which tends to \(0\) in probability by Proposition 41. Hence \(M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \rightarrow M^{\beta}\) in \(B_{p, p}^{- \delta} (\rho^{1 / 2})\) in probability. Moreover \(\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z^{\varepsilon} \rightarrow Z\) weakly in \(H^1_{\operatorname{loc}}\) (since \(\psi_{\varepsilon} \ast Z^{\varepsilon} - Z^{\varepsilon} \rightarrow 0\) in \(L^2_{\operatorname{loc}}\)), so if \(\operatorname{supp} \xi \subseteq B (0, R)\) the functions \(\xi \exp (\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon})\) are bounded in \(H^1\) by the chain rule and, by Rellich's theorem and the Lipschitz property of \(x \mapsto \exp (\beta g^N (x))\), converge almost surely to \(\xi \exp (\beta Z^N)\) in \(L^2\). By interpolation the convergence holds in \(H^r\) for all \(r < 1\) and, since \(p' \geqslant 2\), the Sobolev embedding \(H^r \subseteq B_{p', p'}^{r - 1 + 2 / p'}\) for functions supported in \(B (0, R)\), with \(r \in (1 + \delta - 2 / p', 1)\), gives the convergence in \(B_{p', p'}^{\delta}\). By duality, since \(\rho\) is bounded above and below on \(B (0, R)\),

\(\displaystyle \left| \int \xi e^{\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon}} \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - \int \xi e^{\beta Z^N} \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \right| \lesssim_R \| \xi (e^{\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon}} - e^{\beta Z^N}) \|_{B_{p', p'}^{\delta}} \| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \|_{B_{p, p}^{- \delta} (\rho^{1 / 2})} + \| \xi e^{\beta Z^N} \|_{B_{p', p'}^{\delta}} \| M^{\beta, \varepsilon} - M^{\beta} \|_{B_{p, p}^{- \delta} (\rho^{1 / 2})},\)

which tends to \(0\) in probability. Since

\(\displaystyle \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon}) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \lesssim e^{\beta N} \int \xi \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon},\)

and the r.h.s. is bounded in \(L^p (\tilde{\mathbb{P}})\) uniformly in \(\varepsilon\) by Proposition 40 a) (its law being that of the corresponding quantity on the original space), the family is uniformly integrable and by Vitali's theorem we have

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^{N, \varepsilon}) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^N) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta},\)

and we can conclude. To show the limsup inequality, for given \(\mu\) we need to construct \(\mu^{\varepsilon}\) such that

\(\displaystyle \limsup_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} F^{V^{\xi, f, \varepsilon}} (\mu^{\varepsilon}) \leqslant F^{V^{\xi, f}} (\mu) .\)

To do this we proceed similarly as in the first step. Define \(Z^{N, \delta} = g^N (\psi_{\delta} \ast Z)\), \(\mu^{N, \delta} =\operatorname{Law} (W, M^{\beta}, Z^{N, \delta})\), where \(\psi_{\delta}\) is a sequence of standard mollifiers. Then

\(\displaystyle M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \rightarrow M^{\beta}\)

weakly so

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^{N, \delta}) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta, \varepsilon} \rightarrow \mathbb{E} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^{N, \delta}) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta}\)

since \(Z^{N, \delta}\) is bounded and continuous. Furthermore \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} [\mathcal{E} (Z^{N, \delta})] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{E} (Z^{N, \delta})]\). This implies that

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} F^{V^{\xi, f, \varepsilon}} (\mu^{N, \delta}) = F^{V^{\xi, f}} (\mu^{N, \delta})\)

Now by dominated/monotone convergence we have

\(\displaystyle \lim_{N \rightarrow \infty} \lim_{\delta \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z^{N, \delta}) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta},\)

and

\(\displaystyle \lim_{N \rightarrow \infty} \lim_{\delta \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{E} (Z^{N, \delta})] =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{E} (Z)] \qquad \lim_{N \rightarrow \infty} \lim_{\delta \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [f (W + Z^{N, \delta})] =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [f (W + Z)],\)

so taking a suitable diagonal sequence implies the statement. \(\Box\)

Corollary 48.

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \check{F}^{V^{\xi, \varepsilon}} (\mu) = \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \check{F}^{V^{\xi}} (\mu)\)

and the infimum on the r.h.s is attained.

Proof. The statement will follow from the fundamental theorem of \(\Gamma\)-convergence (see e.g. [18]), once we have established that \(\check{F}^{V^{\xi, \varepsilon}}\) is equicoercive on \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\), but this follows from

\(\displaystyle \check{F}^{V^{\xi, f, \varepsilon}} (\mu) \geqslant \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\mathcal{E} (Z)] - C.\)
\(\Box\)

We can now relate this back to our general framework:

Lemma 49. The functional \(\check{F}^{f, \xi}\) has a minimiser \(\mu^{f, \xi} \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\) and

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}^{\xi, \exp} (f) := - \log \int \exp (- f (\phi)) \mathrm{d} \nu^{\xi, \exp} = \min_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}} \check{F}^{f, \xi} (\mu) - \min_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}} \check{F}^{0, \xi} (\mu) = \check{F}^{f, \xi} (\mu^{f, \xi}) - \check{F}^{0, \xi} (\mu^{0, \xi}) .\)

Furthermore for any minimiser \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\) the following wFBSDE

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \lambda \beta \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) K \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, K) \right] = -\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \int \nabla f (W + Z) K \right]\)

holds for any admissible control \(K\).

Proof. This follows mutatis mutandis as in Lemma 31.\(\Box\)

Lemma 50. The functional \(\check{F}^{0, \xi}\) has a unique minimiser \(\mu^{0, \xi}\) in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\). Furthermore

\(\displaystyle Z \leqslant 0,\)

\(\mu^{0, \xi}\) almost surely, and

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\xi} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{0, \xi}} [\| Z \|^2_{\widehat{\mathfrak{H}}}] < \infty .\)

Proof. Observe that \(F^{0, \xi} (Z)\) is convex in \(Z\). Now let \(\mu, \nu\) be two minimisers for \(\check{F}^{0, \xi}\). Recall from Lemma 19 that there exists \(\upsilon \in \overline{\mathcal{Y}} (\mu)\) such that \(\operatorname{Law}_{\upsilon} (W, Z + K) = \nu\). Now

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \lambda \int \xi \exp (\beta (Z + K)) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} +\mathcal{E} (Z + K) \right] -\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \lambda \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} +\mathcal{E} (Z) \right]\\ & & \qquad \geqslant \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \lambda \beta \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) K \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, K) +\mathcal{E} (K) \right] . \end{eqnarray*}

By Lemma 49 we have that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \lambda \beta \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) K \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, K) \right] = 0,\)

so

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [\mathcal{E} (K)] \leqslant \check{F}^{0, \xi} (\nu) - \check{F}^{0, \xi} (\mu) = 0,\)

which implies \(K = 0\) and therefore \(\nu = \mu\). For the second statement observe that the mapping \(A : Z \rightarrow - | Z |\) leaves the energy \(\mathcal{E} (Z)\) invariant by chain rule, while

\(\displaystyle \int \xi \exp (\beta A (Z)) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \leqslant \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta},\)

so if \(\mu^{0, \xi}\) is a minimiser, so is \(\operatorname{Law}_{\mu^{0, \xi}} (W, A (Z))\). By uniqueness we can conclude. For the third claim we test the wFBSDE with \(K = \rho Z\) and obtain

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \lambda \beta \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) \rho Z \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} + 2\mathcal{E} (Z, \rho Z) \right] = -\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \int \nabla f (W + Z) \rho Z \right] .\)

We can again estimate as in the proof of Lemma 34

\(\displaystyle 2\mathcal{E} (Z, \rho Z) \geqslant \frac{1}{2} \| Z \|^2_{\widehat{\mathfrak{H}}},\)

and

\(\displaystyle \int \nabla f (W + Z) \rho Z \lesssim \| Z \|_{\widehat{\mathfrak{H}}} .\)

Furthermore observe that

\(\displaystyle \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \exp (\beta Z) \rho Z \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \geqslant - C \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \rho \mathrm{d} M^{\beta},\)

since \(\exp (\beta Z) Z\) is bounded below. Summing up, we have the estimate

\(\displaystyle \frac{1}{2} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{0, \xi}} [\| Z \|^2_{\widehat{\mathfrak{H}}}] \lesssim \mathbb{E} [\| Z \|^2_{\widehat{\mathfrak{H}}}]^{1 / 2} +\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \xi \rho \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \right] \lesssim \mathbb{E} [\| Z \|^2_{\widehat{\mathfrak{H}}}]^{1 / 2} + 1\)

where in the last bound we used Proposition 41.\(\Box\)

Lemma 51. Let \(\mu^{\xi} \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}\). For \(\upsilon \in \bar{\mathcal{Y}} (\mu^{\xi})\) we have

\(\displaystyle \check{H}^{\xi, \exp} (\upsilon) := \check{H}^{V^{\xi}} (\upsilon) =\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} [2\mathcal{E} (K, Z) +\mathcal{E} (K)]\)

\(\displaystyle + \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int \xi \exp (\beta (Z + K)) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} - \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \right] .\)

Then \(\check{H}^{\xi, \exp} (\upsilon) \geqslant 0\) for all \(\upsilon \in \bar{\mathcal{Y}} (\mu^{\xi})\) iff \(\mu^{\xi}\) is a minimiser of \(\check{F}^{0, \xi}\). Moreover in this case, thanks to the wFBSDE for \(Z\) we have

\(\displaystyle \check{H}^{\xi, \exp} (\upsilon) =\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \mathcal{E} (K) + \lambda \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) (\exp (\beta K) - 1 - \beta K) \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \right],\)

and

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}^{\xi, \exp} (f) = \inf_{\upsilon \in \bar{\mathcal{Y}} (\mu^{\xi})} \check{G}^{f, \xi, \exp} (\upsilon), \quad \check{G}^{f, \xi, \exp} (\upsilon) := \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} [f (W + Z + K)] + \check{H}^{\xi, \exp} (\upsilon) .\)

Any minimiser \(\upsilon \in \bar{\mathcal{Y}} (\mu^{\xi})\) of \(\check{G}^{f, \xi, \exp}\) satisfies the perturbed wFBSDE

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ 2\mathcal{E} (K, H) + \lambda \beta \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) (\exp (\beta K) - 1) H \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \right]\\ = \hspace{1em} -\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} [\nabla f (W + Z + K) \cdot H] . \end{array} \) (34)

The proof of Lemma 51 is completely analogous to Lemma 33 and Lemma 31, thus we omit the details. Thanks to the convexity of the interaction we have a good control of the locality properties of the solutions to the wFBSDE (34).

Lemma 52. Assume that \(N > 0\) and

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\varphi \in L^2 (\mathbb{R}^2)} \| \nabla f (\varphi) \|_{H^{- 1} (\rho^{- N})} < \infty .\)

Then for any minimiser \(\upsilon^f\) of \(\check{G}^{f, \xi, \exp}\) we have

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon^f} [\| K \|_{H^1 (\rho^{- N})}^2] < \infty .\)

Proof. Testing equation (34) with \(H = \rho^{- 2 N} K\) we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \rho^{- 2 N} \dot{K}_s (1 - \Delta) \dot{K}_s \mathrm{d} s \right]\\ & + \lambda \beta \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) (\exp (\beta K) - 1) \rho^{- 2 N} K \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \right]\\ = & -\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int \rho^{- 2 N} \nabla f (W + Z + K) K \right] \leqslant C\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} [\| K \|_{H^1 (\rho^{- N})}^2]^{1 / 2} \end{array}\)

Now

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \rho^{- 2 N} \dot{K}_s (1 - \Delta) \dot{K}_s \mathrm{d} s \right]\\ & = & \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \rho^{- 2 N} ((1 - \Delta)^{1 / 2} \dot{K}_s)^2 \mathrm{d} s \right]\\ & & +\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} [(1 - \Delta)^{1 / 2}, \rho^{- 2 N}] \dot{K}_s (1 - \Delta)^{1 / 2} \dot{K}_s \mathrm{d} s \right] \end{eqnarray*}

and we can compute, as in the proof of Lemma 34,

\(\displaystyle [(1 - \Delta)^{1 / 2}, \rho^{- 2 N}] \dot{K}_s \leqslant \delta \rho^{- 2 N} \dot{K}_s .\)

Furthermore observe that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int \xi \exp (\beta Z) (\exp (\beta K) - 1) \rho^{- 2 N} K \mathrm{d} M^{\beta} \right] \geqslant 0.\)

Putting everything together, and using the commutator bound to absorb the second term in the decomposition above into the first one, we have

\begin{eqnarray*} (1 - \delta) \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \| K \|^2_{H^1 (\rho^{- N})} & \lesssim & (1 - \delta) \mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} \left[ \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \rho^{- 2 N} ((1 - \Delta)^{1 / 2} \dot{K}_s)^2 \mathrm{d} s \right]\\ & \leqslant & C\mathbb{E}_{\upsilon} [\| K \|^2_{H^1 (\rho^{- N})}]^{1 / 2} \end{eqnarray*}

which implies the statement.\(\Box\)

Definition 53. For \(N > 0\) large (to be chosen suitably below) denote by \(\mathcal{Y}^M (\mu)\) the set of measures \(\upsilon \in \mathcal{P} (\mathfrak{S} \times \widehat{\mathfrak{H}}_w \times \mathfrak{H}_w (\rho^{- N}))\) such that

  1. \(\operatorname{Law}_{\upsilon} (W, M^{\beta}, Z) = \mu\)

  2. \(\exists (Z^n)_n, (K^n)_n \subseteq \mathfrak{\hat{H}}^a\) for which

    \(\displaystyle \operatorname{Law} (W, M^{\beta, n}, Z^n, K^n) \rightarrow \upsilon \text{ and } \sup_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{E} [\| Z^n \|^2_{\widehat{\mathfrak{H}}} + \| K^n \|^2_{\mathfrak{H} (\rho^{- N})}] \leqslant 2 M.\)

Let \(\mathcal{Y}^M := \bigcup_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}^{\exp}} \mathcal{Y}^M (\mu)\).
