\(\displaystyle | \Upsilon^{(5)}_T | \leqslant C_{\varepsilon} \left[ \frac{| \gamma_T |}{\langle T \rangle^{1 / 4}} + \int^T_0 \frac{| \gamma_t | \mathrm{d} t}{\langle t \rangle^{5 / 4}} \right]^2 + \varepsilon \| Z_T \|^2_{H^{1 / 2 - \delta}} + \varepsilon \lambda \| Z_T \|^4_{L^4} .\)

Now note that the definition of \(K_t\) gives

\(\displaystyle K_t = - \lambda \int_0^t \mathbb{1}_{s \leqslant T} J_s^2 (\mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ Z_s^{\flat}) \mathrm{d} s + I_t (l)\)

therefore for any small \(\kappa > 0\),

\(\displaystyle \left\| \int_0^t \mathbb{1}_{s \leqslant T} J_s^2 (\mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ Z_s^{\flat}) \mathrm{d} s \right\|_{H^{1 - \kappa}}^2 \leqslant \int_0^{t \wedge T} \| J_s (\mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ Z_s^{\flat}) \|_{H^{- \kappa}}^2 \mathrm{d} s\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \int_0^{t \wedge T} \frac{1}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \kappa}} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ Z_s^{\flat} \|_{H^{- 1 - \kappa / 2}}^2 \mathrm{d} s\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \int_0^{t \wedge T} \frac{1}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \kappa}} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{B^{- 1 - \kappa / 4}_{4, \infty}}^2 \| Z_s^{\flat} \|_{L^4}^2 \mathrm{d} s\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \int_0^{t \wedge T} \frac{1}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \kappa}} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{B^{- 1 - \kappa / 4}_{4, \infty}}^2 \mathrm{d} s \| Z_T \|_{L^4}^2\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \lambda \left( \int_0^{t \wedge T} \frac{1}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \kappa}} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{B^{- 1 - \kappa / 4}_{4, \infty}}^2 \mathrm{d} s \right)^2 + \frac{1}{\lambda} \| Z_T \|_{L^4}^4\)

where we used that \(| \sigma_s (x) | \lesssim \langle s \rangle^{- 1 / 2} \mathbb{1}_{\langle x \rangle \sim s}\), so that \(\| J_s F \|_{H^{- \kappa}} \lesssim \langle s \rangle^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa / 2} \| F \|_{H^{- 1 - \kappa / 2}}\), and the inequality (valid for any \(\alpha \in \mathbb{R}\) and \(\varepsilon > 0\); here \(\alpha = - 1 - \kappa / 2\) and \(\varepsilon = \kappa / 4\))

\(\displaystyle \| f \prec g \|_{H^{\alpha}}^2 = \| f \prec g \|_{B_{2, 2}^{\alpha}}^2 = \sum_i 2^{2 i \alpha} \| \Delta_i (f \prec g) \|_{L^2}^2 \lesssim \sum_i 2^{2 i \alpha} \sum_{j \sim i} \| \Delta_{\leqslant j - 1} f \Delta_j g \|_{L^2}^2\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_i 2^{2 i \alpha} \sum_{j \sim i} \| \Delta_j g \|_{L^4}^2 \| \Delta_{\leqslant j - 1} f \|_{L^4}^2 \lesssim \| f \|_{L^4}^2 \sum_i 2^{2 i \alpha} \sum_{j \sim i} \| \Delta_j g \|_{L^4}^2\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \| f \|_{L^4}^2 \| g \|_{B^{\alpha}_{4, 2}}^2 \lesssim \| f \|_{L^4}^2 \| g \|_{B^{\alpha + \varepsilon}_{4, \infty}}^2\)

so that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| K_t \|^2_{H^{1 - \kappa}} & \lesssim & \lambda^2 \left\| \int_0^t \mathbb{1}_{s \leqslant T} J_s^2 (\mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ Z_s^{\flat}) \mathrm{d} s \right\|_{H^{1 - \kappa}}^2 + \| I_t (l) \|_{H^{1 - \kappa}}^2\\ & \lesssim & \lambda^3 \left( \int^t_0 \frac{1}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \kappa}} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{B^{- 1 - \kappa / 4}_{4, \infty}}^2 \mathrm{d} s \right)^2 + \lambda \| Z_T \|^4_{L^4} + \int^t_0 \| l_s \|_{L^2}^2 \mathrm{d} s, \end{array} \) (59)

which implies that quadratic functions of the norm \(\| K_t \|_{H^{1 - \kappa}}\) with small coefficients can always be controlled, uniformly in \(t \in [0, \infty]\), by the coercive term

\(\displaystyle \lambda Z^4_T + \frac{1}{2} \int^{\infty}_0 \| l_s \|_{L^2}^2 \mathrm{d} s.\)

Remark 77. Note that

\(\displaystyle \sup_T \left[ \frac{| \gamma_T |}{\langle T \rangle^{1 / 4}} + \int^T_0 \frac{| \gamma_t | \mathrm{d} t}{\langle t \rangle^{5 / 4}} \right] < \infty,\)

provided \(\gamma_T\) does not grow too fast in \(T\) which is indeed guaranteed by the choice of renormalisation made in Lemma 103.

Observe that, from Lemma 70 and from the detailed analysis of the various terms (which we skip but can be found in [5]), we have that

\(\displaystyle | \Phi_T (\mathbb{W}, Z, K) | \leqslant Q_T + \varepsilon \left( \lambda \| Z_T \|^4_{L^4} + \frac{1}{2} \int^{\infty}_0 \| l_t^T (u) \|^2_{L^2} \mathrm{d} t \right),\)

which immediately gives

\(\displaystyle -\mathbb{E} [Q_T] \leqslant -\mathbb{E} [Q_T] + (1 - \varepsilon) \mathbb{E} \left( \lambda \| Z_T \|^4_{L^4} + \frac{1}{2} \int^{\infty}_0 \| l_t^T (u) \|^2_{L^2} \mathrm{d} t \right) \leqslant \mathcal{W}_T (f) . \) (60)

On the other hand for any suitable drift \(\check{u} \in \mathbb{H}_a\) we get the bound

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}_T (f) \leqslant \mathbb{E} [Q_T] + (1 + \varepsilon) \mathbb{E} \left( \lambda \| I_T (\check{u}) \|^4_{L^4} + \frac{1}{2} \int^{\infty}_0 \| l_t^T (\check{u}) \|^2_{L^2} \mathrm{d} t \right), \) (61)

where

\(\displaystyle l_t^T (\check{u}) = \check{u}_t + \lambda \mathbb{1}_{t \leqslant T} J_t (\mathbb{W}_t^3 +\mathbb{W}_t^2 \succ (I_t (\check{u}))^{\flat}) . \) (62)

Therefore it remains to produce an appropriate drift \(\check{u}\) for which the r.h.s. in eq. (61) is finite (and so uniformly in \(| \Lambda |\) and of order \(o (\lambda^3)\)).

One possible strategy is to try and choose \(\check{u}\) such that \(l^T (\check{u}) = 0\), however this fails since estimates on this choice of drift via Gronwall's inequality would rely on the Besov-Hölder norm of \(\mathbb{W}^2\) for which we do not have any uniform control in the volume. In order to overcome this problem we decompose \(\mathbb{W}^2\) and use weighted estimates similarly as done in [26] in the SPDE context.

Consider the decomposition

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{W}_s^2 = \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}_s^2 + \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2,\)

where the random field \(\mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}_s^2\) is constructed as follows. Let \(\varphi\) be a smooth function, positive and supported on \([- 2, 2]^3\) and such that \(\sum_{m \in \Lambda \cap \mathbb{Z}^d} \varphi^2 (\bullet - m) = 1\). Denote \(\varphi_m := \varphi (\bullet - m)\). Let \(\tilde{\chi}\) be a smooth function supported in \(B (0, 1)\), denote by \(\mathcal{X}_{\leqslant N}\) the Fourier multiplier operator \(\tilde{\chi} (\mathrm{D} / N)\) and similarly \(\mathcal{X}_{> N} := (1 - \tilde{\chi} (\mathrm{D} / N))\). Set \(L_m (s) := (1 + \| \varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathscr{C}^{- 1 - \delta}})^{\frac{1}{2 \delta}}\), let

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}_s^2 := \sum_{m \in \Lambda \cap \mathbb{Z}^d} \varphi_m \mathcal{X}_{> L_m (s)} (\varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2)\)

and

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 := \sum_{m \in \Lambda \cap \mathbb{Z}^d} \varphi_m \mathcal{X}_{\leqslant L_m (s)} (\varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2) .\)

(with slight abuse of notation we drop the dependence on time of the operators \(\mathcal{U}_{\leqslant}\), \(\mathcal{U}_{>}\)).

Observe that the laws of both \(\mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}_s^2\) and \(\mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2\) are translation invariant w.r.t. translations by \(m \in \Lambda \cap \mathbb{Z}^d\). By [53], Theorem 2.4.7 and Bernstein inequality

\begin{eqnarray*} \| \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - 3 \delta}} & \lesssim & \sup_m \| \mathcal{X}_{> L_m (s)} (\varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2) \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - 3 \delta}}\\ & \lesssim & \sup_m \frac{1}{1 + \| \varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathscr{C}^{- 1 - \delta}}} \| \varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta}} \lesssim 1. \end{eqnarray*}

Furthermore for a polynomial weight \(\rho\):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 + \delta} (\rho^2)} & \lesssim & \sup_m \| \varphi_m \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 + \delta} (\rho^2)}\\ & \lesssim & \sup_m (1 + \| \varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta}}) \| \varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathscr{C}^{- 1 - \delta} (\rho^2)}\\ & \lesssim & \sup_m \rho (m) (1 + \| \varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta}}) \| \varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathscr{C}^{- 1 - \delta} (\rho)}\\ & \lesssim & \sup_m (1 + \| \varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta} (\rho)}) \| \varphi_m \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta} (\rho)}\\ & \lesssim & 1 + \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta} (\rho)}^2, \end{array} \) (63)

where we used the possibility to compare weighted and unweighted norms once localised via \(\varphi_m\). We now let \(\check{u}\) be the solution to the linear integral equation

\(\displaystyle \check{u}_t = - \lambda \mathbb{1}_{t \leqslant T} [\mathbb{W}^{\langle 3 \rangle}_t + J_t \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}^2_t \succ \theta_t (I_t (\check{u}))], \qquad t \geqslant 0, \) (64)

which can be solved globally. For \(2 \delta < 1 / 2\), \(p \geqslant 1\) and \(t \in [0, T]\), we have

\(\displaystyle \| I_t (\check{u}) \|_{B_{p, p}^{1 / 2 - 2 \delta} (\rho)} \lesssim \lambda \int^t_0 [\| J^2_s \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|_{B_{p, p}^{1 / 2 - 2 \delta} (\rho)} + \| J^2_s \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ \theta_s (I_s (\check{u})) \|_{B_{p, p}^{1 / 2 - 2 \delta} (\rho)}] \mathrm{d} s\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \lambda \int^t_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 / 2 + \delta}} \| J_s \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|_{B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta} (\rho)} + \lambda \int^t_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \delta}} \| \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - 3 \delta}} \| I_s (\check{u}) \|_{B_{p, p}^{1 / 2 - 2 \delta} (\rho)} .\)

Gronwall's lemma, together with the bound \(\| \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - 3 \delta}} \lesssim 1\) (which makes the exponential factor below bounded by a constant), implies that, for \(t \in [0, T]\):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| I_t (\check{u}) \|_{B_{p, p}^{1 / 2 - 2 \delta} (\rho)} & \lesssim & \left( \lambda \int^T_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 / 2 + \delta}} \| J_s \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|_{B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta} (\rho)} \right) \exp \left( \lambda \int^T_0 \frac{\| \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - 3 \delta}} \mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \delta}} \right)\\ & \lesssim & \left( \lambda \int^T_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 / 2 + \delta}} \| J_s \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|_{B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta} (\rho)} \right)\\ & \lesssim & \lambda \| \mathbb{W}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}_+, B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta} (\rho))} . \end{array} \) (65)

Note that eq. (65) is also valid replacing the weighted norm \(B_{p, p}^{1 / 2 - 2 \delta} (\rho)\) with the standard (normalised) norm \(B_{p, p}^{1 / 2 - 2 \delta}\), from which, using Besov embedding we deduce:

\(\displaystyle \sup_T \mathbb{E} \| I_T (\check{u}) \|^4_{L^4} \lesssim \lambda^4 \mathbb{E} \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 / 2 + \delta}} \| J_s \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|_{B_{4, 4}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta}} \right)^4 \lesssim \lambda^4 .\)

Computing \(l^T (\check{u})\) from eq. (62) and (64), we obtain

\(\displaystyle l_t^T (\check{u}) = \lambda \mathbb{1}_{t \leqslant T} J_t \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_t^2 \succ \theta_t (I_t (\check{u})), \qquad t \geqslant 0.\)

It remains to prove that \(\mathbb{E} [\| l^T (\check{u}) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] \lesssim O (\lambda^3)\) uniformly in \(T > 0\). Note that, for \(s \in [0, T]\),

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| J_s \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ \theta_s (I_s (\check{u})) \|_{L^2 (\rho^3)} & \lesssim & \frac{1}{\langle s \rangle^{1 / 2 + \delta / 2}} \| \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ \theta_s (I_s (\check{u})) \|_{B_{2, 2}^{- 1 + \delta / 2} (\rho^3)}\\ & \lesssim & \frac{1}{\langle s \rangle^{1 / 2 + \delta / 2}} \| \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 + \delta / 2} (\rho^2)} \| I_s (\check{u}) \|_{B_{2, 2}^{1 / 2 - 3 \delta} (\rho)} . \end{array} \) (66)

We know that the distribution of \(\check{u}\) is invariant under translation by \(m \in \Lambda \cap \mathbb{Z}^d\). Recalling that \(\sum_{m \in \Lambda \cap \mathbb{Z}^d} \varphi^2 (\bullet - m) = 1\) and letting \(\rho\) be a polynomial weight with sufficient decay and such that \(\rho^5 \geqslant \varphi^2\), we have

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathbb{E} [\| l^T (\check{u}) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] & = & \lambda^2 \mathbb{E} [\| s \mapsto \mathbb{1}_{s \leqslant T} J_s \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ \theta_s (I_s (\check{u})) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]\\ & \leqslant & \lambda^2 \sum_{m \in \Lambda \cap \mathbb{Z}^d} \mathbb{E} [\| s \mapsto \varphi (\bullet - m) J_s \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ \theta_s (I_s (\check{u})) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]\\ \text{(by trans. inv.)} & \lesssim & \lambda^2 | \Lambda | \mathbb{E} [\| s \mapsto \mathbb{1}_{s \leqslant T} \varphi J_s \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ \theta_s (I_s (\check{u})) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]\\ \text{(using $\rho^5 \geqslant \varphi^2$)} & \lesssim & \lambda^2 \int^T_0 \mathrm{d} s\mathbb{E} [\| J_s \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \succ \theta_s (I_s (\check{u})) \|^2_{L^2 (\rho^5)}]\\ \text{(by eq.~\text{{\upshape{(\hyperref[eq:ub3]{66})}}})} & \lesssim & \lambda^2 \int^T_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \delta}} \mathbb{E} [\| \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 + \delta / 2} (\rho^2)}^2 \| I_s (\check{u}) \|_{B_{2, 2}^{1 / 2 - 3 \delta} (\rho)}^2]\\ & \lesssim & \lambda^2 \int^T_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \delta}} \mathbb{E} [\lambda^2 \| \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|^4_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 + \delta / 2} (\rho^2)} + \lambda^{- 2} \| I_s (\check{u}) \|_{B_{2, 2}^{1 / 2 - 3 \delta} (\rho)}^4]\\ \text{(by eqs.~\text{{\upshape{(\hyperref[eq:ub-I]{65})}}}, \text{{\upshape{(\hyperref[boundsmooth]{63})}}})} & \lesssim & \lambda^4 \int^{\infty}_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \delta}} [1 +\mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|^8_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta / 2} (\rho)} +\mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|^4_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}_+, B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta} (\rho))}]\\ & \lesssim & \lambda^4 [1 + \sup_{s \geqslant 0} \mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|^8_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta / 2} (\rho)} +\mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|^4_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}_+, B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta} (\rho))}]\\ & \lesssim & O (\lambda^4) . \end{eqnarray*}

The last inequality is the consequence of bounds on the two expectations on the r.h.s. obtained as follows. For \(p\) sufficiently large (namely \(3 / p \leqslant \delta / 4\), so that by Besov embedding \(B_{p, p}^{- 1 - \delta / 4} (\rho^p) \subset \mathscr{C}^{- 1 - \delta / 2} (\rho)\)) we have

\(\displaystyle [\mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|^8_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta / 2} (\rho)}]^{p / 8} \leqslant \mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|^p_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \delta / 2} (\rho)} \leqslant \mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}_s^2 \|^p_{B_{p, p}^{- 1 - \delta / 4} (\rho^p)}\)
\(\displaystyle = \sum_{i \geqslant - 1} 2^{i (- 1 - \delta / 4) p} \int_{\Lambda} \mathrm{d} x | \rho (x) |^p \mathbb{E} | \Delta_i \mathbb{W}_s^2 (x) |^p \lesssim \sum_{i \geqslant - 1} 2^{i (- 1 - \delta / 4) p} \mathbb{E} | \Delta_i \mathbb{W}_s^2 (0) |^p \lesssim 1,\)

uniformly in \(s \geqslant 0\). Similarly, we have

\(\displaystyle [\mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|^4_{B_{p, p}^{- \delta / 2} (\rho)}]^{p / 4} \leqslant \mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|_{B_{p, p}^{- \delta / 2} (\rho)}^p \lesssim \mathbb{E} | \mathbb{W}_s^3 (0) |^p .\)

By Lemma 104

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} | \mathbb{W}_s^3 (0) |^p \lesssim (\mathbb{E} | \mathbb{W}_s^3 (0) |^2)^{p / 2} \lesssim \langle s \rangle^{3 p / 2},\)

and using the standard multiplier bounds for \(J_s\) we conclude

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathbb{E} \| \mathbb{W}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|^4_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}_+, B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta} (\rho))} & \lesssim & \mathbb{E} \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \| J_s \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|^2_{B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta} (\rho)} \mathrm{d} s \right)^2\\ & \lesssim & \mathbb{E} \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \left\| \frac{\sigma_s (\mathrm{D})}{\langle \mathrm{D} \rangle} \mathbb{W}_s^3 \right\|^2_{B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \delta} (\rho)} \mathrm{d} s \right)^2\\ & \lesssim & \mathbb{E} \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \langle s \rangle^{- 1 - \delta} (\langle s \rangle^{- 3 / 2} \| \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|_{B_{p, p}^{- \delta / 2} (\rho)})^2 \mathrm{d} s \right)^2\\ & \lesssim & \int^{\infty}_0 \langle s \rangle^{- 1 - \delta} \mathbb{E} (\langle s \rangle^{- 3 / 2} \| \mathbb{W}_s^3 \|_{B_{p, p}^{- \delta / 2} (\rho)})^4 \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & 1. \end{eqnarray*}

Remark 78. The decomposition of the noise is similar to the one given in [26] but differs in the fact that we choose the frequency cutoff dependent on the size of the noise instead of the point, to preserve translation invariance. The price to pay is that the decomposition is nonlinear in the noise, however this does not present any inconvenience in our context.

7.3Removal of the UV cutoff

From Lemma 70 we learned that

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}_T (f) = \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} F_T (u),\)

with

\(\displaystyle F_T (u) =\mathbb{E} \left[ \Phi_T (\mathbb{W}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_T (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^T (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2 \right],\)

where \(l^T (u), Z (u), K (u)\) are functions of \(u\). The goal of this section is to establish the \(T \rightarrow \infty\) limit of this variational problem:

Theorem 79. We have

\(\displaystyle \lim_{T \rightarrow \infty} \mathcal{W}_T (f) =\mathcal{W} (f) := \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} F_{\infty} (u),\)

where

\(\displaystyle F_{\infty} (u) =\mathbb{E} \left[ \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{W}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2 \right],\)

with \(\Phi_{\infty}\) and \(l^{\infty}\) introduced in Lemma 81 below.

Proof. The statement will be a direct consequence of Theorem 93 below.\(\Box\)

The functions \(\Phi_{\infty}, Z_{\infty}, l^{\infty}\) are suitable limits of \(\Phi_T, Z_T, l^T\). Therefore our first task is to analyse their continuity and to identify suitable topologies. As \(T \rightarrow \infty\) the variational functional tends to keep \(l\) in \(\mathcal{H}\) but not \(u\), so we need at first to understand in which space \(u\) will live in the limit.

We denote

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{H}^{\alpha, p} := L^2 ([0, \infty) ; W^{\alpha, p}), \qquad \alpha \in \mathbb{R}, 1 < p < \infty,\)

and by \(\mathcal{H}_w^{\alpha, p}\) the reflexive Banach space \(\mathcal{H}^{\alpha, p}\) endowed with the weak topology. With these definitions we have \(\mathcal{H}^{\alpha} =\mathcal{H}^{\alpha, 2}\) and \(\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}^{0, 2}\). Moreover for small enough \(\kappa > 0\) (fixed once and for all) we let

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{L} := \mathcal{H}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa, 3} .\)

This is the space in which we will put \(u\), and next we show that it is sufficient to get control over \(Z\).

Lemma 80. For \(\kappa\) small enough, \(u \mapsto Z (u)\) is a compact map \(\mathcal{L} \rightarrow C ([0, \infty], L^4)\).

Proof. By definition of \(Z\) we have for any \(0 < \varepsilon < 1 / 8 - \kappa / 2\),

\begin{eqnarray*} \| Z_{t_2} (u) - Z_{t_1} (u) \|_{W^{\varepsilon, 4}} & = & \left\| \int^{t_2}_{t_1} J_s u_s \mathrm{d} s \right\|_{W^{\varepsilon, 4}} \leqslant \int^{t_2}_{t_1} \left\| \frac{\sigma_s (\mathrm{D})}{\langle \mathrm{D} \rangle} u_s \right\|_{W^{\varepsilon, 4}} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \int^{t_2}_{t_1} \| \langle \mathrm{D} \rangle^{- 1 + \varepsilon} u_s \|_{W^{\varepsilon, 4}} \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 / 2 + \varepsilon}}\\ & \lesssim & \int^{t_2}_{t_1} \| \langle \mathrm{D} \rangle^{- 1 + \varepsilon} u_s \|_{W^{1 / 4 + \varepsilon, 3}} \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 / 2 + \varepsilon}}\\ & \lesssim & \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \| u_s \|^2_{W^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa, 3}} \mathrm{d} s \right)^{1 / 2} \left( \int^{t_2}_{t_1} \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + 2 \varepsilon}} \right)^{1 / 2} \\ & \lesssim & \left( \int^{t_2}_{t_1} \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + 2 \varepsilon}} \right)^{1 / 2} \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} . \end{eqnarray*}

where we have used the Sobolev embedding \(W^{1 / 4 + \varepsilon, 3} \hookrightarrow W^{\varepsilon, 4}\). Since

\(\displaystyle \lim_{t_1 \rightarrow t_2} \int^{t_2}_{t_1} \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + 2 \varepsilon}} = 0, \qquad \int^{\infty}_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} s}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + 2 \varepsilon}} < \infty,\)

for any \(t_2 \in [0, \infty]\), we can conclude by the Rellich–Kondrachov embedding theorem and the Ascoli–Arzelà theorem, that bounded sets in \(\mathcal{L}\) are mapped to compact sets in \(C ([0, \infty], L^4)\), proving the claim.\(\Box\)

In the sequel, by an abuse of notation, we will denote both a generic element of \(\mathfrak{S}\) and the canonical random variable on \(\mathfrak{S}\) by

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{X}= (\mathbb{X}^1, \mathbb{X}^2, \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle}, \mathbb{X}^{[3] \circ 1}, \mathbb{X}^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}, \mathbb{X}^{2 \diamond [3]}) .\)

The following lemma establishes point-wise convergence for the functional \(\Phi_T\) defined in Lemma 70.

Lemma 81. Define \(l^{\infty} (u) = l^{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, u) \in \mathbb{H}_a\) by

\(\displaystyle l_t^{\infty} (u) := u_t + \lambda \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle}_t + \lambda J_t (\mathbb{X}_t^2 \succ Z_t^{\flat} (u)), \qquad t \geqslant 0. \) (67)

For any sequence \((\mathbb{X}^T, u^T)_T\) such that \(u^T \rightarrow u\) in \(\mathcal{L}_w\), \(l^T = l^T (\mathbb{X}^T, u^T) \rightarrow l = l^{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, u)\) in \(\mathcal{H}_w\) and

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{cll} \mathbb{X}^T & = & (\mathbb{X}^{T, 1}, \mathbb{X}^{T, 2}, \mathbb{X}^{T, \langle 3 \rangle}, \mathbb{X}^{T, [3] \circ 1}, \mathbb{X}^{T, \langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}, \mathbb{X}^{T, 2 \diamond [3]})\\ \downarrow & & \\ \mathbb{X} & = & (\mathbb{X}^1, \mathbb{X}^2, \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle}, \mathbb{X}^{[3] \circ 1}, \mathbb{X}^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}, \mathbb{X}^{2 \diamond [3]}) \end{array}\)

in \(\mathfrak{S}\) we have

\(\displaystyle \lim_{T \rightarrow \infty} \Phi_T (\mathbb{X}^T, Z (u^T), K (u^T)) = \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) .\)

Here \(Z_t (u) = I_t (u)\), we let \(K_t (u) := Z_t (u) + \lambda \mathbb{X}^{[3]}_t\) and \(\Phi_{\infty}\) is defined by

\(\displaystyle \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) := f (\mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} + Z_{\infty} (u)) + \sum_{i = 1}^6 \Upsilon_{\infty}^{(i)} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)),\)

with \(\Upsilon_{\infty}^{(i)} (\mathbb{X}, Z, K) = \Upsilon_{\infty}^{(i)}\) given by

\begin{eqnarray*} \Upsilon^{(1)}_{\infty} & := & - \frac{\lambda}{2} \mathfrak{K}_2 (\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^2, K_{\infty}, K_{\infty}) + \frac{\lambda}{2} (\mathbb{X}^2_{\infty} \prec K_{\infty}) K_{\infty} - \lambda^2 (\mathbb{X}^2_{\infty} \prec \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]}) K_{\infty},\\ \Upsilon^{(2)}_{\infty} & = & 0,\\ \Upsilon^{(3)}_{\infty} & := & \lambda \int_0^{\infty} (\mathbb{X}^2_t \succ \dot{Z}^{\flat}_t) K_t \mathrm{d} t,\\ \Upsilon^{(4)}_{\infty} & := & 4 \lambda \mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} K^3_{\infty} - 12 \lambda^2 (\mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]}) K^2_{\infty} + 12 \lambda^3 \mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]})^2 K_{\infty},\\ \Upsilon^{(5)}_{\infty} & := & - 2 \lambda^2 \int^{\infty}_0 \gamma_t Z^{\flat}_t \dot{Z}_t^{\flat} \mathrm{d} t,\\ \Upsilon^{(6)}_{\infty} & := & - \lambda^2 \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{2 \diamond [3]} K_{\infty} - \frac{\lambda^2}{2} \int_0^{\infty} \mathbb{X}^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_t (Z^{\flat}_t)^2 \mathrm{d} t - \frac{\lambda^2}{2} \int^{\infty}_0 \mathfrak{K}_{3, t} (\mathbb{X}_t^2, \mathbb{X}_t^2, Z^{\flat}_t, Z^{\flat}_t) \mathrm{d} t, \end{eqnarray*}

where \(\mathfrak{K}_1, \mathfrak{K}_2, \mathfrak{K}_{3, t}\) are the multilinear forms defined in Proposition 73, Proposition 74 and Proposition 75 respectively and where, with abuse of notation, we let

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]} & := & \mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} \succ \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]} +\mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} \prec \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]} +\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3] \circ 1},\\ \mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]})^2 & := & \mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]} \circ \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]}) + 2\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3] \circ 1} \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]} + 2 \mathfrak{K}_1 (\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]}, \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]}, \mathbb{X}^1_{\infty})\\ & & + 2\mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} \succ (\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]} \succ \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]}) + 2\mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} \prec (\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]} \succ \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]}) . \end{array} \) (68)

Proof. Lemma 80 implies that for any \(u^T \rightarrow u\) in \(\mathcal{L}_w\) we have \(Z (u^T) \rightarrow Z (u)\) in \(C ([0, \infty], L^4)\) and by the convergence of \(l^T \rightarrow l\) in \(\mathcal{H}_w\) we have also \(K (u^T) \rightarrow K (u)\) in \(C ([0, \infty], H^{1 - \kappa})\). The products \(\mathbb{X}^{T, 1}_T \mathbb{X}_T^{T, [3]}\) and \(\mathbb{X}^{T, 1}_T (\mathbb{X}_T^{T, [3]})^2\) can be decomposed using paraproducts and, after identifying the resonant products with the corresponding stochastic objects in \(\mathbb{X}^T\), we obtain the finite \(T\) analogs of the expressions in eq. (68). After this preprocessing, it is easy to see by continuity that we have \(\mathbb{X}^{T, 1}_T \mathbb{X}_T^{T, [3]} \rightarrow \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^1 \mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]}\) and \(\mathbb{X}^{T, 1}_T (\mathbb{X}_T^{T, [3]})^2 \rightarrow \mathbb{X}^1_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}_{\infty}^{[3]})^2\) in \(\mathscr{C}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}\). For \(\Upsilon^{(1)}\) and \(\Upsilon^{(4)}\) and the first term of \(\Upsilon^{(6)}\) the statement follows from the fact that they are bounded multilinear forms on \(\mathfrak{S} \times C ([0, \infty], H^{1 / 2 - \kappa}) \times C ([0, \infty], H^{1 - \kappa})\) . For the remaining terms \(\Upsilon^{(2)}, \Upsilon^{(3)}, \Upsilon^{(5)}, \Upsilon^{(6)}\) the convergence follows from the detailed analysis in [5].\(\Box\)

This settles the framework for the point-wise convergence of the cost function. In order to use \(\Gamma\)-convergence, we need to modify the variational setting to guarantee enough compactness as \(T \rightarrow \infty\).

As long as \(T\) is finite, the original potential \(V_T\) is bounded below so in particular we have

\(\displaystyle - C_T +\mathbb{E} \left[ \frac{1}{2} \| u \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2 \right] \leqslant F_T (u), \) (69)

which quantifies the coercivity of \(F_T\). Unfortunately, this estimate does not survive the limit. However the analytic estimates of the renormalised control problem allow to infer that there exists a small \(\delta \in (0, 1)\), and a finite constant \(C > 0\) independent of \(T\), such that

\(\displaystyle - C + (1 - \delta) \mathbb{E} \left[ \lambda \| Z_T (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^T (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2 \right] \leqslant F_T (u), \) (70)

and

\(\displaystyle F_T (u) \leqslant C + (1 + \delta) \mathbb{E} \left[ \lambda \| Z_T (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^T (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2 \right] .\) (71)

Moreover the cost functional \(\lambda \| Z_T (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^T (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2\) controls the \(\mathcal{L}\) norm of \(u\) uniformly in \(T\), modulo constants depending only on \(\| \mathbb{W} \|_{\mathfrak{S}}\) and which are bounded in average uniformly in \(T\). More precisely we have (in a more general setting, useful below)

Lemma 82. Let \(\mu\) be a probability measure on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) with first marginal \(\operatorname{Law}_{\mathbb{P}} (\mathbb{W})\) and denote with \((\mathbb{X}, u)\) the canonical variable on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\). Then there exists a constant \(C\), depending only on \(\lambda\), such that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] \lesssim C + 2 \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| l^T (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] .\)

Proof. We use \(\| l^T (u) \|_{\mathcal{L}} \lesssim \| l^T (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}\) in the bound

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] & \lesssim & \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|_{\mathcal{L}}^2] + \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| s \mapsto J_s (\mathbb{X}_s^2 \succ \theta_s Z_T (u)) \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| l^T (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]\\ & \lesssim & \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] + \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \int^{\infty}_0 \frac{\| \mathbb{X}_s^2 \|^2_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \kappa}}}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \kappa}} \| Z_T (u) \|^2_{L^4} \mathrm{d} s \right]\\ & & +\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| l^T (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]\\ & \lesssim & \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] + \frac{\lambda}{2} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \int^{\infty}_0 \frac{\| \mathbb{X}_s^2 \|^4_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \kappa}}}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \kappa}} \mathrm{d} s \right] + 2 \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}]\\ & & +\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| l^T (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] . \end{array}\)

\(\Box\)

From this we conclude that we can relax the optimisation problem and ask that \(u \in \mathbb{L}_a\) where \(\mathbb{L}_a\) is the space of predictable processes in \(\mathcal{L}\):

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}_T (f) = \inf_{u \in \mathbb{L}_a} F_T (u) .\)

For future reference note that eq. (70) implies also that for any sequence \((u^T)_T\) such that \(F_T (u^T)\) remains bounded we must have that also

\(\displaystyle \sup_T \mathbb{E} [\| l^T (u^T) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] < \infty . \) (72)

To prove \(\Gamma\)-convergence we need to set up the problem in a space with a topology which, on the one hand is strong enough to enable to prove the \(\Gamma\)-liminf inequality, and on the other hand allows to obtain enough compactness from \(F_T\). Almost sure convergence on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) would allow for the former but is too strong for the latter. For this reason we need a setting based on convergence in law as made precise in the following definition.

Definition 83. Denote by \((\mathbb{X}, u)\) the canonical variables on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) and consider the space of probability measures

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{Y} := \{ \mu \in \mathcal{P} (\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}) | \text{$\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] < \infty$} \}\)

equipped with the following topology: \(\mu_n \rightarrow \mu\) iff

  1. \(\mu_n\) converges to \(\mu\) weakly on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\),

  2. \(\sup_n \mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] < \infty\).

Let

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{X} := \{ \mu \in \mathcal{Y} | \text{$\mu =\operatorname{Law}_{\mathbb{P}} (\mathbb{W}, u)$ for some $u \in \mathbb{L}_a$} \}\)

and denote by \(\overline{\mathcal{X}} \subseteq \mathcal{Y}\) the closure of \(\mathcal{X}\) in \(\mathcal{Y}\).

Remark 84. Condition (b) allows to exclude pathological points in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\) and makes possible Lemma 89 below.

With these new notations we have

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}_T (f) = \inf_{\mu \in \mathcal{X}} \breve{F}_T (\mu), \) (73)

where

\(\displaystyle \breve{F}_T (\mu) := \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \Phi_T (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_T (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^T (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right]\)

and where \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\) denotes the expectation on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) wrt. the probability measure \(\mu\). We also define the corresponding limiting functional as

\(\displaystyle \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) := \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right] . \) (74)

As far as the topology of \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\) is concerned, we have the following tightness and equicoercivity results.

Lemma 85. Let \(G \subseteq \overline{\mathcal{X}}\) such that \(\sup_{\mu \in G} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] < \infty\). Then \(G\) is tight on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\) and in particular compact in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\).

Proof. Observe that for all \(\mu \in G\), \(\operatorname{Law}_{\mu} (\mathbb{X}) =\operatorname{Law}_{\mathbb{P}} (\mathbb{W})\) and that \(\operatorname{Law}_{\mathbb{P}} (\mathbb{W})\) on \(\mathfrak{S}\) is tight since \(\mathfrak{S}\) is a separable metric space, so for any \(\varepsilon > 0\), we can find a compact set \(\mathcal{K}^1_{\varepsilon} \subset \mathfrak{S}\) such that \(\mu ((\mathfrak{S} \setminus \mathcal{K}^1_{\varepsilon}) \times \mathcal{L}) < \varepsilon / 2\). Now let \(\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}^2 := \mathcal{K}^1_{\varepsilon} \times B (0, C) \subset \mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\), for some large \(C\) to be chosen later. Then \(\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}^2\) is a compact subset of \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\) and

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{P}_{\mu} [(\mathbb{X}, u) \notin \mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}^2] \leqslant \frac{\varepsilon}{2} + \frac{1}{C} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] .\)

Choosing \(C > \sup_{\mu \in G} 2\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] / \varepsilon\) gives tightness of the family \(G\).\(\Box\)

Lemma 86. The family \((\breve{F}_T)_T\) is equicoercive on \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\).

Proof. Define for some \(K > 0\) large enough

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{K} := \{ \mu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}} : \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] \leqslant K \} .\)

Note that \(\mathcal{K}\) is compact from Lemma 85. From eq. (70) we have

\(\displaystyle \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] + \frac{1}{2} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| l^T (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] \leqslant C + 2 \breve{F}_T (\mu) .\)

Indeed, note that the analytic estimates of Section 7.2 are path-wise and hold also wrt. \((\mathbb{X}, u)\) under the measure \(\mu\) (the point is that here \(u\) is not necessarily adapted to \(\mathbb{X}\)), while for the probabilistic estimates on \(Q_T (\mathbb{W})\) we have \(\mathbb{E} [Q_T (\mathbb{W})] =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [Q_T (\mathbb{X})]\) since \(\operatorname{Law}_{\mu} (\mathbb{X}) =\operatorname{Law}_{\mathbb{P}} (\mathbb{W})\). From this we deduce that for some \(C, c > 0\)

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \breve{F}_T (\mu)\\ & \geqslant & \frac{\lambda}{2} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] + \frac{1}{4} \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| l^T (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] - C\\ & \geqslant & c\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] - C \end{eqnarray*}

where in the last line we have used Lemma 82. Therefore \(\inf_{\mu \in \mathcal{K}^c} \breve{F}_T (\mu) \geqslant c K - C\). On the other hand from eq. (71) it follows that \(\sup_T \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}_T (\mu) < \infty\). So for \(K\) large enough

\(\displaystyle \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}_T (\mu) = \inf_{\mu \in \mathcal{K}} \breve{F}_T (\mu) .\)

\(\Box\)

To be able to use this equicoercivity we will need to show that we can extend the infimum in (73) to \(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\). For this we will first need some properties of the space \(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\).

In particular we will need to show that measures with sufficiently high moments are dense in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\) in a way which behaves well with respect to \(\breve{F}_T\).

We skip the proofs of the next three lemmas.

Lemma 87. Let \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\). There exist \((\mu_n)_n\) in \(\mathcal{X}\) such that \(\mu_n \rightarrow \mu\) on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) (now with the norm topology) and \(\sup_n \mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] < \infty\).

Lemma 88. Let \(\mu_n \rightarrow \mu\) on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\), such that \(\sup_n \mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] < \infty\). Then

  1. for every Lipschitz function \(f\) on \(\mathcal{L}\), \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [f ( u) ] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [f ( u) ]\);

  2. for every Lipschitz function \(f\) on \(C ([0, \infty], L^4)\) we have \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu_n} [f (Z (u))] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [f (Z (u))]\).

The next lemma proves that we can approximate measures in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\) by measures with bounded support in the second marginal which are still in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\).

Lemma 89. Let \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\) such that \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] < \infty\). For any \(L > 0\) there exists \(\mu_L \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\) such that \(\| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} \leqslant L\), \(\mu_L\)-almost surely, \(\mu_L \rightarrow \mu\) weakly on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) as \(L \rightarrow \infty\),

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu_L} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}], \quad \text{and} \quad \mathbb{E}_{\mu_L} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] .\)

Furthermore for any \(\mu_L\) there exists \((\mu_{L, n})_n \subset \mathcal{X}\) such that \(\| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} \leqslant L\), \(\mu_{L, n}\)-almost surely and \(\mu_{L, n} \rightarrow \mu_L\) weakly on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\).

With this setup we are ready to extend the variational problem. To illustrate the proofs we need some approximation procedures.

Definition 90. Let \(u \in \mathcal{L}\), \(N \in \mathbb{N}\), and \((\eta_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon > 0}\) be a smooth Dirac sequence on \(\Lambda\) and \((\varphi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon > 0}\) be another smooth Dirac sequence compactly supported on \(\mathbb{R}_+ \times \Lambda\). Denote by \(\ast_{\Lambda}\) the convolution only wrt the space variable, and by \(\ast\) the space-time convolution. Define the following approximations of the identity:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} (\text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon} (u)) & := & u \ast_{\Lambda} \eta_{\varepsilon},\\ (\text{reg}_{t : x, \varepsilon} (u)) (t) & := & e^{- \varepsilon t} u \ast \varphi_{\varepsilon} (t) = e^{- \varepsilon t} \int^t_0 u (t - s) \mathord{*}_{\Lambda} \varphi_{\varepsilon} (s) \mathrm{d} s. \end{array}\)

Let

\(\displaystyle \tilde{T}^N (u) := \inf \left\{ t \geqslant 0 | \int^t_0 \| u (s) \|^2_{W^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa, 3}} \mathrm{d} s \geqslant N \right\},\)

and

\(\displaystyle (\text{cut}_N (u)) (t) := u (t) \mathbb{1}_{\{t \leqslant \tilde{T}^N (u) \}} .\)

Observe the following properties of these maps:

  • \(\text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon}\) is a continuous map \(\mathcal{L}_w \rightarrow \mathcal{H}_w\) and \(\mathcal{L} \rightarrow \mathcal{H}\);

  • \(\text{reg}_{t : x, \varepsilon}\) is a continuous map \(\mathcal{L}_w \rightarrow \mathcal{H}\);

  • \(\text{cut}_N\) is continuous as a map \(\mathcal{L} \rightarrow B (0, N^{1 / 2}) \subset \mathcal{L}\);

  • if \(u\) is a predictable process then \(\text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon} (u)\), \(\text{reg}_{t : x, \varepsilon} (u)\), \(\text{cut}_N (u)\) will also be predictable.

Furthermore we have the bounds

\(\displaystyle \| \text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon} (u) \|_{\mathcal{L}}, \| \text{reg}_{t : x, \varepsilon} (u) \|_{\mathcal{L}}, \| \text{cut}_N (u) \|_{\mathcal{L}} \leqslant \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} .\)

uniformly in \(\varepsilon, N\), and for every \(u \in \mathcal{L}\),

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \| \text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon} (u) - u \|_{\mathcal{L}} = \lim_{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \| \text{reg}_{t : x, \varepsilon} (u) - u \|_{\mathcal{L}} = \lim_{N \rightarrow \infty} \| \text{cut}_N (u) - u \|_{\mathcal{L}} = 0.\)

With abuse of notation, for \(\mu \in \mathcal{P} (\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L})\) and \(f : \mathcal{L} \rightarrow \mathcal{L}\), we let

\(\displaystyle f_{\ast} \mu = (\operatorname{Id}, f)_{\ast} \mu =\operatorname{Law}_{\mu} (\mathbb{X}, f (u)) .\)

Remark 91. Let us briefly comment on the rationale for these approximations. \(\text{reg}_{t : x, \varepsilon}\) will be used when one wants to obtain a sequence of weakly convergent measures on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{H}\) or \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) from a sequence of measures weakly convergent on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\). \(\text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon}\) will be used when one wants to obtain a measure on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{H}\) from one on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\), while preserving the estimates on the moments of \(Z (u)\) since \(Z (u \ast_{\Lambda} \eta_{\varepsilon}) = Z (u) \ast_{\Lambda} \eta_{\varepsilon}\).

Let us now proceed.

Lemma 92. If \(T < \infty\) we have

\(\displaystyle \inf_{\mu \in \mathcal{X}} \breve{F}_T (\mu) = \inf_{\mu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}_T (\mu) .\)

Proof. To prove the claim it is enough to show that for any \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\), for any \(\alpha > 0\), there exists a sequence \(\mu_n \in \mathcal{X}\) such that \(\limsup_{n \rightarrow \infty} \breve{F}_T (\mu_n) \leqslant \breve{F}_T (\mu) + \alpha\). W.l.o.g we can assume that \(\breve{F}_T (\mu) < \infty\). Observe that, as long as \(T < \infty\) we can also express

\(\displaystyle \breve{F}_T (\mu) =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ \frac{1}{| \Lambda |} V_T^f (\mathbb{X}^1_T + Z_T (u)) + \frac{1}{2} \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right],\)

and deduce that \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2] < \infty\) since \(V_T\) is bounded below at fixed \(T\). By Lemma 89 there exists a sequence \((\mu_L)_L \subset \overline{\mathcal{X}}\) , such that \(\mu_L (\| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} \leqslant L) = 1\), \(\mu_L \rightarrow \mu\) on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) and by weak convergence and domination,

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu_L} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}], \qquad \mathbb{E}_{\mu_L} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] .\)

First we have to improve the regularity of \(\mu_L\) to get convergence on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{H}_w\) but without affecting our control on the moments of \(Z_T\), so let \(\mu^{\varepsilon}_L := (\text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon})_{\ast} \mu_L\) and \(\mu^{\varepsilon} := (\text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon})_{\ast} \mu\). Then

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}_L} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}], \qquad \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}_L} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}],\)

and \(\mu^{\varepsilon}_L \rightarrow \mu^{\varepsilon}\) on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{H}\). By continuity of \(\breve{F}_T\) and the bound (71), \(\breve{F}_T (\mu^{\varepsilon}_L) \rightarrow \breve{F}_T (\mu^{\varepsilon})\) as \(L \rightarrow \infty\) and \(\breve{F}_T (\mu^{\varepsilon}) \rightarrow \breve{F}_T (\mu)\) as \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\). In particular we can find \(L\) and \(\varepsilon\) such that \(| \breve{F}_T (\mu^{\varepsilon}_L) - \breve{F}_T (\mu) | < \alpha / 2\). By Lemma 89 there exists a sequence \((\mu_{n, L})_{n, L} \subset \mathcal{X}\) such that each measure \(\mu_{n, L}\) is supported on \(\mathfrak{S} \times B (0, L)\) and \(\mu_{n, L} \rightarrow \mu_L\) weakly on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\). Setting \(\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L} := (\text{reg}_{t : x, \delta})_{\ast} (\text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon})_{\ast} \mu_{n, L}\) and \(\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L := (\text{reg}_{t : x, \delta})_{\ast} (\text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon})_{\ast} \mu_L\) we have \(\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L} \rightarrow \mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L\) on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{H}\) with norm topology. It is not hard to see that \(V_T (\mathbb{X}^1_T + Z_T (u)) \lesssim_T \| \mathbb{X} \|^4_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|^4_{\mathcal{H}}\) and since on the support of \(\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}\), \(\| u \|_{\mathcal{H}} \leqslant L\) and the first marginal of \(\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}\) is fixed we have again by domination and weak convergence

\(\displaystyle \lim_{n \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} \left[ \frac{1}{| \Lambda |} V_T^f (\mathbb{X}^1_T + Z_T (u)) + \frac{1}{2} \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right] =\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L} \left[ \frac{1}{| \Lambda |} V_T^f (\mathbb{X}^1_T + Z_T (u)) + \frac{1}{2} \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right]\)

and by dominated convergence (since \(\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L\) is supported on \(\mathfrak{S} \times B (0, L)\)) we can find a \(\delta\) such that \(| \breve{F}_T (\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L) - \breve{F}_T (\mu^{\varepsilon}_L) | < \alpha / 2\) which proves the statement. \(\Box\)

Finally we can state the key result of this section.

Theorem 93. The family \((\breve{F}_T)_T\) \(\Gamma\)–converges to \(\breve{F}_{\infty}\) on \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\). Moreover

\(\displaystyle \lim_T \mathcal{W}_T (f) = \lim_T \inf_{\mu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}_T (\mu) = \inf_{\mu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) =\mathcal{W} (f) .\)

Proof.

Step 1. (Relaxation) We will prove below that:

  1. the family \((\breve{F}_T)_T\) is indeed equicoercive on \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\) (Lemma 86);

  2. the variational problems for \(\breve{F}_T\) (with \(T < \infty\) or \(T = \infty\)) on \(\mathcal{X}\) and on \(\bar{\mathcal{X}}\) are equivalent (Lemma 92 and Lemma 96).

Step 2. (liminf inequality) Consider a sequence \(\mu^T \rightarrow \mu\) in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\). We need to prove that

\(\displaystyle \liminf_{T \rightarrow \infty} \breve{F}_T (\mu^T) \geqslant \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) .\)

It is enough to prove this statement for a subsequence, the full statement follows from the fact that every sequence has a subsequence satisfying the inequality. Take a subsequence (not relabelled) such that

\(\displaystyle \sup_T \breve{F}_T (\mu^T) < \infty . \) (75)

If there is no such subsequence there is nothing to prove. Otherwise tightness for the subsequence follows like in the proof of equicoercivity in Lemma 86 above. Then invoking the Skorokhod representation theorem of [37] we can extract a subsequence (again, not relabelled) and find random variables \((\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T, \tilde{u}^T)_T\) and \((\tilde{\mathbb{X}}, \tilde{u})\) on some probability space \((\tilde{\Omega}, \tilde{\mathbb{P}})\) such that \(\operatorname{Law}_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}} (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T, \tilde{u}^T) = \mu^T\), \(\operatorname{Law}_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}} (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}, \tilde{u}) = \mu\) and almost surely \(\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T \rightarrow \tilde{\mathbb{X}}\) in \(\mathfrak{S}\), \(\tilde{u}^T \rightarrow \tilde{u}\) in \(\mathcal{L}_w\). Note that \(\tilde{l}^T := l^T (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T, \tilde{u}^T) \rightarrow l := l^{\infty} (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}, \tilde{u})\) in \(\mathcal{L}_w\) and using (75) we deduce the almost sure convergence \(l^T \rightarrow l\) in \(\mathcal{H}_w\), maybe modulo taking another subsequence, again not relabelled. Note that, by the analytic estimates of Section 7.2 (which hold point-wise on the probability space) we have

\(\displaystyle \Phi_T (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T, Z (\tilde{u}^T), K (\tilde{u}^T)) + \lambda \| Z_T (\tilde{u}^T) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^T (\tilde{u}^T) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} + Q (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T) \geqslant 0,\)

for some \(L^1 (\tilde{\mathbb{P}})\) random variable \(Q (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T)\) such that \(\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}} [Q (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T)] =\mathbb{E} [Q (\mathbb{W})]\) (for example we can take \(Q (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T) = C (1 + \| \tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T \|^p_{\mathfrak{S}})\) for some large enough \(p\)). Fatou's lemma and Lemma 81 then give

\(\displaystyle \liminf_{T \rightarrow \infty} \breve{F}_T (\mu^T) = \liminf_{T \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}} \left[ \Phi_T (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T, Z (\tilde{u}^T), K (\tilde{u}^T)) + \lambda \| Z_T (\tilde{u}^T) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^T \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right]\)
\(\displaystyle = \liminf_{T \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}} \left[ \Phi_T (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T, Z (\tilde{u}^T), K (\tilde{u}^T)) + \lambda \| Z_T (\tilde{u}^T) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^T \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} + Q (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T) \right] -\mathbb{E} [Q (\mathbb{W})]\)
\(\displaystyle \geqslant \mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}} \liminf_{T \rightarrow \infty} \left[ \Phi_T (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T, Z (\tilde{u}^T), K (\tilde{u}^T)) + \lambda \| Z_T (\tilde{u}^T) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^T \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} + Q (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}^T) \right] -\mathbb{E} [Q (\mathbb{W})]\)
\(\displaystyle \geqslant \mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}} \left[ \Phi_{\infty} (\tilde{\mathbb{X}}, Z (\tilde{u}), K (\tilde{u})) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (\tilde{u}) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (\tilde{u}) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right] = \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu),\)

which is the \(\Gamma\)-liminf inequality.

Step 3.(limsup inequality) Now all that remains is constructing a recovery sequence, for this we can again assume w.l.o.g that \(\breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) < \infty\). From Lemma 95 there is \(\mu_L\) such that \(| \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) - \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu_L) | < \frac{1}{L}\) and (81) is satisfied. Then choosing \(\mu_L^T =\operatorname{Law}_{\mu_L} (\mathbb{X}, \mathbb{1}_{\{ t \leqslant T \}} u_t)\) we obtain that \(l^T (\mathbb{1}_{\{ \cdot \leqslant T \}} u) = \mathbb{1}_{\{ \cdot \leqslant T \}} l^{\infty} (u)\), so \(\| l^T (\mathbb{1}_{\{ \cdot \leqslant T \}} u) \|_{\mathcal{H}} \leqslant \| l^{\infty} (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}\), and \(\| Z_T (\mathbb{1}_{\{ \cdot \leqslant T \}} u) \|^4_{L^4} = \| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4} \lesssim \| u \|^4_{\mathcal{L}}\), which is integrable by (81). By dominated convergence and Lemma 81 we obtain \(\lim_{T \rightarrow \infty} \breve{F}_T (\mu^T_L) = \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu_L)\). Extracting a suitable diagonal sequence gives the required recovery sequence.\(\Box\)

We will deal now with the case that \(T = \infty\).

The proof of Lemma 92 does not apply when \(T = \infty\). An additional difficulty derives from the fact that in approximating the drift \(u\) we might destroy the regularity of \(l^{\infty} (u)\), since now \(l^{\infty} (u)\) needs to be more regular than \(u\), contrary to the finite \(T\) case. To resolve this problem we need to be able to smooth out the remainder without destroying the bound on \(Z_T (u)\). To do so smoothing \(l^{\infty} (u)\) directly, and constructing a corresponding new \(u\) will not work, since \(l^{\infty} (u)\) by itself does not give enough control on \(u\) and \(Z (u)\). However we are still able to prove the following lemma by regularising an “augmented” version of \(l^{\infty} (u)\).

Lemma 94. There exists a family of continuous functions \(\text{rem}_{\varepsilon} : \mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L} \rightarrow \mathcal{L}\), which are also continuous \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w \rightarrow \mathcal{L}_w\), such that for any \(T \in [0, \infty]\),

\begin{eqnarray*} \| \text{rem}_{\varepsilon} (\mathbb{X}, u) \|_{\mathcal{L}} & \lesssim & \| \mathbb{X} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}},\\ \| Z_T (\text{rem}_{\varepsilon} (\mathbb{X}, u)) \|_{L^4} & \lesssim & \| \mathbb{X} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| Z_T (u) \|_{L^4},\\ \| l^{\infty} (\text{rem}_{\varepsilon} (\mathbb{X}, u)) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} & \lesssim_{\varepsilon} & (1 + \| \mathbb{X} \|_{\mathfrak{S}})^4 + \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4} + \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}, \end{eqnarray*}

and \(\| l^{\infty} (\text{rem}_{\varepsilon} (\mathbb{X}, u)) \|_{\mathcal{H}}\) depends continuously on \((\mathbb{X}, u) \in \mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\). Furthermore

\(\displaystyle \text{rem}_{\varepsilon} (\mathbb{X}, u) \rightarrow u \text{ in } \mathcal{L},\)

and if \(l^{\infty} (u) \in \mathcal{H}\)

\(\displaystyle l^{\infty} (\text{rem}_{\varepsilon} (\mathbb{X}, u)) \rightarrow l^{\infty} (u) \text{ in } \mathcal{H} \text{ as } \varepsilon \rightarrow 0.\)

Proof. Let \(\mathbb{X}^2 = \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{X}^2 + \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{X}^2\) be the decomposition introduced in Section 7.2, and observe that for any \(c > 0\) we can easily modify it to ensure that \(\| \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{X}^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \kappa}} < c\), almost surely for any \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\) and for any \(1 \leqslant p < \infty\), \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{X}^2 \|^p_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 + \kappa}}] \leqslant C\) where \(C\) depends on \(| \Lambda |, \kappa, c, p\). Now set \(\tilde{l}_t (u) = - \lambda J_t (\mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{X}_t^2 \succ Z_t^{\flat} (u)) + l_t^{\infty} (u)\). Then \(u\) satisfies

\(\displaystyle u_s = - \lambda \mathbb{X}_s^{\langle 3 \rangle} - \lambda J_s (\mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{X}_s^2 \succ Z^{\flat}_s) + \tilde{l}_s (u) .\)

From this equation we can see that, like in Section 7.2,

\(\displaystyle \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} \lesssim \lambda \| \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \lambda \int^{\infty}_0 \frac{1}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \varepsilon}} \| \mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{X}_s^2 \|_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 - \kappa}} \mathrm{d} s \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \| \tilde{l} (u) \|_{\mathcal{L}},\)

and choosing \(c\) small enough we get

\(\displaystyle \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} \lesssim \lambda \| \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \| \tilde{l} (u) \|_{\mathcal{L}} . \) (76)

Similarly we observe that

\(\displaystyle Z_T (u) = - \lambda \mathbb{X}_T^{[3]} - \lambda \int^T_0 J^2_s (\mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{X}_s^2 \succ Z^{\flat}_s (u)) \mathrm{d} s + Z_T (\tilde{l} (u)),\)

so again with \(c\) small enough and since \(Z^{\flat}_s = \theta_s Z_T\) for \(s \leqslant T\):

\(\displaystyle \| Z_T (u) \|_{L^4} \lesssim \lambda \| \mathbb{X}_T^{[3]} \|_{L^4} + \| Z_T (\tilde{l} (u)) \|_{L^4} . \) (77)

Conversely, it is not hard to see that we have the inequalities

\begin{eqnarray*} \| Z_T (\tilde{l} (u)) \|_{L^4} & \lesssim & \lambda \| \mathbb{X}_T^{[3]} \|_{L^4} + \| Z_T (u) \|_{L^4}, \end{eqnarray*} (78)

and

\(\displaystyle \| \tilde{l} (u) \|_{\mathcal{L}} \lesssim \lambda \| \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} . \) (79)

Clearly the map \((\mathbb{X}, u) \mapsto (\mathbb{X}, \tilde{l} (u))\) is continuous as a map \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L} \rightarrow \mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) and using Lemma 80 also as a map \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w \rightarrow \mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\) , and the inverse is clearly continuous \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L} \rightarrow \mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\). We now show that it is also continuous as a map \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w \rightarrow \mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\). Assume that \(\tilde{l} (u^n) \rightarrow \tilde{l} (u)\) weakly, since then \(\| \tilde{l} (u^n) \|_{\mathcal{L}}\) bounded, this implies by (76) that also \(\| u^n \|_{\mathcal{L}}\) is bounded, and so we can select a weakly convergent subsequence, converging to \(u^{\star}\). Then \(u^{\star}\) solves the equation

\(\displaystyle u^{\star}_s = - \lambda \mathbb{X}_s^{\langle 3 \rangle} - \lambda J_s (\mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{X}_s^2 \succ Z^{\flat}_s (u^{\star})) + \tilde{l}_s (u),\)

(which can be seen for example by testing with some \(h \in \mathcal{L}^{\ast}\)) which implies that \(u^{\star} = u\) (e.g. by Gronwall). Now define \(\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u)\) to be the solution to the equation

\(\displaystyle \operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u)_s = - \lambda \mathbb{X}_s^{\langle 3 \rangle} - \lambda J_s (\mathcal{U}_{>} \mathbb{X}_s^2 \succ Z^{\flat}_s (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u))) + \text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon} (\tilde{l}_s (u)) .\)

Then by the properties discussed above \((\mathbb{X}, u) \mapsto (\mathbb{X}, \operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u))\) is continuous in both the weak and the norm topology and we also have from (76) and (79) that

\begin{eqnarray*} \| \operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u) \|_{\mathcal{L}} & \lesssim & \lambda \| \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} . \end{eqnarray*}

From (77) we have

\(\displaystyle \| Z_T (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u)) \|_{L^4} \lesssim \lambda \| \mathbb{X}_T^{[3]} \|_{L^4} + \| Z_T (u) \|_{L^4}, \)

and by definition of \(\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u)\)

\(\displaystyle \| \tilde{l} (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u)) \|_{\mathcal{H}}\) \(\displaystyle =\) \(\displaystyle \| \text{reg}_{x, \varepsilon} (\tilde{l} (u)) \|_{\mathcal{H}}\)
\(\displaystyle \) \(\displaystyle \lesssim_{\varepsilon}\) \(\displaystyle \lambda \| \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} . \) (80)

Now observe that

\begin{eqnarray*} \| l^{\infty} (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u)) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} & \lesssim & \| s \mapsto \lambda J_s (\mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{X}_s^2 \succ Z_s^{\flat} (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u))) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} + \| \tilde{l} (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u)) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}\\ & \lesssim_{\varepsilon} & \lambda \int^{\infty}_0 \frac{1}{\langle s \rangle^{1 + \kappa}} \| \mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{X}_s^2 \|^2_{\mathcal{\mathscr{C}}^{- 1 + \kappa}} \| Z^{\flat}_s (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u)) \|^2_{L^4} \mathrm{d} s + \lambda \| \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|^2_{\mathcal{L}} + \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}\\ & \lesssim & \lambda (1 + \| \mathbb{X} \|_{\mathfrak{S}})^4 + \| Z_{\infty} (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u)) \|^4_{L^4} + \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}\\ & \lesssim & \lambda (1 + \| \mathbb{X} \|_{\mathfrak{S}})^4 + \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4} + \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}} . \end{eqnarray*}

Since also \(\| \lambda J_t (\mathcal{U}_{\leqslant} \mathbb{X}_t^2 \succ Z_t^{\flat} (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (u))) \|_{\mathcal{H}}\) depends continuously on \((\mathbb{X}, u)\) (both in the weak and strong topology on \(\mathcal{L}\)) we obtain the statement.\(\Box\)

Lemma 95. For any \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\) such that \(\breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) < \infty\) there exists a sequence of measures \(\mu_L \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\) such that

  1. For any \(p < \infty\),

    \(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu_L} [\| u \|^p_{\mathcal{L}}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu_L} [\| l^{\infty} (u) \|^p_{\mathcal{H}}] < \infty, \) (81)

  2. \(\mu_L \rightarrow \mu\) weakly on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) and \(\operatorname{Law}_{\mu_L} (l^{\infty} (u)) \rightarrow \operatorname{Law}_{\mu} (l^{\infty} (u))\) weakly on \(\mathcal{H}\),

  3. \(\displaystyle \lim_{L \rightarrow \infty} \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu_L) = \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu),\)

  4. For any \(\mu_L\) there exists a sequence \(\mu_{n, L} \in \mathcal{X}\) such that

    \(\displaystyle \sup_n (\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{n, L}} [\| u \|^p_{\mathcal{L}}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{n, L}} [\| l^{\infty} (u) \|^p_{\mathcal{H}}]) < \infty, \) (82)

    \(\mu_{n, L} \rightarrow \mu_L\) weakly on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\) and \(\operatorname{Law}_{\mu_{n, L}} (l^{\infty} (u)) \rightarrow \operatorname{Law}_{\mu_L} (l^{\infty} (u))\) weakly on \(\mathcal{H}_w\).

Proof. By Lemma 89 there exists a sequence \(\mu_{\tilde{L}} \rightarrow \mu\) weakly on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) such that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\tilde{L}}} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}], \qquad \mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\tilde{L}}} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}],\)

and \(\mu_{\tilde{L}}\) is supported on \(\mathfrak{S} \times B (0, \tilde{L}) \subset \mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\). Now set \(\mu^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{L}} := (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon})_{\ast} \mu_{\tilde{L}}\). Then \(\mu^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{L}} \rightarrow \mu^{\varepsilon} := (\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon})_{\ast} \mu\) on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\) and by the bounds from Lemma 94 also \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{L}}} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}]\) and \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{L}}} [\| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} [\| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]\). The bounds from Lemma 94 imply also \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_T (u) \|^4_{L^4}]\), \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}} [\| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]\), and furthermore

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{L}}} [\| u \|^p_{\mathcal{L}}] \lesssim \mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\tilde{L}}} (\| \mathbb{X} \|^p_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|^p_{\mathcal{L}}) \lesssim \mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\tilde{L}}} (\| \mathbb{X} \|^p_{\mathfrak{S}}) + \tilde{L}^p,\)

and similarly

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{L}}} [\| l^{\infty} (u) \|^p_{\mathcal{H}}] \lesssim \mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\tilde{L}}} (\| \mathbb{X} \|^p_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|^p_{\mathcal{L}}) \lesssim \mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\tilde{L}}} (\| \mathbb{X} \|^p_{\mathfrak{S}}) + \tilde{L}^p,\)

and by continuity of \(\breve{F}_{\infty}\) and domination using (71) we are also able to deduce that we can find \(\varepsilon\) small enough and \(\tilde{L}\) large enough depending on \(\varepsilon\) such that \(| \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu^{\varepsilon}) - \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) | < 1 / (2 L)\) and \(| \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{L}}) - \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu^{\varepsilon}) | < 1 / (2 L)\). Choosing \(\mu_L = \mu^{\varepsilon}_{\tilde{L}}\) we obtain the first three points of the Lemma. For the fourth point recall that from Lemma 89 we have sequences \(\mu_{n, \tilde{L}} \rightarrow \mu_{\tilde{L}}\) weakly on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\), and \(\mu_{n, \tilde{L}} \in \mathcal{X}\), which have support in \(\mathfrak{S} \times B (0, \tilde{L})\) and since \(\operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon}\) is continuous on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\) setting \(\mu^{\varepsilon}_{n, \tilde{L}} := (\text{rem}_{\varepsilon})_{\ast} \mu_{n, \tilde{L}}\) we obtain the desired sequence. \(\Box\)

Lemma 96. If \(T = \infty\) we have

\(\displaystyle \inf_{\mu \in \mathcal{X}} \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) = \inf_{\mu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) .\)

Proof. One can now proceed very similarly to the proof of Lemma 92. Let \(\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\) such that \(\breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) < \infty\). By Lemma 95, for any \(L, \mu \in \bar{\mathcal{X}}\), there exists a \(\mu_L\) such that \(| \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu) - \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu_L) | < 1 / L\), and a sequence \((\mu_{n, L})_n\) such that \(\mu_{n, L} \in \mathcal{X}\), \(\mu_{n, L} \rightarrow \mu_L\) weakly on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}_w\), and such that (82) is satisfied. Define \(\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L} := \operatorname{Law} (\mathbb{X}, \operatorname{rem}_{\varepsilon} (\text{reg}_{t : x, \delta} (u)))\), and observe that now \(\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L} \rightarrow \mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L\) on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\), \(\operatorname{Law}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} (\mathbb{X}, l^{\infty} (u)) \rightarrow \operatorname{Law}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L} (\mathbb{X}, l^{\infty} (u))\) on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{H}\), and that we have \(\sup_n (\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} [\| u \|^p_{\mathcal{L}}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} [\| l^{\infty} (u) \|^p_{\mathcal{H}}]) < \infty\). Then for some \(\chi \in C (\mathbb{R}, \mathbb{R}), \chi = 1 \text{ on } B (0, 1)\) supported on \(B (0, 2)\), for any \(N \in \mathbb{N}\), the function

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \chi \left( \frac{\| \mathbb{X} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \| l^{\infty} (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}}{N} \right) \left( \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right)\\ & = : & \tilde{\chi}_N (\mathbb{X}, u) \left( \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right) \end{eqnarray*}

is bounded and continuous on \(\mathfrak{S} \times \mathcal{L}\), and so by weak convergence

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \lim_{n \rightarrow \infty} | \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}) - \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L) |\\ & \leqslant & \lim_{n \rightarrow \infty} \left| \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} \left[ \tilde{\chi}_N (\mathbb{X}, u) \left( \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right) \right] - \right.\\ & & \qquad \left. -\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L} \left[ \tilde{\chi}_N (\mathbb{X}, u) \left( \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right) \right] \right|\\ & & + \sup_n \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} \left[ \left| (1 - \tilde{\chi}_N (\mathbb{X}, u)) \left( \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right) \right| \right]\\ & & +\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L} \left[ \left| (1 - \tilde{\chi}_N (\mathbb{X}, u)) \left( \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right) \right| \right]\\ & \leqslant & 2 \sup_n \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} \left[ \mathbb{1}_{\{ \| \mathbb{X} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \| l^{\infty} (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}} > N \}} \left| \Phi_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right| \right]\\ & \lesssim & \sup_n \left( \mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L} (\| \mathbb{X} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \| l^{\infty} (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}} > N)^{1 / 2} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} [\| \mathbb{X} \|^p_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|^8_{\mathcal{L}} + \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^4_{\mathcal{H}}]^{1 / 2} \right)\\ & \lesssim & \sup_n \left( \left( \frac{1}{N} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} [\| \mathbb{X} \|_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} + \| l^{\infty} (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}] \right)^{1 / 2} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_{n, L}} [\| \mathbb{X} \|^p_{\mathfrak{S}} + \| u \|^8_{\mathcal{L}} + \| l^{\infty} (u) \|^4_{\mathcal{H}}]^{1 / 2} \right)\\ & \rightarrow & 0 \quad \text{ as $N \rightarrow \infty$} \end{eqnarray*}

As we can find \(\varepsilon, \delta\) such that \(| \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu^{\varepsilon, \delta}_L) - \breve{F}_{\infty} (\mu_L) | < 1 / L\) we conclude. \(\Box\)

7.4Large deviations

In this section we want to discuss a Laplace principle for the \(\Phi_3^4\) measure in the “small noise limit”. We introduce the family \(\nu^{\hbar}_T\) of measures given by

\(\displaystyle \int g (\phi) \nu^{\hbar}_T (\mathrm{d} \phi) = \frac{\mathbb{E} \left[ g (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_T) e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} V^{\hbar}_T (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_T)} \right]}{\mathscr{Z}_T^{\hbar}},\) (83)

where similarly to above

\(\displaystyle V^{\hbar}_T (\varphi) := \lambda \int_{\Lambda} (| \varphi (\xi) |^4 - a^{\hbar}_T | \varphi (\xi) |^2 + b^{\hbar}_T) \mathrm{d} \xi \qquad \mathscr{Z}^{\hbar}_T := \mathbb{E} \left[ e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} V^{\hbar}_T (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_T)} \right] = \int e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} V^{\hbar}_T (\hbar^{1 / 2} \phi_T)} \vartheta (\mathrm{d} \phi)\)

for any bounded measurable \(g : \mathscr{S}' (\Lambda) \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\). In this section we will take all integrals to be non-normalised for simplicity since we wish to derive a large deviations principle for fixed volume.

This corresponds (modulo renormalisation) to the measure heuristically defined by

\(\displaystyle e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} \int \lambda \varphi (\xi)^4 + \frac{1}{2} \varphi (\xi)^2 + \frac{1}{2} | \nabla \varphi (\xi) |^2 \mathrm{d} \xi} \mathrm{d} \varphi\)

Our goal is now to show that \(\nu^{\hbar}\) given as the weak limit of \(\nu_T^{\hbar}\) satisfies a Laplace principle according to the following definition.

Definition 97. A sequence of measures \(\rho_{\hbar}\) on \(\mathscr{S}' (\Lambda)\) satisfies the Laplace principle with rate function \(I\) if for any continuous bounded function \(f : \mathscr{S}' (\Lambda) \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\)

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} - \hbar \log \int e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} f (\psi)} \mathrm{d} \rho_{\hbar} (\psi) = \inf_{\psi \in \mathscr{S}' (\Lambda)} \{ f (\psi) + I (\psi) \} .\)

Our goal for the rest of this section will be to prove the following theorem:

Theorem 98. The sequence of measures \(\nu_T^{\hbar}\) converges to a unique limit \(\nu^{\hbar}\) as \(T \rightarrow \infty\). Furthermore \(\nu^{\hbar}\) satisfies a Laplace principle with rate function

\(\displaystyle I (\psi) = \lambda \int \psi^4 + \frac{1}{2} \int \psi^2 + \frac{1}{2} \int | \nabla \psi |^2 \) (84)

as \(\hbar \rightarrow 0\).

We will have to analyse the quantity

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}_T^{\hbar} (f) = - \hbar \log \mathbb{E} \left[ e^{- \frac{1}{\hbar} (f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_T) + V^{\hbar}_T (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_T))} \right]\)

First we have to choose \(V^{\hbar}_T\) appropriately. Set \(W_T^{\hbar} = \hbar^{1 / 2} W_T\). Again we choose \(a^{\hbar}_T, b^{\hbar}_T\) so that

\(\displaystyle V^{\hbar}_T (W_T^{\hbar}) = \lambda \int \llbracket (W_T^{\hbar})^4 \rrbracket - \lambda^2 \gamma^{\hbar}_T \int \llbracket (W_T^{\hbar})^2 \rrbracket - \delta^{\hbar}_T,\)

where now the Wick product is taken with respect to the law of \(W_T^{\hbar}\) and not \(W_T\), and \(\gamma^{\hbar}, \delta^{\hbar}\) will be fixed below. Now most of the analysis from Sections 7.2, 7.3 carries over into this situation for fixed \(\hbar\). By the BD formula we have

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}_T^{\hbar} (f) = \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} \tilde{F}^{\hbar}_T (u)\)

with

\(\displaystyle \tilde{F}_T^{\hbar} (u) =\mathbb{E} \left[ f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_T + \hbar^{1 / 2} I_T (u)) + V^{\hbar}_T (W_T^{\hbar} + \hbar^{1 / 2} I_T (u)) + \frac{\hbar}{2} \int^T_0 \| u_s \|^2_{L^2} \right] .\)

And by the change \(\hbar^{1 / 2} u \rightarrow u\) of variables we get

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}_T^{\hbar} (f) = \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} F^{\hbar}_T (u),\)

with

\(\displaystyle F_T^{\hbar} (u) =\mathbb{E} \left[ f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_T + I_T (u)) + V^{\hbar}_T (W_T^{\hbar} + I_T (u)) + \frac{1}{2} \int^T_0 \| u_s \|^2_{L^2} \right] .\)

Lemma 99. Define \(l = l^{T, \hbar} (u) \in \mathbb{H}_a\), \(Z = Z (u) \in C ([0, \infty], H^{1 / 2 - \kappa})\), \(K = K^{\hbar} (u) \in C ([0, \infty], H^{1 - \kappa})\) such that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{l} Z_t (u) := I_t (u),\\ l_t^{T, \hbar} (u) := u_t + \lambda \hbar^{3 / 2} \mathbb{1}_{t \leqslant T} \mathbb{W}^{\langle 3 \rangle}_t + \lambda \hbar \mathbb{1}_{t \leqslant T} J_t (\mathbb{W}_t^2 \succ Z_t^{\flat} (u)),\\ K^{\hbar}_t (u) := I_t (w^{\hbar} (u)), \text{with } w^{\hbar}_t (u) := - \lambda \hbar \mathbb{1}_{t \leqslant T} J_t (\mathbb{W}_t^2 \succ Z_t^{\flat} (u)) + l_t^{T, \hbar} (u), \end{array} \quad t \geqslant 0.\) (85)

Then the functional \(F^{\hbar}_T (u)\) takes the form

\begin{eqnarray*} F^{\hbar}_T (u) & = & \mathbb{E} \left[ f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_T + Z_T) + \Phi^{\hbar}_T (\mathbb{W}, Z (u), K (u)) + \lambda (Z_T (u))^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{T, \hbar} (u) \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2 \right], \end{eqnarray*}

where

\(\displaystyle \Phi^{\hbar}_T (\mathbb{W}, Z, K^{\hbar}) := \sum_{i = 1}^6 \Upsilon_T^{(i), \hbar},\)

\begin{eqnarray*} \Upsilon^{(1), \hbar}_T & := & - \frac{\lambda}{2} \hbar \mathfrak{K}_2 (\mathbb{W}^2_T, K^{\hbar}_T, K^{\hbar}_T) + \frac{\lambda}{2} \hbar (\mathbb{W}^2_T \prec K^{\hbar}_T) K^{\hbar}_T - \lambda^2 \hbar^{5 / 2} (\mathbb{W}^2_T \prec \mathbb{W}_T^{[3]}) K^{\hbar}_T,\\ \Upsilon^{(2), \hbar}_T & := & \lambda \hbar \int (\mathbb{W}^2_T \succ (Z_T - Z^{\flat}_T)) K^{\hbar}_T,\\ \Upsilon^{(3), \hbar}_T & := & \lambda \hbar \int_0^T \int (\mathbb{W}^2_t \succ \dot{Z}^{\flat}_t) K^{\hbar}_t \mathrm{d} t,\\ \Upsilon^{(4), \hbar}_T & := & 4 \lambda \hbar^{1 / 2} \int W_T (K^{\hbar}_T)^3 - 12 \lambda^2 \hbar^2 \int W_T \mathbb{W}_T^{[3]} (K^{\hbar}_T)^2 + 12 \lambda^3 \hbar^{7 / 2} \int W_T (\mathbb{W}_T^{[3]})^2 K^{\hbar}_T,\\ \Upsilon^{(5), \hbar}_T & := & - 2 \lambda^2 \hbar^2 \int \gamma_T Z^{\flat}_T (Z_T - Z^{\flat}_T) - \lambda^2 \hbar^2 \int \gamma_T (Z_T - Z^{\flat}_T)^2 - 2 \lambda^2 \hbar^2 \int^T_0 \int \gamma_t Z^{\flat}_t \dot{Z}_t^{\flat} \mathrm{d} t,\\ \Upsilon^{(6), \hbar}_T & := & - \lambda^2 \hbar^{5 / 2} \int \mathbb{W}^{2 \diamond [3]}_T K^{\hbar}_T - \frac{\lambda^2}{2} \hbar^2 \int_0^T \int \mathbb{W}_t^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle} (Z^{\flat}_t)^2 \mathrm{d} t - \frac{\lambda^2}{2} \hbar^2 \int_0^T \mathfrak{K}_{3, t} (\mathbb{W}^2_t, \mathbb{W}^2_t, Z^{\flat}_t, Z^{\flat}_t) \mathrm{d} t. \end{eqnarray*}

Moreover we have chosen \(\gamma_T^{\hbar} = \hbar^2 \gamma_T\) and the renormalisation constant \(\delta^{\hbar}_T\) to be

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \delta^{\hbar}_T & := & - \frac{\lambda^2}{2} \hbar^3 \mathbb{E} \int_0^T \int (\mathbb{W}_t^{\langle 3 \rangle})^2 \mathrm{d} t + \frac{\lambda^3}{2} \hbar^4 \mathbb{E} \int \mathbb{W}^2_T (\mathbb{W}_T^{[3]})^2\\ & & + 2 \lambda^3 \hbar^2 \gamma^{\hbar}_T \mathbb{E} \int W_T \mathbb{W}_T^{[3]} - 4 \lambda^4 \hbar^5 \mathbb{E} \int W_T (\mathbb{W}_T^{[3]})^3 . \end{array}\) (86)

The proof of Lemma 99 is analogous to the proof of Lemma 70.

Now for fixed \(\hbar\) reasoning analogous to Theorem 79 we can conclude that

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \lim_{T \rightarrow \infty} \mathcal{W}_T^{\hbar} (f)\\ & = & \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (f)\\ & = & \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} \mathbb{E} \left[ f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_{\infty} + I_{\infty} (u)) + \Phi^{\hbar}_{\infty} (\mathbb{W}, Z (u), K^{\hbar} (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} \|^4_{L^4 (\Lambda)} + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}_+ \times \Lambda)} \right] \end{eqnarray*}

where

\(\displaystyle l_t^{\hbar} (u) = u_t + \lambda \hbar^{3 / 2} \mathbb{W}^{\langle 3 \rangle}_t + \lambda \hbar J_t (\mathbb{W}_t^2 \succ Z_t^{\flat} (u)) .\)

and \(\Phi^{\hbar}_{\infty} (\mathbb{W}, Z, K^{\hbar}) := \sum_{i = 1}^6 \Upsilon_{\infty}^{(i), \hbar}\)

\begin{eqnarray*} \Upsilon^{(1), \hbar}_{\infty} & := & - \frac{\lambda}{2} \hbar \mathfrak{K}_2 (\mathbb{W}^2_{\infty}, K^{\hbar}_{\infty}, K^{\hbar}_{\infty}) + \frac{\lambda}{2} \hbar \int (\mathbb{W}^2_{\infty} \prec K^{\hbar}_{\infty}) K^{\hbar}_{\infty} - \lambda^2 \hbar^{5 / 2} \int (\mathbb{W}^2_{\infty} \prec \mathbb{W}_{\infty}^{[3]}) K^{\hbar}_{\infty},\\ \Upsilon^{(2), \hbar}_{\infty} & := & 0\\ \Upsilon^{(3), \hbar}_{\infty} & := & \lambda \hbar \int_0^{\infty} \int (\mathbb{W}^2_t \succ \dot{Z}^{\flat}_t) K^{\hbar}_t \mathrm{d} t,\\ \Upsilon^{(4), \hbar}_{\infty} & := & 4 \lambda \hbar^{1 / 2} \int W_{\infty} (K^{\hbar}_{\infty})^3 - 12 \lambda^2 \hbar^2 \int W_{\infty} \mathbb{W}_{\infty}^{[3]} (K^{\hbar}_{\infty})^2 + 12 \lambda^3 \hbar^{7 / 2} \int W_{\infty} (\mathbb{W}_{\infty}^{[3]})^2 K^{\hbar}_{\infty},\\ \Upsilon^{(5), \hbar}_{\infty} & := & - 2 \lambda^2 \hbar^2 \int^{\infty}_0 \int \gamma_t Z^{\flat}_t \dot{Z}_t^{\flat} \mathrm{d} t,\\ \Upsilon^{(6), \hbar}_{\infty} & := & - \lambda^2 \hbar^{5 / 2} \int \mathbb{W}^{2 \diamond [3]}_{\infty} K^{\hbar}_{\infty} - \frac{\lambda^2}{2} \hbar^2 \int_0^{\infty} \int \mathbb{W}_t^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle} (Z^{\flat}_t)^2 \mathrm{d} t\\ & & - \frac{\lambda^2}{2} \hbar^2 \int_0^{\infty} \mathfrak{K}_{3, t} (\mathbb{W}^2_t, \mathbb{W}^2_t, Z^{\flat}_t, Z^{\flat}_t) \mathrm{d} t. \end{eqnarray*}

Now it remains to prove

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (f) = \inf_{\psi \in \mathscr{S}' (\Lambda)} \{ f (\psi) + I (\psi) \}\)

with \(I\) defined by (84). For this in analogy with Section 7.3 we introduce

\(\displaystyle \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu) := \mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ f (\hbar^{1 / 2} W_{\infty} + I_{\infty} (u)) + \Phi^{\hbar}_{\infty} (\mathbb{X}, Z (u), K^{\hbar} (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right]\)

where the functional is again defined on the space \(\mathcal{X}\) and

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (f) = \inf_{\mu \in \mathcal{X}} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu)\)

and in the same way as for Lemma 96 we can show that

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{W}^{\hbar} (f) = \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu)\)

Next we claim that taking

\(\displaystyle \breve{F}^0 (\mu) =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} \left[ f (Z_{\infty} (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right]\)

we have the following statements

Lemma 100. The family \(\breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu)\) is equicoercive on \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\)

Proof. It is not hard to see that

\(\displaystyle \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu) \geqslant - C + \frac{1}{2} \mathbb{E} \left[ \lambda \| Z_{\infty} \|_{L^4}^4 + \frac{1}{2} \| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right]\)

and also that \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}}] \lesssim C + 2 \lambda \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]\). Define the set

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{K} = \{ \mu : \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] \leqslant K \}\)

note that \(\mathcal{K}\) is compact from Lemma 85. Now we can prove in the same way as in Section 7.2 that

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\hbar} \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu) < \infty .\)

So choosing \(K\) large enough we have that

\(\displaystyle \inf_{\mu \notin \mathcal{K}} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu) \geqslant c K - C > \sup_{\hbar} \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu)\)

which implies

\(\displaystyle \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu) = \inf_{\mu \in \mathcal{K}} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu)\)

\(\Box\)

Lemma 101. \(\breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu)\) \(\Gamma\)-converges to \(\breve{F}^0 (\mu)\) on \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\)

Proof.

Step 1. First we prove the \(\liminf\) inequality. Consider a sequence \(\mu_{\hbar} \rightarrow \mu\) in \(\overline{\mathcal{X}}\), our aim is to prove that

\(\displaystyle \liminf_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu^{\hbar}) \geqslant \breve{F}^0 (\mu) .\)

As before it is enough to prove the statement for a subsequence of \(\mu^{\hbar}\). W.l.o.g. we can assume that

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\hbar} (\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\hbar}} [\| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\hbar}} [\| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]) < \infty . \) (87)

Now from the estimates in Section 7.2 and the definition of \(\Phi_{\infty}^{\hbar}\) we observe that for any \(\mu_{\hbar} \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}\)

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\hbar}} [| \Phi_{\infty}^{\hbar} |] \leqslant \hbar^{1 / 2} (\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\hbar}} [\| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\hbar}} [\| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]),\)

so for any sequence satisfying \(\textrm{(\hyperref[eq:bound-momenta-ld]{87})}\) \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\hbar}} [| \Phi_{\infty}^{\hbar} |] \rightarrow 0\). By the Portmanteau Lemma we have

\(\displaystyle \liminf_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\hbar}} [\| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4}] \geqslant \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4}] .\)

We claim that also

\(\displaystyle \liminf_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{\hbar}} [\| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] \geqslant \mathbb{E}_{\mu} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] .\)
