For this we find a subsequence of \(\mu_{\hbar}\) (not relabelled) and a sequence of random variables \((\mathbb{X}^{\hbar}, u^{\hbar})\) on a probability space \((\tilde{\Omega}, \tilde{\mathbb{P}})\) such that \(\operatorname{Law} (\mathbb{X}^{\hbar}, u^{\hbar}) = \mu_{\hbar}\) and \(\mathbb{X}^{\hbar} \rightarrow \mathbb{X}\) in \(\mathfrak{S}\) and \(u^{\hbar} \rightarrow u^0\) in \(\mathcal{L}_w\), where \(\operatorname{Law} (\mathbb{X}, u^0) = \mu\). Then from eq. (87) we can pick a further subsequence such that \(l^{\hbar} (u^{\hbar}) \rightarrow l^{\star}\) in \(\mathcal{H}_w\). The definition of \(l^{\hbar}\) implies

\begin{eqnarray*} l_t^{\hbar} (u) & = & u_t + \lambda \hbar^{3 / 2} \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle, \hbar}_t + \lambda \hbar J_t (\mathbb{X}_t^{2, \hbar} \succ Z_t^{\flat} (u)) \end{eqnarray*}

and testing and taking limits this implies

\(\displaystyle l^{\star} = u^0,\)

which implies our claim. Now from weak convergence it follows that provided that \(f\) is bounded and continuous on \(H^{- \delta} (\Lambda)\),

\(\displaystyle \lim_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mu_{\hbar}} [f (\hbar^{1 / 2} X_{\infty} + Z_{\infty} (u))] =\mathbb{E}_{\mu} [f (Z_{\infty} (u))],\)

which completes Step 1.

Step 2. Now we construct the recovery sequence, more precisely we prove that for every \(\mu\) there exists \(\mu^{\hbar} \rightarrow \mu\) such that

\(\displaystyle \limsup_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu^{\hbar}) \leqslant \breve{F}^0 (\mu) .\)

By Lemma \(\hyperref[lemma-boundedapprox]{89}\) for any \(\mu\) such that \(\breve{F}^0 (\mu) < \infty\) (otherwise there is nothing to prove) we have a sequence \(\mu^n\) such that \(\| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} \leqslant n\) \(\mu^n\)-almost surely and \(\lim_{n \rightarrow \infty} \breve{F}^0 (\mu^n) = \breve{F}^0 (\mu)\). By a diagonal argument it is enough to find sequences \(\mu^{n, \hbar}\) such that

\(\displaystyle \limsup_{\hbar \rightarrow 0} \breve{F}^{\hbar} (\mu^{n, \hbar}) \leqslant \breve{F}^0 (\mu^n) .\)

For this consider \(u^{\hbar}\) to be the solution to

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} u_t & = & u^{\hbar}_t + \lambda \hbar^{3 / 2} \mathbb{X}^{\langle 3 \rangle}_t + \lambda \hbar J_t (\mathbb{X}_t^2 \succ Z_t^{\flat} (u^{\hbar})) . \end{array}\)

This can be proven to exist by a standard fix-point and Gronwall argument. Applying \(J\) and integrating we obtain

\(\displaystyle Z_t (u^{\hbar}) = Z_t (u) - \lambda \hbar^{3 / 2} \mathbb{X}^{[3]}_t - \lambda \hbar \int^t_0 J_s^2 (\mathbb{X}_s^2 \succ Z_s^{\flat} (u^{\hbar})) \mathrm{d} s \) (88)

so, setting \(g_t := \int^t_0 \langle s \rangle^{- (2 - \delta)} \| \mathbb{X}_s^2 \|_{\mathscr{C}^{- 1 - \delta} (\Lambda)} \mathrm{d} s\), by Gronwall's lemma (recall also \(\| Z_s (u) \|_{L^4} \lesssim \| u \|_{\mathcal{L}}\))

\(\displaystyle \sup_{s \leqslant t} \| Z_s (u^{\hbar}) \|_{L^4 (\Lambda)}\) \(\displaystyle \leqslant\) \(\displaystyle C (\sup_{s \leqslant t} \| Z_s (u) \|_{L^4} + \lambda \hbar^{3 / 2} \sup_{s \leqslant t} \| \mathbb{X}^{[3]}_s \|_{L^4 (\Lambda)}) e^{C \lambda \hbar g_t}\)
\(\displaystyle \) \(\displaystyle \leqslant\) \(\displaystyle C (\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{L}} + \lambda^2 \hbar^3 \sup_{s \leqslant t} \| \mathbb{X}^{[3]}_s \|^2_{L^4 (\Lambda)}) + e^{2 C \lambda \hbar g_t} . \) (89)

This together with (88) implies that

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \| Z_t (u^{\hbar}) - Z_t (u) \|^4_{L^4}\\ & \leqslant & C \lambda^4 \hbar^6 \| \mathbb{X}^{[3]}_t \|^4_{L^4} + C \lambda^4 \hbar^4 g_t^4 \sup_{s \leqslant t} \| Z_s (u^{\hbar}) \|^4_{L^4}\\ & \leqslant & C \lambda^4 \hbar^4 (\hbar^2 \| \mathbb{X}^{[3]}_t \|^4_{L^4} + g_t^8 + \| u \|^{16}_{\mathcal{L}} + \lambda^{16} \hbar^{24} \sup_{s \leqslant t} \| \mathbb{X}^{[3]}_s \|^{16}_{L^4} + e^{16 C \lambda \hbar g_t}) . \end{eqnarray*}

Since the stochastic terms have moments of all orders and \(g_t\) (an integral of Besov norms of a second chaos field) satisfies \(\mathbb{E} [g_{\infty}^p]^{1 / p} \lesssim p\), hence has exponential moments of small order, the expectation of the right-hand side is \(O (\hbar^4)\) for \(\hbar\) small, uniformly in \(t\), and

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^n} \| Z_t (u^{\hbar}) - Z_t (u) \|^4_{L^4} \rightarrow 0\)

since \(\| u \|_{\mathcal{L}} \leqslant n\) \(\mu^n\) almost surely. Now set \(\mu^{n, \hbar} =\operatorname{Law}_{\mu^n} (\mathbb{X}, u^{\hbar})\). Clearly \(l^{\hbar} (u^{\hbar}) = u\), so \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{n, \hbar}} [\| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}] =\mathbb{E}_{\mu^n} [\| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]\) and we have already established that \(\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{n, \hbar}} [\| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4 (\Lambda)}] \rightarrow \mathbb{E}_{\mu^n} [\| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4 (\Lambda)}]\).

Now together with the fact that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E}_{\mu^{n, \hbar}} [| \Phi^{\hbar}_{\infty} (\mathbb{W}^{\hbar}, Z (u), K^{\hbar} (u)) |] \leqslant \hbar^{1 / 2} (\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{n, \hbar}} [\| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4 (\Lambda)}] +\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{n, \hbar}} [\| l^{\hbar} (u) \|^2_{\mathcal{H}}]) \rightarrow 0\)

we can conclude. \(\Box\)

Lemma 102.

\(\displaystyle \inf_{\mu \in \overline{\mathcal{X}}} \breve{F}^0 (\mu) = \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} F (u) = \inf_{\psi \in \mathscr{S}' (\Lambda)} \{ f (\psi) + I (\psi) \}\)

where

\(\displaystyle F (u) =\mathbb{E} \left[ f (Z_{\infty} (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4} + \frac{1}{2} \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \right]\)

Proof. The first equality can easily be proven in the same fashion as Lemma 96. We only prove the second equality.

Step 1. First we prove

\(\displaystyle \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} F (u) \leqslant \inf_{\psi \in \mathscr{S}' (\Lambda)} \{ f (\psi) + I (\psi) \} .\)

Restricting the infimum to processes of the form

\(\displaystyle u_s = J_s \langle \nabla \rangle^2 \psi\)

with \(\psi \in H^2 (\Lambda)\), we see that

\(\displaystyle Z_{\infty} (u) = \int^{\infty}_0 J_s u_s \mathrm{d} s = \int^{\infty}_0 J^2_s \langle \nabla \rangle^2 \psi \mathrm{d} s = \psi\)

we also compute

\(\displaystyle \| u \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} = \int^{\infty}_0 \int_{\Lambda} u^2_s \mathrm{d} s = \int^{\infty}_0 \langle J^2_s \langle \nabla \rangle^2 \psi, \langle \nabla \rangle^2 \psi \rangle_{L^2 (\Lambda)} \mathrm{d} s = \langle \psi, \langle \nabla \rangle^2 \psi \rangle_{L^2 (\Lambda)} = \| \psi \|^2_{H^1}\)

so

\(\displaystyle \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} F (u) \leqslant \inf_{u_s = J_s \langle \nabla \rangle^2 \psi} F (u) = \inf_{\psi \in H^2} \{ f (\psi) + I (\psi) \} = \inf_{\psi \in \mathscr{S}' (\Lambda)} \{ f (\psi) + I (\psi) \}\)

where the last equality follows from approximation of \(\psi \in H^1 (\Lambda)\) with \(H^2 (\Lambda)\) functions, and \(f (\psi) + I (\psi)\) is understood to be \(= \infty\) if \(\psi \not\in H^1 (\Lambda)\).

Step 2. We now prove the converse inequality

\(\displaystyle \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} F (u) \geqslant \inf_{\psi \in \mathscr{S}' (\Lambda)} \{ f (\psi) + I (\psi) \} .\)

Recall that from the proof of Lemma 64 \(\| u \|_{\mathcal{H}} \geqslant \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|_{H^1}\) so

\begin{eqnarray*} \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} F (u) & \geqslant & \inf_{u \in \mathbb{H}_a} \mathbb{E} \left[ f (Z_{\infty} (u)) + \lambda \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^4_{L^4} + \frac{1}{2} \| Z_{\infty} (u) \|^2_{H^1} \right]\\ & \geqslant & \inf_{\psi \in \mathscr{S}' (\Lambda)} \{ f (\psi) + I (\psi) \} . \end{eqnarray*}
\(\Box\)

7.5Stochastic estimates

In this section we close our argument proving the following lemmas which give uniform estimates as \(T \rightarrow \infty\) of some of the stochastic terms appearing in our analytic estimates.

Lemma 103. For any \(\varepsilon > 0\) and any \(p > 1, r < \infty, q \in [1, \infty]\), there exists a constant \(C (\varepsilon, p, q)\) which does not depend on \(\Lambda\) such that

\(\displaystyle \sup_T \mathbb{E} [\| W_T \circ W_T^{[3]} \|_{B^{- \varepsilon}_{r, q}}^p] \leqslant C (\varepsilon, p, q) . \) (90)

Moreover there exists a function \(\gamma_t \in C^1 (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R})\) such that for any \(\varepsilon > 0\) and any \(p > 1\),

\(\displaystyle \sup_T \mathbb{E} [\| (\mathbb{W}^2_T \circ \mathbb{W}_T^{[3]} + 2 \gamma_T W_T) \|_{B^{- 1 / 2 - \varepsilon}_{r, q}}^p] \leqslant C (\varepsilon, p, q), \) (91)
\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} \left[ \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \| J_t \mathbb{W}^2_t \circ J_t \mathbb{W}^2_t + 2 \dot{\gamma}_t \|_{B^{- \varepsilon}_{r, q}} \mathrm{d} t \right)^p \right] \leqslant C (\varepsilon, p, q) . \) (92)

\(\displaystyle \sup_t \mathbb{E} [\| J_t \mathbb{W}^2_t \circ J_t \mathbb{W}^2_t + 2 \dot{\gamma}_t \|_{B^{- \varepsilon}_{r, q}}] \leqslant C (\varepsilon, p, q)\)

and

\(\displaystyle | \gamma_t | + \langle t \rangle | \dot{\gamma}_t | \lesssim 1 + \log \langle t \rangle, \qquad t \geqslant 0. \) (93)

Furthermore \(\gamma\) is independent of \(\Lambda\). By Besov embedding, the Besov-Hölder norms of these objects are also uniformly bounded in \(T\) (but not uniformly in \(\Lambda\)).

Proof. We will concentrate on proving the bounds on the renormalised terms in eqs. (91) and (92) and leave to the reader to fill the details for the easier term in eq. (90). Recall the representation of \((W_t)_t\) in terms of the family of Brownian motions \((B_t^n)_{t, n}\) in eq. (38). Wick's products of the Gaussian field \(W_T\) can be represented as iterated stochastic integrals wrt. \((B_t^n)_{t, n}\). In particular, if we let \(\mathrm{d} w_s (k) = \langle k \rangle^{- 1} \sigma_s (k) \mathrm{d} B_s^k\), we have

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{W}^2_T (x) = 12 \llbracket W_T^2 \rrbracket (x) = 24 \sum_{k_1, k_2} e^{i (k_1 + k_2) \cdot x} \int_0^T \int_0^{s_2} \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \mathrm{d} w_{s_2} (k_2),\)
\(\displaystyle \mathbb{W}^{[3]}_T (x) = 24 \sum_{k_1, k_2, k_3} e^{i k_{(123)} \cdot x} \int_0^T \int_0^{s_3} \int_0^{s_2} \left( \int_{s_3}^T \frac{\sigma_u^2 (k_{(123)})}{\langle k_{(123)} \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} u \right) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \mathrm{d} w_{s_2} (k_2) \mathrm{d} w_{s_3} (k_3),\)

where we denote \(k_{(1 \cdots n)} := k_1 + \cdots + k_n\) for any \(n \geqslant 2\). Now products of iterated integrals can be decomposed in sums of iterated integrals and we get

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \Delta_q (\mathbb{W}^{2 \diamond [3]}_T) (x) & = & \Delta_q (\mathbb{W}^2_T \circ \mathbb{W}^{[3]}_T + 2 \gamma_T W_T) (x)\\ & = & \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_5} \int_{A_T^5} G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 5}) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \cdots \mathrm{d} w_{s_5} (k_5)\\ & & + \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_3} \int_{A_T^3} G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{1, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 3}) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \cdots \mathrm{d} w_{s_3} (k_3)\\ & & + \sum_{k_1} \int_{A_T^1} G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{2, q} ((s, k)_1) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1), \end{array} \) (94)

where \(A^n_T := \{ 0 \leqslant s_1 < \cdots < s_n \leqslant T \} \subseteq [0, T]^n\) and where the deterministic kernels are given by

\begin{eqnarray*} G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 5}) & := & (24^2) \varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 5)}) e^{i (k_{(1 \cdots 5)}) \cdot x} \sum_{\sigma \in \operatorname{Sh} (2, 3)} \sum_{i \sim j} \times\\ & & \times \varrho_i (k_{(\sigma_1 \sigma_2)}) \varrho_j (k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4 \sigma_5)}) \left( \int_{s_{\sigma_5}}^T \frac{\sigma_u (k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4 \sigma_5)})^2}{\langle k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4 \sigma_5)} \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} u \right),\\ G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{1, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 3}) & := & (24^2) \varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 3)}) e^{i (k_{(1 \cdots 3)}) \cdot x} \sum_{\sigma \in \operatorname{Sh} (1, 2)} \sum_{i \sim j} \sum_p \int_0^T \mathrm{d} r \frac{\sigma_r (p)^2}{\langle p \rangle^2} \times\\ & & \times \varrho_i (k_{\sigma_1} + p) \varrho_j (k_{(\sigma_2 \sigma_3)} - p) \left( \int_{s_{\sigma_3} \vee r}^T \frac{\sigma_u (k_{(\sigma_2 \sigma_3)} - p)^2}{\langle k_{(\sigma_2 \sigma_3)} - p \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} u \right),\\ G^{2 \circ [3]}_{2, q} ((s, k)_1) & := & (24^2) \varrho_q (k_1) e^{i k_1 \cdot x} \sum_{i \sim j} \sum_{p_1, p_2} \int_0^T \mathrm{d} r_1 \int_0^T \mathrm{d} r_2 \frac{\sigma_{r_1} (p_1)^2}{\langle p_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{r_2} (p_2)^2}{\langle p_2 \rangle^2} \times\\ & & \times \varrho_i (p_1 + p_2) \varrho_j (k_1 - p_1 - p_2) \left( \int_{r_1 \vee r_2 \vee s_1}^T \frac{\sigma_u (k_1 - p_1 - p_2)^2}{\langle k_1 - p_1 - p_2 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} u \right),\\ G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{2, q} ((s, k)_1) & := & G^{2 \circ [3]}_{2, q} ((s, k)_1) + 2 \gamma_T \varrho_q (k_1) e^{i k_1 \cdot x}, \end{eqnarray*}

where \(\operatorname{Sh} (k, l)\) is the set of permutations \(\sigma\) of \(\{ 1, \ldots, k + l \}\) keeping the orders \(\sigma (1) < \cdots < \sigma (k)\) and \(\sigma (k + 1) < \cdots < \sigma (k + l)\) and where, for any symbol \(z\), we denote with expression of the form \(z_{1 \cdots n}\) the vector \((z_1, \ldots, z_n)\). Estimation of \(\Delta_q (\mathbb{W}^2_T \circ \mathbb{W}^{[3]}_T) (x)\) reduces then to estimate each of the three iterated integrals using BDG inequalities to get, for any \(p \geqslant 2\),

\(\displaystyle I_{0, q} = \left\{ \mathbb{E} \left[ \left| \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_5} \int_{A_T^5} G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 5}) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \cdots \mathrm{d} w_{s_5} (k_5) \right|^{2 p} \right] \right\}^{1 / p}\)

\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_5} \int_{A_T^5} | G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 5}) |^2 \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_5} (k_5)^2}{\langle k_5 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_5 .\)

The kernel \(G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 5})\) being a symmetric function of its argument, we can simplify this expression into an integral over \([0, T]^5\):

\(\displaystyle I_{0, q} \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_5} \int_{[0, T]^5} | G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 5}) |^2 \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_5} (k_5)^2}{\langle k_5 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_5 .\)

Under the measure \(\frac{\sigma_{s_5} (k_5)^2}{\langle k_5 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_5\), we have

\(\displaystyle \left| \int_{s_{\sigma_5}}^T \frac{\sigma_u (k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4 \sigma_5)})^2}{\langle k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4 \sigma_5)} \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} u \right| \lesssim \frac{\mathbb{1}_{\langle k_{\sigma_5} \rangle \leqslant 2 \langle k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4 \sigma_5)} \rangle}}{\langle k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4 \sigma_5)} \rangle^2},\)

since \(\sigma_u (k) \neq 0\) only for \(\langle k \rangle < u < 2 \langle k \rangle\), \(\int_0^{\infty} \sigma_u (k)^2 \mathrm{d} u = 1\), and \(\langle k_{\sigma_5} \rangle < s_{\sigma_5}\) on the support of the measure. Therefore with some standard estimates (using the symmetry in \(\sigma\)) we can reduce this to

\(\displaystyle I_{0, q} \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_5} \int_{[0, T]^5} \frac{\varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 5)})^2}{\langle k_{(345)} \rangle^4} \mathbb{1}_{\langle k_5 \rangle \leqslant 2 \langle k_{(345)} \rangle} \mathbb{1}_{k_{(12)} \sim k_{(345)}} \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_5} (k_5)^2}{\langle k_5 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_5\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_5} \frac{\varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 5)})^2}{\langle k_{(345)} \rangle^4} \mathbb{1}_{\langle k_5 \rangle \leqslant 2 \langle k_{(345)} \rangle} \mathbb{1}_{k_{(12)} \sim k_{(345)}} \frac{1}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{1}{\langle k_5 \rangle^2}\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{p_1, p_2} \mathbb{1}_{p_1 \sim p_2} \frac{\varrho_q (p_1 + p_2)^2}{\langle p_2 \rangle^4} \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_5} \mathbb{1}_{k_{(12)} = p_1, k_{(345)} = p_2} \mathbb{1}_{\langle k_5 \rangle \leqslant 2 \langle p_2 \rangle} \frac{1}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{1}{\langle k_5 \rangle^2}\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{p_1, p_2} \mathbb{1}_{p_1 \sim p_2} \varrho_q (p_1 + p_2)^2 \frac{1}{\langle p_1 \rangle} \frac{1}{\langle p_2 \rangle^4} \lesssim \sum_{p_1, r} \mathbb{1}_{p_1 \sim r - p_1} \varrho_q (r)^2 \frac{1}{\langle p_1 \rangle} \frac{1}{\langle r - p_1 \rangle^4} \lesssim \sum_r \varrho_q (r)^2 \frac{1}{\langle r \rangle^2} \lesssim 2^q .\)

Here we used \(\sum_{k_1 + k_2 = m} \langle k_1 \rangle^{- 2} \langle k_2 \rangle^{- 2} \lesssim \langle m \rangle^{- 1}\) in \(d = 3\), so that the sum over \(k_3, k_4, k_5\) is bounded by \(\sum_{\langle k_5 \rangle \leqslant 2 \langle p_2 \rangle} \langle k_5 \rangle^{- 2} \langle p_2 - k_5 \rangle^{- 1} \lesssim 1\), and that \(p_1 \sim r - p_1\) forces \(\langle p_1 \rangle \sim \langle r - p_1 \rangle \gtrsim \langle r \rangle\). Now by similar reasoning we also have

\(\displaystyle | G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{1, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 3}) | \lesssim \sum_{\sigma \in \operatorname{Sh} (1, 2)} | \varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 3)}) | \sum_{i \sim j} \sum_p \int_0^T \mathrm{d} r \frac{\sigma_r (p)^2 | \varrho_i (k_{\sigma_1} + p) \varrho_j (k_{(\sigma_2 \sigma_3)} - p) |}{\langle p \rangle^2 \langle k_{\sigma_1} + p \rangle^2}\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{\sigma \in \operatorname{Sh} (1, 2)} \frac{| \varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 3)}) |}{\langle k_{\sigma_1} \rangle + 2^q}\)

since the constraint \(i \sim j\) and the support of \(\varrho_q\) force \(\langle k_{\sigma_1} + p \rangle \gtrsim 2^q\) and, in \(d = 3\), \(\sum_{p : \langle k + p \rangle \gtrsim 2^q} \langle p \rangle^{- 2} \langle k + p \rangle^{- 2} \lesssim (\langle k \rangle + 2^q)^{- 1}\), so

\(\displaystyle I_{1, q} = \left\{ \mathbb{E} \left[ \left| \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_3} \int_{A_T^3} G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{1, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 3}) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \cdots \mathrm{d} w_{s_3} (k_3) \right|^{2 p} \right] \right\}^{1 / p}\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_3} \int_{[0, T]^3} \left| \sum_{\sigma \in \operatorname{Sh} (1, 2)} \frac{| \varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 3)}) |}{\langle k_{\sigma_1} \rangle + 2^q} \right|^2 \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_3} (k_3)^2}{\langle k_3 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_3\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_3} \frac{| \varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 3)}) |^2}{(\langle k_1 \rangle + 2^q)^2 \langle k_1 \rangle^2 \langle k_2 \rangle^2 \langle k_3 \rangle^2} \lesssim \sum_{k_1, r} \frac{\varrho_q (r)^2}{(\langle k_1 \rangle + 2^q)^2 \langle k_1 \rangle^2 \langle r - k_1 \rangle} \lesssim 2^q,\)

where, by symmetry, we took \(\sigma_1 = 1\) and used \(\sum_r \varrho_q (r)^2 \langle r - k_1 \rangle^{- 1} \lesssim 2^{2 q}\) uniformly in \(k_1\) and \(\sum_{k_1} (\langle k_1 \rangle + 2^q)^{- 2} \langle k_1 \rangle^{- 2} \lesssim 2^{- q}\).

Finally, we note that the same strategy cannot be applied to the first chaos, since the kernel \(G^{2 \circ [3]}_{2, q}\) cannot be uniformly bounded. We let

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} A_T (s_1, k_1) & := & (24^2) \sum_{i \sim j} \sum_{q_1, q_2} \int_0^T \mathrm{d} r_1 \int_0^T \mathrm{d} r_2 \frac{\sigma_{r_1} (q_1)^2}{\langle q_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{r_2} (q_2)^2}{\langle q_2 \rangle^2} \times\\ & & \times \varrho_i (q_1 + q_2) \varrho_j (k_1 - q_1 - q_2) \left( \int_{r_1 \vee r_2 \vee s_1}^T \frac{\sigma_u^2 (k_1 - q_1 - q_2)}{\langle k_1 - q_1 - q_2 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} u \right), \end{array}\)

so

\(\displaystyle G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{2, q} ((s, k)_1) = \varrho_q (k_1) e^{i k_1 \cdot x} [A_T (s_1, k_1) + 2 \gamma_T] .\)

Observe that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} A_T (0, 0) & = & (24^2) \sum_{q_1, q_2} \int_0^T \mathrm{d} r_1 \int_0^T \mathrm{d} r_2 \frac{\sigma_{r_1} (q_1)^2}{\langle q_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{r_2} (q_2)^2}{\langle q_2 \rangle^2} \times\\ & & \times \int_{r_1 \vee r_2}^T \frac{\sigma_u^2 (q_1 + q_2)}{\langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} u \sum_{i \sim j} \varrho_i (q_1 + q_2) \varrho_j (- q_1 - q_2) . \end{array}\)

We choose \(\gamma_T\) as (note that \(\gamma_T \leqslant 0\): the first chaos of \(\mathbb{W}^2_T \circ \mathbb{W}^{[3]}_T\) has a positive divergent coefficient, so that with the sign convention of (49) the counterterm \(- \lambda^2 \gamma_T \llbracket Y_T^2 \rrbracket\) is a positive mass term)

\(\displaystyle \gamma_T = - \frac{1}{2} A_T (0, 0) = - (12^2 \cdot 2) \sum_{q_1, q_2} \int_0^T \mathrm{d} u \int_0^u \mathrm{d} r_1 \int_0^u \mathrm{d} r_2 \frac{\sigma_{r_1} (q_1)^2}{\langle q_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{r_2} (q_2)^2}{\langle q_2 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_u^2 (q_1 + q_2)}{\langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle^2} \) (95)

where we used the fact that for all \(q \in \mathbb{R}^d\) we have \(\sum_{i \sim j} \varrho_i (q) \varrho_j (q) = 1\), since \(\int f \circ g = \int f g\). Note that, as claimed,

\(\displaystyle | \gamma_T | \lesssim \sum_{q_1, q_2} \frac{\mathbb{1}_{| q_1 |, | q_2 |, | q_1 + q_2 | \lesssim T}}{\langle q_1 \rangle^2 \langle q_2 \rangle^2 \langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle^2} \lesssim 1 + \log \langle T \rangle .\)

Now

\(\displaystyle A_T (s_1, k_1) + 2 \gamma_T = (24^2) \sum_{q_1, q_2} \int_0^T \mathrm{d} r_1 \mathrm{d} r_2 \frac{\sigma_{r_1} (q_1)^2}{\langle q_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{r_2} (q_2)^2}{\langle q_2 \rangle^2} \sum_{i \sim j} \varrho_i (q_1 + q_2) \times\)
\(\displaystyle \times \left( \varrho_j (k_1 - q_1 - q_2) \int_{s_1 \vee r_1 \vee r_2}^T \frac{\sigma_u^2 (k_1 - q_1 - q_2)}{\langle k_1 - q_1 - q_2 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} u - \varrho_j (q_1 + q_2) \int_{r_1 \vee r_2}^T \frac{\sigma_u^2 (q_1 + q_2)}{\langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} u \right)\)

so there exists a constant \(C\) such that, when \(| q_1 + q_2 | \geqslant C | k_1 |\), the quantity in round brackets can be estimated by \(| k_1 | \langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle^{- 3}\) while when \(| q_1 + q_2 | \leqslant C | k_1 |\) it is estimated by \(\langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle^{- 2}\). We have

\(\displaystyle | A_T (s_1, k_1) + 2 \gamma_T | \lesssim \sum_{q_1, q_2} \frac{1}{\langle q_1 \rangle^2} \frac{1}{\langle q_2 \rangle^2} \frac{1}{\langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle^2} \left( \mathbb{1}_{| q_1 + q_2 | \leqslant C | k_1 |} + \mathbb{1}_{| q_1 + q_2 | \geqslant C | k_1 |} \frac{| k_1 |}{\langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle} \right)\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim 1 + \log \langle k_1 \rangle .\)

And then with this choice of \(\gamma_T\) the kernel \(G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{2, q}\) stays uniformly bounded as \(T \rightarrow \infty\) and satisfies

\(\displaystyle | G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{2, q} ((s, k)_1) | \lesssim \varrho_q (k_1) \log \langle k_1 \rangle .\)

From this we easily deduce that

\(\displaystyle I_{2, q} = \left\{ \mathbb{E} \left[ \left| \sum_{k_1} \int_{A_T} G^{2 \diamond [3]}_{2, q} ((s, k)_1) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \right|^{2 p} \right] \right\}^{1 / p} \lesssim q^2 2^q, \qquad q \geqslant - 1.\)

All together these estimates imply that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} \| \Delta_q \mathbb{W}^{2 \diamond [3]}_T \|_{L^{2 p}}^{2 p} \lesssim (q 2^{q / 2})^{2 p}, \qquad q \geqslant - 1.\)

Standard argument allows to deduce eq. (91). The analysis of the other renormalised product proceeds similarly. Let

\(\displaystyle V (t) := \mathbb{W}^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_t = J_t \mathbb{W}^2_t \circ J_t \mathbb{W}^2_t + 2 \dot{\gamma}_t, \qquad t \geqslant 0.\)

First note that by definition of Besov spaces we have

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} \left[ \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \| V (t) \|_{B^{- \varepsilon - d / r}_{r, r}} \mathrm{d} t \right)^p \right] \lesssim \mathbb{E} \left[ \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \left( \sum_q 2^{- q r (\varepsilon + d / r)} \| \Delta_q V (t) \|_{L^r}^r \right)^{1 / r} \mathrm{d} t \right)^p \right] .\)

By Minkowski's integral inequality this is bounded by

\(\displaystyle \lesssim \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \mathrm{d} t \left\{ \mathbb{E} \left[ \left( \sum_q 2^{- q r (\varepsilon + d / r)} \| \Delta_q V (t) \|_{L^r}^r \right)^{p / r} \right] \right\}^{1 / p} \right)^p .\)

When \(r \geqslant p\) Jensen's inequality and Fubini's theorem give

\(\displaystyle \lesssim \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \mathrm{d} t \left\{ \sum_q 2^{- q r (\varepsilon + d / r)} \int_{\Lambda} \frac{\mathrm{d} x}{| \Lambda |} \mathbb{E} [| \Delta_q V (t) (x) |^r] \right\}^{1 / r} \right)^p .\)

Finally hypercontractivity and stationarity allow to reduce this to bound

\(\displaystyle \lesssim \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \mathrm{d} t \left\{ \sum_q 2^{- q r (\varepsilon + d / r)} (\mathbb{E} [| \Delta_q V (t) (0) |^2])^{r / 2} \right\}^{1 / r} \right)^p .\)

Letting \(I_q (t) =\mathbb{E} [| \Delta_q V (t) (0) |^2]\) we have

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} \left[ \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \|\mathbb{W}^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_t \|_{B^{- \varepsilon - d / r}_{r, r}} \mathrm{d} t \right)^p \right] \lesssim \left( \int^{\infty}_0 \mathrm{d} t \left\{ \sum_q 2^{- q r (\varepsilon + d / r)} (I_q (t))^{r / 2} \right\}^{1 / r} \right)^p .\)

Now we decompose the random field \(\Delta_q (\mathbb{W}^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_t) (x)\) into homogeneous stochastic integrals as above and obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \Delta_q (\mathbb{W}^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_t) (x) & = & \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_4} \int_{A_t^4} G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 4}) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \cdots \mathrm{d} w_{s_4} (k_4)\\ & & + \sum_{k_1, k_2} \int_{A_t^2} G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{1, q} ((s, k)_{1 2}) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \mathrm{d} w_{s_2} (k_2)\\ & & + G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{2, q} \end{array} \) (96)

with

\begin{eqnarray*} G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 4}) & = & (24^2) \varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 4)}) e^{i (k_{(1 \cdots 4)}) \cdot x} \times\\ & & \times \sum_{\sigma \in \operatorname{Sh} (2, 2)} \sum_{i \sim j} \varrho_i (k_{(\sigma_1 \sigma_2)}) \varrho_j (k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4)}) \frac{\sigma_t (k_{(\sigma_1 \sigma_2)})}{\langle k_{(\sigma_1 \sigma_2)} \rangle} \frac{\sigma_t (k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4)})}{\langle k_{(\sigma_3 \sigma_4)} \rangle}\\ G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{1, q} ((s, k)_{12}) & = & (24^2) \varrho_q (k_{(12)}) e^{i (k_{(12)}) \cdot x} \sum_{\sigma \in \operatorname{Sh} (1, 1)} \sum_{i \sim j} \sum_p \times\\ & & \times \int_0^t \mathrm{d} r \frac{\sigma_r^2 (p)}{\langle p \rangle^2} \varrho_i (k_{\sigma_1} + p) \varrho_j (k_{\sigma_2} - p) \left( \frac{\sigma_t (k_{\sigma_1} + p)}{\langle k_{\sigma_1} + p \rangle} \frac{\sigma_t (k_{\sigma_2} - p)}{\langle k_{\sigma_2} - p \rangle} \right)\\ G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{2, q} & = & (24^2) \mathbb{1}_{q = - 1} \sum_{i \sim j} \sum_{q_1, q_2} \int_0^t \mathrm{d} r_1 \int_0^t \mathrm{d} r_2 \times\\ & & \times \frac{\sigma_{r_1} (q_1)^2}{\langle q_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{r_2} (q_2)^2}{\langle q_2 \rangle^2} \varrho_i (q_1 + q_2) \varrho_j (- q_1 - q_2) \frac{\sigma_t (q_1 + q_2)^2}{\langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle^2}\\ & & + 2 \dot{\gamma}_t \mathbb{1}_{q = - 1} . \end{eqnarray*}

Using our choice of \(\gamma_T\) in eq. (95) we have that

\(\displaystyle \dot{\gamma}_t = - (12^2 \cdot 2) \sum_{q_1, q_2} \int_0^t \mathrm{d} r_1 \int_0^t \mathrm{d} r_2 \frac{\sigma_{r_1} (q_1)^2}{\langle q_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{r_2} (q_2)^2}{\langle q_2 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_t^2 (q_1 + q_2)}{\langle q_1 + q_2 \rangle^2},\)

which implies also that

\(\displaystyle G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{2, q} = 0, \quad \text{and} \qquad | \dot{\gamma}_t | \lesssim \frac{1 + \log \langle t \rangle}{\langle t \rangle} .\)

as claimed. We pass now to estimate the other two chaoses. The technique is the same we used above. Consider first

\(\displaystyle I_{0, q} (t) := \mathbb{E} \left[ \left| \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_4} \int_{A_t^4} G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 4}) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \cdots \mathrm{d} w_{s_4} (k_4) \right|^2 \right]\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_4} \int_{A_t^4} | G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 4}) |^2 \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_4} (k_4)^2}{\langle k_4 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_4\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_4} \int_{[0, t]^4} | G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{0, q} ((s, k)_{1 \cdots 4}) |^2 \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_4} (k_4)^2}{\langle k_4 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_4\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_4} \varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 4)})^2 \int_{[0, t]^4} \frac{\sigma_t^2 (k_{(12)})}{\langle k_{(12)} \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_t^2 (k_{(34)})}{\langle k_{(34)} \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_4} (k_4)^2}{\langle k_4 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_4\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{k_1, \ldots, k_4} \varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 4)})^2 \frac{\sigma_t^2 (k_{(12)})}{\langle k_{(12)} \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_t^2 (k_{(34)})}{\langle k_{(34)} \rangle^2} \frac{1}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{1}{\langle k_4 \rangle^2}\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \frac{\mathbb{1}_{2^q \lesssim t}}{\langle t \rangle^6} \sum_{| k_{(12)} |, | k_{(34)} | \sim t} \frac{\varrho_q (k_{(1 \cdots 4)})^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2 \langle k_2 \rangle^2 \langle k_3 \rangle^2 \langle k_4 \rangle^2}\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \frac{\mathbb{1}_{2^q \lesssim t}}{\langle t \rangle^6} \sum_{l, m : | l |, | m | \sim t} \frac{\varrho_q (l + m)^2}{\langle l \rangle \langle m \rangle} \lesssim \frac{\mathbb{1}_{2^q \lesssim t}}{\langle t \rangle^5} 2^{3 q}\)

where we used that \(| \sigma_t (x) | \lesssim t^{- 1 / 2} \mathbb{1}_{x \sim t}\) (which also forces \(2^q \lesssim t\)), the bound \(\sum_{k_1 + k_2 = l} \langle k_1 \rangle^{- 2} \langle k_2 \rangle^{- 2} \lesssim \langle l \rangle^{- 1}\) in \(d = 3\), and that there are \(O (2^{3 q})\) values of \(l + m\) in the support of \(\varrho_q\) and \(O (t^3)\) values of \(l\) with \(| l | \sim t\). (The constraint \(| k_{(12)} |, | k_{(34)} | \sim t\) cannot be dropped, since the four-fold convolution of \(\langle k \rangle^{- 2}\) diverges in \(d = 3\).) Now taking \(0 < \varepsilon + d / r < 3 / 2\) we have

\(\displaystyle \int^{\infty}_0 \mathrm{d} t \left\{ \sum_q 2^{- q r (\varepsilon + d / r)} (I_{0, q} (t))^{r / 2} \right\}^{1 / r} \lesssim \int^{\infty}_0 \mathrm{d} t \left\{ \sum_{q : 2^q \lesssim t} \frac{2^{q r (3 / 2 - \varepsilon - d / r)}}{\langle t \rangle^{5 r / 2}} \right\}^{1 / r}\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \int^{\infty}_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} t}{\langle t \rangle^{1 + \varepsilon + d / r}} \lesssim 1.\)

Taking into account that \(| k_1 |, | k_2 | \lesssim t\) we can estimate

\(\displaystyle | G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{1, q} ((s, k)_{12}) | \lesssim | \varrho_q (k_{(12)}) | \sum_p \frac{\mathbb{1}_{| p | \lesssim t}}{\langle p \rangle^2} \left( \frac{\sigma_t (k_1 + p)}{\langle k_1 + p \rangle} \frac{\sigma_t (k_2 - p)}{\langle k_2 - p \rangle} \right) \lesssim | \varrho_q (k_{(12)}) | \langle t \rangle^{- 2},\)

from which we deduce that

\(\displaystyle I_{1, q} (t) := \mathbb{E} \left[ \left| \sum_{k_1, k_2} \int_{A_t^2} G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{1, q} ((s, k)_{12}) \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \mathrm{d} w_{s_2} (k_2) \right|^2 \right]\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \sum_{k_1, k_2} \int_{A_t^2} | G^{\langle 2 \rangle \diamond \langle 2 \rangle}_{1, q} ((s, k)_{12}) |^2 \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{s_2} (k_2)^2}{\langle k_2 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \mathrm{d} s_2\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{- 4} \sum_{k_1, k_2} | \varrho_q (k_{(12)}) |^2 \int_{[0, t]^2} \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\sigma_{s_2} (k_2)^2}{\langle k_2 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \mathrm{d} s_2\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{- 4} \sum_{k_1, k_2} | \varrho_q (k_{(12)}) |^2 \frac{\mathbb{1}_{k_1 \lesssim t}}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \frac{\mathbb{1}_{k_2 \lesssim t}}{\langle k_2 \rangle^2} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{- 4} 2^{2 q} \mathbb{1}_{2^q \lesssim t},\)

and then, as for \(I_{0, q}\), we have

\(\displaystyle \int^{\infty}_0 \mathrm{d} t \left( \sum_q 2^{- q r (\varepsilon + d / r)} (I_{1, q} (t))^{r / 2} \right)^{1 / r} \lesssim \int^{\infty}_0 \frac{\mathrm{d} t}{\langle t \rangle^2} \left( \sum_q 2^{q r (1 - \varepsilon - d / r)} \mathbb{1}_{2^q \lesssim t} \right)^{1 / r} \lesssim 1,\)

as claimed. From these estimates standard arguments give eq. (92).\(\Box\)

Lemma 104. We have

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [\| \mathbb{W}^3_T \|^p_{L^p}]^{1 / p} \lesssim T^{3 / 2} .\)

This implies that \(\mathbb{W}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \in C ([0, \infty], B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}) \cap L^2 (\mathbb{R}_+, B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa})\) for any \(p < \infty\) uniformly in the volume and \(\mathbb{W}^{\langle 3 \rangle} \in C ([0, \infty], \mathscr{C}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}) \cap L^2 (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathscr{C}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa})\).

Proof. Observe that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{W}^3_T (x) = 4 \llbracket W_T^3 \rrbracket (x) = 24 \sum_{k_1, k_2, k_3} e^{i (k_1 + k_2 + k_3) \cdot x} \int_0^T \int_0^{s_3} \int^{s_2}_0 \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \mathrm{d} w_{s_2} (k_2) \mathrm{d} w_{s_3} (k_3) .\)

By space homogeneity, we get for any \(p\),

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathbb{E} [\| \mathbb{W}^3_T \|^p_{L^p}] & = & \mathbb{E} [| \mathbb{W}^3_T (0) |^p]\\ & = & 24^p \mathbb{E} \left[ \left| \sum_{k_1, k_2, k_3} \int_0^T \int_0^{s_3} \int^{s_2}_0 \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \mathrm{d} w_{s_2} (k_2) \mathrm{d} w_{s_3} (k_3) \right|^p \right]\\ & \lesssim & \left( \mathbb{E} \left[ \left| \sum_{k_1, k_2, k_3} \int_0^T \int_0^{s_3} \int^{s_2}_0 \mathrm{d} w_{s_1} (k_1) \mathrm{d} w_{s_2} (k_2) \mathrm{d} w_{s_3} (k_3) \right|^2 \right] \right)^{p / 2}\\ & = & \left( \sum_{k_1, k_2, k_3} \int_0^T \int_0^{s_3} \int^{s_2}_0 \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_3} (k_3)^2}{\langle k_3 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_3 \right)^{p / 2}\\ & \lesssim & (T^{3 / 2})^p . \end{eqnarray*}

Since

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \sum_{k_1, k_2, k_3} \int_0^T \int_0^{s_3} \int^{s_2}_0 \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_3} (k_3)^2}{\langle k_3 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_3\\ & \leqslant & \sum_{k_1, k_2, k_3} \int_0^T \int_0^T \int^T_0 \frac{\sigma_{s_1} (k_1)^2}{\langle k_1 \rangle^2} \cdots \frac{\sigma_{s_3} (k_3)^2}{\langle k_3 \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s_1 \cdots \mathrm{d} s_3\\ & = & \left( \sum_k \int^T_0 \frac{\sigma_s (k)^2}{\langle k \rangle^2} \mathrm{d} s \right)^3 \lesssim T^3 . \end{eqnarray*}

Now the remaining properties follow by the fact that \(\sigma_t\) is supported in an annulus of radius \(t\), so

\(\displaystyle \| \mathbb{W}_t^{\langle 3 \rangle} \|_{B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} = \left\| \frac{\sigma_t (\mathrm{D})}{\langle \mathrm{D} \rangle} \mathbb{W}_t^3 \right\|_{B_{p, p}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \lesssim \| \sigma_t (\mathrm{D}) \mathbb{W}_t^3 \|_{B_{p, p}^{- 3 / 2 - \kappa}} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa} \langle t \rangle^{- 3 / 2} \| \mathbb{W}_t^3 \|_{L^p}\)

and the Hölder estimates follow by Besov embedding (but with constants which depend on the volume).\(\Box\)
